# 08 — Production Gate & Risk Register

**Scope:** the single authoritative register of gates that must clear before production go-live. Every gate has an owner (role), milestone, required evidence, a pass/fail rule, current state, and blocking severity. **Go-live is authorized separately by the owner after M9**, only when every blocking gate is `PASS`.

**Severity:** `BLOCKER` (go-live cannot proceed), `HIGH` (must be resolved or explicitly owner-waived), `MED` (resolve or accept with documented risk).
**Current state:** `OPEN` (not started/in progress), `PARTIAL`, `PASS`.

| Gate | Risk it closes | Owner | Milestone | Evidence required | Pass rule | Current | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **G-HIST** | M0 historical exploitation can't be disproven (historical **evidence limitation**, not a waivable active vulnerability) | Security | M8 (investigation) → M9 (disposition) | **M8:** bounded (defined scope + timebox), **read-only** attempt to retrieve existing authorized historical evidence (Cloud Audit Logs / version history) **without enabling new retroactive collection**; classify if present. **If the investigation surfaces evidence of actual PII/CHD exposure, the statutory breach-notification obligation activates** (G-PRIVACY / G-INCIDENT). **M9:** if no reconstructing evidence exists, explicit **owner risk acknowledgement** documenting: exposure window existed; current state showed no privileged mismatch; Firestore `auditEvents` empty; historical non-exploitation unprovable; fail-closed rules active; compensating controls + future monitoring established | M8 evidence reviewed & no unremediated compromise (breach-notification triggered if exposure found), OR M9 owner risk-acknowledgement recorded with all six points | OPEN | HIGH |
| **G-EMU** | Firestore rules unproven semantically | QA | M2 | Emulator semantic suite green in a Java-enabled env | Suite passes; rerun in CI before any rules deploy | **PARTIAL — the semantic suite passes locally (41/41, ephemeral Nix JDK21 + `emulators:exec` on a `demo-` project, no creds) and in CI, which runs it on every push to `main` (`ci.yml` quality job, step "Firestore semantic emulator suite"; green on `8883473d`, run 34779503875); static guard retained (21/21). The pass rule's rerun before any rules deploy still applies; moving the gate to `PASS` is the owner's decision** | BLOCKER |
| **G-4USER** | 4 noncanonical users hold client-only roles | Platform IAM | M5 | Migration executed per [04](./04-canonical-iam-and-four-user-migration.md); post-migration authz verification | All four canonically mapped & verified; none unmapped in prod | OPEN | BLOCKER |
| **G-CPLOGIN** | No separate Backend CP login/session | Platform | M4 | Admin login exchange + HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite cookie + CSRF + MFA + step-up; real-socket test | Separate admin session boundary live; tenant session cannot reach admin | **OPEN — M4 slices 1–2 are delivered in the runtime only (committed in `dd89ff9`):** separate administrative and tenant/store session boundaries with host-distinct origin allowlists (enforced at composition), HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite=Strict `__Host-` cookies, session-bound CSRF, login client and account limits, admin MFA and recent authentication (a provider-verified second factor and an authentication at most 5 minutes old), admission revalidation on every protected request of both boundaries (the tenant boundary since M5-GIAM-D1-P2, the administrative boundary since M5-GIAM-D1-P4; the admin boundary re-asked every 1 min before that) with per-principal revocation, bounded port calls, and a real-socket suite (`server/runtime/sessions.test.ts`) in which neither boundary's session reaches the other's routes. Nothing is live: the durable session store, both boundaries' admissions and authorizers (migrations 011 and 013) and, since M6-RATE-P2, the distributed rate limiter (migration 014) exist in code, built by the transaction root and bound by `durableSessionAdapters`, so the provider-aware composition root (`server/composition/productionSessions.ts`), handed that bundle and a valid `RATE_LIMIT_KEY`, no longer names `rate_limit_store_unavailable` — but until M6-ENTRY-P1 no production entry composed either root (`server/runtime/server.ts`, since deleted, served the probes only; see M6-ENTRY-P1 below); the login/logout audit record is not delivered *(until M6-ADMIN-AUDIT-P1, below, for the administrative boundary only)*; and step-up is delivered only for the COMMAND path (M5-GIAM-D1-P2: a route may require a provider-verified authentication no older than ten minutes, bound to the session at login and re-judged inside the committing transaction, refused as a bounded `step_up_required`) — there is no re-authentication flow a person can complete without logging in again, and no client offers one *(until M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1, below)*.

**M4-ADMIN-UI-P1 (committed in `dd89ff9`) adds the browser half:**
- The admin console signs in through the existing Firebase client SDK, using an in-memory instance.
- It completes the provider's MFA challenge.
- It exchanges the one ID token at `POST /admin/v1/session/login`.
- It keeps the session CSRF token in memory only.
- It renders the read-only shell only after `GET /admin/v1/session` confirms the session.
- In production it runs only on a configured administration origin and refuses everywhere else.

It is not live either: with no composed boundary, the console reports sign-in as unavailable.

**M4-ADMIN-UI-P2 (committed in `dd89ff9`) adds:**
- A read-only Command Center read model (`server/runtime/commandCenter.ts`): `GET /admin/v1/command-center` behind the admin session and `{ scope: 'platform', permission: 'view_command_center' }`, with no state-changing operation. It maps an injected reader port into a bounded view (posture counts, per-area attention counts, governance signals, service health; each section available, unavailable or not configured, with `asOf` and `stale`), and no reader string reaches the output. The route is uncomposed — neither the composition root nor the deployable entry imports it — and no authoritative source exists (M7h), so its data is synthetic: test adapters and hermetic browser interception only. The console dashboard (`/admin`) refreshes manually only, and an unavailable section never shows a number, including a reading that ages past 24 hours while the page is open (counted from the server's generation instant).
- The admin second factor is TOTP only, in the console and the server login policy: phone needs reCAPTCHA hosts the admin CSP refuses ([03](./03-backend-control-plane-login-session-blueprint.md) §2a).

**Pre-operation security requirement.** This blocks production session activation, operational data access (the Command Center's included) and every sensitive admin action, and nothing in M4-ADMIN-UI-P2 bypasses it; it does not block the read-only shell.

The problem is that concurrent requests on one session can interleave admission revalidation with revocation:
- A request authenticated just before a concurrent denial can still run its handler.
- The store contract does not refuse a `validatedAt` or `lastSeenAt` update, or a login's session create, issued after a concurrent `revokePrincipal`.

What is required:
- The durable store must make those updates and creates conditional on the record still being live and on the principal's current security version.
- Sensitive handlers must re-verify that version inside their own write transaction.

**Status (M5-ADMIN-REVOKE-P1, in code only, not deployed; [ADR-30](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** For the **administrative boundary** the create condition and the in-transaction re-verification of sensitive handlers are implemented. The clause requiring stamp UPDATES to be conditional on the principal's current security version is **not** implemented: updates are conditional on liveness only (DA-77). Both implemented parts rest on one ordering rule: every administrative change takes effect when its transaction commits, except DA-76.
- **Creates.** `establish` (login and recent sign-in) re-reads the principal's standing first, on its own transaction, with the actor's account, identity links and grants locked until its COMMIT. It requires the witness it recomputes to equal the new record's. A recent sign-in also requires the presented record to be live, administrative and the same principal's, and to be ended by that same transaction. Otherwise nothing is created, ended or recorded.
- **Updates (not implemented as written).** The `validatedAt` and `lastSeenAt` stamps are conditional on liveness only, not on the version. No revival or authority path was found through this:
  - an update changes an existing row only, so a session already ended answers `absent` and is never recreated;
  - on this boundary every protected request re-reads admission and compares the witness (`ADMISSION_REVALIDATE_MS.admin = 0`), so no stamp spares a later request that check.

  Amending the clause, or implementing a version-conditional update, is an open owner decision (DA-77).
- **Sensitive handlers.** The two tenant-management commands re-verify inside their committing transaction. They hold the actor's account, links and grants (migration 009) and the row of the session that presented them, then re-read the standing (migration 013) and require the witness bound to that session.
- **Changes 009 cannot lock.** Migration 015 makes them wait for a command or login holding the actor's account.

A read request may still complete (200) when its session read came before a logout or revocation and its admission read came before a standing change. This is permitted behaviour, not a defect. It includes a logout that commits between the request's two reads (M5R-02c), because the admission read never reads the session row. That request's refresh recreates nothing, and the next request is 401.

The requirement was **unmet for the tenant boundary** until M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1: its login revoked and then created with no standing re-read, no entry composed it, and it had no business route (DA-39). *Since M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1, in code only:* the tenant login is one `establish` transaction that holds its actor's standing and re-reads it, so the create condition is implemented on the tenant boundary too (its stamps are conditional on liveness only, as on this boundary, DA-77). *Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P1:* one tenant business route, `GET /api/v1/customers`, exists in code. *Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2, commit 2, in code only:* the compiled tenant entry composes the tenant boundary and serves that route on its own host; no tenant hostname is configured, and nothing is deployed (DA-87). *Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3, in code only ([ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):* the same entry also serves `POST /api/v1/customers`, the first tenant command. Its transaction holds the presenting session and the actor's standing until COMMIT, so a logout, a revocation or a standing change committed first refuses it (503, nothing written), and one attempted later waits for its COMMIT; not deployed. Deployment is not authorized, and G-CPLOGIN stays OPEN for the tenant boundary. A tenant request that its boundary admitted before a suspension can still be refused 403 by the authorizer's own read (DA-63, fail closed).

The requirement therefore stays open for the administrative boundary until that owner decision is made (DA-77), and G-CPLOGIN stays OPEN.

**Deployment invariants (M4-ADMIN-UI-P2).** Before any admin host serves the console:
- The Firebase project must not enforce reCAPTCHA (Enterprise) for email/password sign-in while the admin CSP excludes reCAPTCHA; otherwise every admin password sign-in fails closed as "unavailable".
- HSTS `includeSubDomains` requires every subdomain of the admin host — and of the runtime host, where the production entry (`server/entry/productionEntry.ts`) sends HSTS on every response (`hsts: true`) — to serve HTTPS.
- The Firebase web API key the console uses must not rely on an HTTP-referrer restriction: the Auth SDK sends its identity-toolkit requests with `referrerPolicy: 'no-referrer'` (and the admin document's `Referrer-Policy` is `no-referrer`), so a referrer-restricted key refuses every admin sign-in.
- The admin host must serve the console through `createAdminWebListener`, with `/admin/v1/*` routed to the runtime — never a static host with SPA fallback. No admin-host deployment exists yet.

**M5-GIAM-D1-P4 makes the administrative boundary's admission and authorization durable, in code only ([ADR-25](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):**
- **Admission.** Migration 013's one `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` read, `tmpos_admission.m5_admit_platform_session`, answers in one snapshot migration 007's resolution narrowed to the actor's PLATFORM grants, plus the actor's platform authority version. The adapter (`server/persistence/postgresPlatformAdmission.ts`) reports that answer and decides nothing. *(Since M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1 it and `platformStandingOf` accept the platform authority version only as canonical decimal text in 0..2^53−1, what 013 can answer; anything else is an outage, M5PA-08b and M5PS-05b.)* `createPlatformSessionAdmission` (`server/composition/platformAuthorization.ts`) admits only an `active` or `trialing` account with no read-only cap, holding exactly one active platform grant (none is unauthorized, two are ambiguous, and there is no first match) whose role the canonical catalog defines. A tenant or store grant never confers platform authority, whatever role id it carries.
- **Witness.** The session is bound to `p1:` plus a SHA-256 digest over the actor, the account status, its cap, 007's security version, the grant, its role and the platform authority version. The boundary re-asks on EVERY protected request, the current-session read included (`ADMISSION_REVALIDATE_MS.admin = 0`; it was 1 min); logout never asks. A denial revokes every administrative session of the principal and no tenant session; a changed witness revokes the session presented; an outage is a bounded 503 that revokes nothing. The authority version only increases, so a restoration — a grant re-enabled or re-inserted, a role changed back, an account restored — revives no session. A tenant's state is not in the witness, so suspending a tenant logs out no administrator.
- **Authorization.** The canonical authorizer (`createAdmittedPlatformAuthorizer`) decides on the scope that same request's admission selected, never on a second read; `createPlatformRouteAuthorizer` is removed. A command still re-reads its authority inside the committing transaction (migration 009, unchanged).
- **Composition.** The transaction root builds the PostgreSQL platform admission over its one kernel, and the sessions root binds the admission and the authorizer together from it (`durableSessionAdapters`), never one alone. Handed that bundle, `admin_admission_unavailable` and `admin_authorizer_unavailable` no longer arise; `rate_limit_store_unavailable` remains, so production composition still refuses *(until M6-RATE-P2, below)*, and no production entry composes either root. Readiness now includes the administrative admission's probe. The tenant admission offers no probe, so it is still not part of readiness.
- **Evidence**, against disposable PostgreSQL only: `tests/db/platformAdmission.integration.test.mjs` (migration 013) and `tests/db/adminBoundary.integration.test.mjs` (the composed administrative boundary over real local sockets).

**M6-RATE-P2 narrows the rate-limit part of this gate, in code only ([ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the transaction root builds the PostgreSQL rate limiter over its one kernel (migration 014), and the sessions root binds it with the `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` bytes it parsed itself. Handed that bundle and a valid key, the composition no longer names `rate_limit_store_unavailable`; it still names it whenever the bundle is absent, and beside `rate_limit_key_missing` or `rate_limit_key_invalid` when the key is the cause. The login client and account limits are counted in one store shared by every instance, on the database's clock; a store outage or a mismatched key refuses the login with a bounded 503 and fails readiness, and never allows it. The gate stays OPEN: no production entry composes either root. The deployable entry is the next composition task, and `server/runtime/server.ts` still serves the probes only *(until M6-ENTRY-P1, below)*.

**M6-ENTRY-P1 narrows only the deployable-entry part of this gate, in code only ([02 §2.1](./02-target-production-architecture.md), [ADR-27](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the deployable entry (`server/entry/productionEntry.ts`) composes the administrative boundary only and serves exactly the 8 shipping routes; every route but the two probes answers only on the admin host, and the probes answer on any host. Its startup preflight checks the runtime login against the approved runtime boundary (no superuser, BYPASSRLS, CREATEROLE, etc.; exactly the tmpos_app and tmpos_audit_writer memberships; the one direct privilege, SELECT on public.schema_migrations — G-DBROLE row). The entry binds the session store, admin admission, authorization, rate limiter, idempotency store and command-transaction port at composition. Before it listens, it probes the idempotency store and the command-transaction port (step 7), then (step 9) reads the session store once (a random key that names no session) and probes the administrative admission and the composed rate limiter. `/readiness` reports the rate limiter, the idempotency store, the command-transaction port and the administrative admission; it does not ask the session store, which only that one startup read checks (DA-59). The entry is code-only and not deployed.

Delivered with it as controls: configuration refuses `FIREBASE_AUTH_EMULATOR_HOST` (`identity_emulator_forbidden` — the Firebase Admin SDK would accept unsigned tokens), `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED` (`tls_verification_override_forbidden`) a widened trust store (`NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, or `NODE_OPTIONS` carrying `--use-openssl-ca`: `tls_trust_store_override_forbidden`), the owner credentials (`SUPABASE_DATABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`: `owner_credential_forbidden`, DA-58) and an admin origin whose host is an IP literal (`session_admin_origin_invalid`: a served host's last label must contain a letter); the listener pins Node's strict HTTP parser (`insecureHTTPParser: false`); step 10's listen is bounded by the step deadline (`listen_timeout`), and a socket that binds late is closed at once. The gate stays OPEN. Still open under this gate: deployment on the real admin hostname (DA-9 dependency), the step-up experience (DA-34), the login/logout audit record, the tenant boundary composition (not in scope for this stage), and the pre-operation security requirement (concurrent-revocation race, still blocks production session activation); the pre-operation requirement is not re-judged by this stage. Residuals: DA-46 to DA-51 (M5-GIAM-D1-P4), carried unchanged; DA-52 and DA-53 (M6-RATE-P2); new DA-54–DA-58 (M6-ENTRY-P1).

**M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1 narrows only the step-up part of this gate, in code only ([03 §2a](./03-backend-control-plane-login-session-blueprint.md), [ADR-28](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the step-up experience (DA-34) is delivered without a new route or policy change, and without a new migration. `POST /admin/v1/session/login` gains one opt-in mode: sent with `X-TMPOS-Reauthenticate: 1`, a login runs every step an ordinary login runs, in the same order (host, Origin and intent, client and account limits, provider verification, the TOTP second factor, the 5-minute authentication age, admission), and then requires the presented session cookie to authenticate as a valid (unexpired, unrevoked) administrative session of the SAME principal, whose security version equals the version this login's admission just reported. Only then does it rotate exactly as a login does: the presented session is revoked, a new one is issued bound to the new provider-verified authentication time, and the old cookie answers 401 from then on; nothing a request carries sets or extends that time. An absent, expired or revoked presented session, a different administrator, or a presented session with a stale security version (`login_reauth_stale_version`; the next ordinary session request revokes it through the existing version-mismatch path) is the generic 401; a malformed or repeated header is refused the same way and never served as an ordinary login; a store outage is the bounded 503. These reauthentication checks run after the ordinary login steps and create and revoke nothing; an admission denial at the ordinary step still ends every session of that principal, as at any login. Without the header a login is unchanged. The console's recent-sign-in dialog drives it in-page (no redirect, nothing persisted) and resumes nothing on its own: the administrator re-reads the tenant and confirms again with a new reason and a new key. `RECENT_AUTH_MAX_AGE_MS` (10 minutes), the transaction-time check, the cookie, Origin, CSRF, host, trusted-proxy and rate-limit rules and `SHIPPING_ROUTES` are unchanged. The gate stays OPEN. Still open under this gate: deployment on the real admin hostname and the real-edge check (DA-9); the login/logout audit record (G-AUDIT); the tenant boundary composition (the reauthentication mode is shared code there, and no client or entry uses it); and the pre-operation security requirement (the concurrent-revocation race, still blocks production session activation), not re-judged by this stage. Nothing is deployed. Residuals: DA-34 closed; new DA-59–DA-62.

**M6-ADMIN-AUDIT-P1 narrows only the login/logout-audit part of this gate, and only for the administrative boundary, in code only ([03 §2b](./03-backend-control-plane-login-session-blueprint.md), [ADR-29](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):**
- **Login and reauthentication.** Each is one store call, `establish`: one PostgreSQL transaction ends the live presented session, creates the new one and writes its `audit_event` row. The row carries the actor that this login's own admission selected. An administrative admission that names no scope is now a 503 `admission_unavailable`.
- **Logout.** Logout is `end`: when a live session is there, one transaction ends it and writes its row. The row has a null actor and carries the `sessionRef` the login row gave that session; the store derives both, and no database link enforces it. When nothing is live, `end` answers `absent`, records nothing, and the logout is a 204. If `end` fails, a warning is logged and the plain revocation runs. The answer is then 204 with the record unconfirmed, or 503 with the cookie cleared if the revocation fails too (DA-64).
- **Failure before commit.** A failure before commit now keeps the presented session and issues nothing. A commit whose answer was lost is an outcome unknown (DA-61).
- **Unchanged.** No route, migration, grant or routine is added.

The gate stays OPEN. Still open under this gate:
- deployment on the real admin hostname and the real-edge check (DA-9);
- the tenant boundary composition, whose session changes stay unaudited (DA-65);
- the pre-operation security requirement: the concurrent-revocation race still blocks production session activation, and this stage does not re-judge it.

Nothing is deployed. Residuals: DA-61 rewritten, the DA-35 decision recorded, and new DA-63–DA-69.

**M5-ADMIN-REVOKE-P1 implements the pre-operation security requirement's create condition and the in-transaction re-verification of sensitive commands for the administrative boundary, in code only; its stamp-update clause is not implemented and is an open owner decision (DA-77) ([ADR-30](./10-architecture-decision-records.md), migration 015; status block above).** Every administrative change — a logout, a revocation, a grant removal or restoration, an account-status change, an identity-link insert or change, or an identity-anchor change — takes effect when its transaction commits; the one recorded exception is DA-76. Logins, recent sign-ins and commands are each ordered against those changes in their own transaction. DA-76 is the one standing input left unordered: a tenant or store status change that moves the actor across 007's 200-grant bound, which the next request refuses.
- **Login and recent sign-in.** `establish` first reads the standing (013), takes migration 009's locks for the scope that read selected (the account FOR UPDATE, its links and grants FOR SHARE, held until COMMIT), and reads the standing again. The witness must equal the new record's.
  - Otherwise the answer is 401 `login_standing_changed`, and nothing is created, ended or recorded.
  - A recent sign-in also requires the presented session to be live, administrative and the same principal's, and to be ended by this same transaction. Otherwise the answer is 401 `login_reauth_no_session`, and again nothing is written.
- **Commands.** The committing transaction of a tenant-management command holds the actor's account, links and grants (009) and the row of the session that presented it (a touch that changes nothing). Under those locks it re-reads 013 for that session's provider reference. The recomputed witness must equal the session's, and the selected scope must equal the command's.
  - A change committed first: the command commits nothing and answers 503 `transaction_unavailable` (DA-70). The next request is 401.
  - A change not yet committed waits for the command's COMMIT.
- **Changes 009 cannot lock.** Under migration 015 these writes first take FOR KEY SHARE on the actor's account, so each waits for a command or login that holds it (DA-72, DA-73):
  - in every mode, an anchor repointed, and an identity link inserted for the actor or re-pointed to it;
  - in replica mode, where no foreign-key check or cascade runs, also a grant inserted for the actor or re-pointed to it, and an anchor deleted. In an ordinary session their foreign keys already made those wait.
- **Reads.** A request's session is decided by its session read and its authority by its admission read. A change committed before the read that decides it refuses the request (401).
  - Permitted, not a defect: a request may complete with 200 when its session read came before a logout or revocation and its admission read came before a standing change. That includes a logout that commits between the request's session read and its admission read, because the admission read never reads the session row (M5R-02c answered 200 in each of those three runs).
  - Such a request's refresh runs against the deleted row, so it recreates nothing, and the next request is 401 `session_unknown`.
- **Outage.** An outage before COMMIT is a bounded 503 that writes and revokes nothing. A COMMIT whose answer was lost is still an outcome unknown (DA-61(a)).

**Evidence.** The evidence comes from disposable PostgreSQL only: `tests/db/adminRevocation.integration.test.mjs`, 27 tests over two production roots on two kernels, with competing writes scheduled behind confirmed row locks.
- On the base code (`1e14207`) the suite failed 13 of 27, each a case the old code did not order.
- It passed 27 of 27 in three runs on this stage's code with migration 015 at `0ed1a314…`, before the final change to 015, which only adds the refusal unless the applier owns schema `tmpos_admission`. The stage's final verification and CI re-run the suite on the committed bytes.
- The races reproduced at the base are now refused or serialized: a recent sign-in or login committing after a logout, a revocation or a grant removal, and a command committing after its own logout, after a removal and restoration, or after an anchor repoint.
- `tests/db/platformAdmission.integration.test.mjs` M5P-09–M5P-11 prove 015's waits (in replica mode too), its non-waits and its postcondition.

The gate stays OPEN. Still open under this gate:
- deployment on the real admin hostname and the real-edge check (DA-9);
- the stamp-update clause of the pre-operation requirement on the administrative boundary: an open owner decision (DA-77);
- the tenant boundary composition. Its login still revokes and creates with no standing re-read, so the pre-operation requirement stays unmet there, and its session changes stay unaudited (DA-65).

Nothing is deployed. Residuals: DA-61(b) closed; DA-63 resolved as a classification; DA-49's last sentence superseded; DA-67 unchanged; new DA-70–DA-77.

**M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1 orders and audits the tenant boundary's sessions, in code only ([ADR-31](./10-architecture-decision-records.md), migration 016).** Every tenant-plane standing change takes effect when its transaction commits (for every writer but the owner acting outside 016's rules, DA-41's residuals): a change to the account, an identity link or anchor change, a grant of any scope inserted, changed, removed, restored or re-pointed, a tenant or store status change, a store moved to another tenant, and a tenant or store deleted. A tenant's or a store's key never changes: 016 refuses the UPDATE. The tenant login and recent sign-in are ordered against it in their own transaction. This is the tenant half of the pre-operation create condition, closed in code.
- **Login and recent sign-in.** `establish` is one transaction. Its first step holds the actor's standing until COMMIT (016's `m5_hold_tenant_standing`: the account FOR UPDATE, then its links, every grant, every tenant and every store a grant names FOR SHARE, each in key order). Its next statement re-reads `m5_admit_tenant_session_v2`, and the recomputed `t2:` witness must equal the new record's.
  - Otherwise the answer is 401 `login_standing_changed`, and nothing is ended, created or recorded.
  - A recent sign-in also requires the presented session to be live, of the tenant audience and the same principal's, and to be ended by this same transaction. Otherwise the answer is 401 `login_reauth_no_session`, and nothing is written. At most one of several concurrent recent sign-ins presenting one cookie succeeds.
- **Changes.** A change committed before the re-read refuses the login. A change not yet committed waits for the login's COMMIT: an account write; a link or grant of the actor changed or removed; a grant or link inserted for the actor or re-pointed to it; an anchor change; and an update or delete of a tenant or store that a grant names, 010's status command included (DA-78). The next protected request then refuses.
- **Reads.** ADR-30's rule, unchanged (DA-63).
- **Outage.** A fault in the hold, the re-read or the store is a bounded 503 that ends, creates and records nothing. A COMMIT whose answer was lost is a 503 with the outcome unknown.
- **Audit.** Each successful tenant login, recent sign-in and logout writes one `tenant.session.*` row in the same transaction (scope `none`; the actor the tenant admission resolved, none at logout). A logout whose audited end fails falls back to the plain revocation (204 `logout_audit_unconfirmed`, DA-64).
- **A monotonic witness (DA-41).** Tenant and store access versions move on every status change, a store's also on a move to another tenant, and a store INSERT starts at 1. A tenant's `tenant_id` and a store's `store_id` never change: the UPDATE raises 23514, as a replica too. A per-actor counter moves on every tenant- or store-scope grant write and on every delete of a tenant or store such a grant names. So a session idle across a restoration, a move and move back, or a delete and re-insert as a replica is refused, and a replica re-key followed by an INSERT under the old key cannot happen (M5A-16b).

**Evidence.** Disposable PostgreSQL only. `tests/db/tenantSessionOrdering.integration.test.mjs` runs two production roots on two kernels. On the base (`a4328974`) it had 42 tests: 16 passed, 25 failed and 1 was skipped; each failure was a case the base did not order, record or version. Against 016's first draft, which moved versions on status changes only, M5T-H3–H5 failed (an idle store session served again), and the final 016 refuses them. It now has 48 tests. The stage's final verification and CI run it on the committed bytes. `tests/db/tenantAdmission.integration.test.mjs` M5A-14–M5A-20 cover 016, M5A-16b the refused re-key.

**Stores, stated separately.** DA-41's store case is closed in code (M5T-H2–H5). A store login is ordered like a tenant one: a store suspension committed after its admission read and before the hold is 401 `login_standing_changed`, with nothing created or recorded (M5T-H7), and one issued while the login holds the store waits for its COMMIT, after which the next request is 401 (M5T-H8). A tenant-scope grant carries no store version by design (DA-41). No store route and no store permission is in production: every store permission is undecidable, and so is every tenant permission except the tenant route key `view_customers` (Customers at View, added in M7A-CUSTOMERS-P1); startup refuses any requirement it cannot decide. The deployable entry still composes `audiences: ['admin']` only. *(M7B-INVOICES-P1, [ADR-47](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): in code, not deployed, the tenant entry now serves three store routes, `/api/v1/stores/{storeId}/invoice-drafts` (POST and GET) and `.../{id}` (GET), under two store route permissions decidable at store scope only, `view_invoice_drafts` and `create_invoice_drafts`; every other store permission is still undecidable. A store route counts only the actor's own active store grant at exactly the path store, never a tenant grant, and the revalidator re-selects that grant inside the save's transaction; `GET /api/v1/stores` lists the stores so usable. Nothing is in production.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): six store templates after commit `573de6c` — the drafts' save, list, reopen and creation eligibility, the edit (`PATCH …/invoice-drafts/{id}`) and its edit eligibility (`GET …/invoice-drafts/{id}/edit-eligibility`) — under three store route permissions, `view_invoice_drafts`, `create_invoice_drafts` and `edit_invoice_drafts`; the revalidator re-selects the store grant inside the edit's transaction too.)*

The gate stays OPEN. Still open under this gate:
- deployment on the real admin hostname and the real-edge check (DA-9);
- the stamp-update clause of the pre-operation requirement, on both boundaries: an open owner decision (DA-77);
- the tenant boundary's deployment. Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 (commit 2; [ADR-33](./10-architecture-decision-records.md) decision 8) it is composed in code by the compiled tenant entry (`server/entry/tenantMain.ts`, `npm run start:tenant`), which serves the tenant login, current session and logout and `GET /api/v1/customers` (and, since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3, `POST /api/v1/customers`, [ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md); since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1, `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels`, [ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md); since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2, `GET /api/v1/roles/levels`, [ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md); since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`, [ADR-38](./10-architecture-decision-records.md); since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile`, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md); since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}`, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)) on the tenant host alone and composes the session store with the tenant issuer key. No tenant hostname is configured, the real-edge check is not done (DA-107), and nothing is deployed (DA-87).

Nothing is deployed. G-IAM and G-AUDIT stay OPEN. Residuals: DA-41 closed in code; DA-61(b) closed for the tenant boundary; DA-65 delivered for the tenant audience; DA-60, DA-61(a), DA-64, DA-66, DA-67, DA-68, DA-71, DA-76 and DA-77 updated; new DA-78–DA-82, DA-80 closed in code. | BLOCKER |
| **G-CLIENTAUTH** | Browser role/`sessionStorage` establishes authority | Platform | M5 | Server derives authorization; client role/entitlement demoted to render-only | No server decision depends on client-supplied role/entitlement/status | OPEN | BLOCKER |
| **G-IAM** | Canonical IAM not enforced server-side | Platform IAM | M5 | Server authorization service resolves identity→membership→role→perm→entitlement→status; family-aware permission semantics | Deny-by-default; authz-matrix suite green; family contract enforced in all comparison paths | OPEN — M5-GIAM-D1-P1 (owner decision D1) delivers the **first server-enforced command**, `PATCH /admin/v1/tenants/display-name` under the canonical platform permission `edit_tenant_profile`: the server resolves the session's principal to its app-owned actor, selects that actor's one platform grant, decides the permission from the role's definition (no role-name shortcut, System Owner by the same rule), revalidates the actor inside the committing transaction and audits it ([ADR-20](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)). **M5-GIAM-D1-P1-R1** makes that transaction-time check exact — the very grant and role authorized, exactly one active platform grant, the account neither denied nor read-only, the security version, and the permission re-decided on the role's current definition, with grant changes serialized against the command (migration 009, [ADR-21](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)). **M5-GIAM-D1-P2** adds the second command, `PATCH /admin/v1/tenants/status` under `change_tenant_status` (tenant_management at Manage or above, sensitive): exactly `active <-> suspended`, a bounded operator reason on the durable audit row, a trusted recent authentication re-judged on the database clock, and the tenant access version a suspension moves so that sessions admitted before it can be refused once an admission adapter reads it — which none does yet (migration 010, [ADR-22](./10-architecture-decision-records.md), DA-32). It also closes DA-31: every route requiring idempotency must now DECLARE a trusted authorization context, and startup refuses one that could execute without it. **M5-GIAM-D1-P2-R1** makes that suspension ENFORCEABLE and closes DA-32: migration 011 adds the SCHEMA the tenant boundary was missing — the durable session table, its five fixed operations and one admission read that joins 007's grants to each tenant's `access_version` — and the adapters above it (`postgresSessionStore.ts`, `postgresTenantAdmission.ts`) digest that answer into the witness a session is bound to and re-checked against on every protected request, with no grace window ([ADR-23](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)). Proved over real sockets and disposable PostgreSQL: a suspension refuses the next request and revokes the session, and a reactivation does not revive it. It does NOT advance the tenant plane's authorization: the role-to-permission decision stays undecidable (G-IAM/D1 — owner decision D1 on the first tenant-plane production route; GAP-11 itself is closed at repository level), **no production tenant route exists**, and the proof runs against a harness-only witness route (DA-39). **M5-GIAM-D1-P3** adds the first platform READ, `GET /admin/v1/tenants` under `view_tenants` (tenant_management at View or above) with a required trusted context, over migration 012's one `SECURITY DEFINER` routine, which re-confirms the named platform grant in the same snapshot as the page; and it binds both commands to the admin console's Tenant Management page ([ADR-24](./10-architecture-decision-records.md), DA-34, DA-44, DA-45). The page's `actions` hints are computed by the canonical rule and grant nothing: every command is authorized again by its own chain. **M5-GIAM-D1-P4** makes the administrative boundary's admission and authorization durable and production-composed in code ([ADR-25](./10-architecture-decision-records.md); G-CPLOGIN row): migration 013's definer read answers the actor's platform grants and a monotonic platform authority version in one snapshot, an administrative session is re-admitted on every protected request against a witness of that standing, exactly one platform grant is admitted (no first match; a tenant or store grant confers no platform authority), and the authorizer decides on the scope that same request's admission selected. It closes nothing here: the step-up experience (DA-34) stays open, and role definitions stay code (DA-47). **M6-RATE-P2** changes nothing here either: it adds the distributed rate-limit store only; the step-up experience and the first tenant-plane route (D1) stay open, and DA-47 is carried unchanged. **M6-ENTRY-P1** changes nothing here: DA-47 is unchanged, and no tenant or store production route exists. **M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1** changes nothing here either: the status command's permission, its authorization chain and its recent-authentication rule are unchanged, and the step-up journey only lets the same administrator renew that authentication; DA-47 is carried, and D1 stays open. Two platform commands and one platform read are not canonical IAM: tenant/store-scope authorization, durable custom roles and their editor, entitlement and status authority for every other action, the production composition of the tenant boundary (the admin boundary is now composed in code by the deployable entry, not deployed), and every other command stay open. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P1** decides the first tenant-plane permission, in code only (owner decision D1 for one route, [ADR-32](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): `GET /api/v1/customers` under the new canonical tenant route permission `view_customers` (the Customers domain at View or above), decided at tenant scope only by the catalog's own materializer on the role of the actor's one active tenant grant. Every other tenant and store key stays undecidable, and startup still refuses a route requiring one (`route_permission_undecidable`). The route is served by no deployment, and staff holding only store grants are refused (DA-87); since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 (commit 2) the compiled tenant entry composes it in code, not deployed. The server decides from the four built-in roles' catalog defaults, so a role an owner edited in the client is not honoured server-side (DA-85). **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3** decides the second tenant-plane permission, in code only (the owner's decision for one route, [ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): `POST /api/v1/customers` under `create_customers`, the Customers domain at Create or above, at tenant scope only. It is decided by the same materializer, and no role default is changed: `store_owner`, `manager` and `sales_staff` hold it, `technician` does not, and a `read_only` limitation (an account or tenant `read_only` or `overdue`) denies it. It is decided again inside the committing transaction, on the role of the very grant the authorizer selected, and migration 019 restates it in SQL as the fixed role set {`store_owner`, `manager`, `sales_staff`}, pinned to the catalog by a parity test. Every other tenant and store key stays undecidable. A role an owner narrowed in the client can now create customers through the server (DA-85). Only the compiled tenant entry serves it, and it is not deployed. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1** makes both customer permissions follow an owner's edit, in code only ([ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): migration 020 stores, per tenant, the Customers level of each of the three editable built-in roles (`manager`, `technician`, `sales_staff`; the default until edited; `store_owner` Full and never stored), and the store owner alone edits it through `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` under the new sensitive, owner-only tenant route permission `edit_role_levels`, with a recent sign-in, a reason, an audit record and tenant-scope idempotency. Both are decided on the stored level: `view_customers` by the authorizer's levels read on every decided request, then in the database by 020's `m7a_customer_page_v3` (the read has no committing transaction and no revalidator); `create_customers` by the authorizer, again by the revalidator in the create's committing transaction, and again by 020's create routine. 020's routines replace 019's role set and 018's level-free read; `tmpos_app` no longer executes either. A narrowing below a route's required level denies that role's next such request, an already-issued session included. The shop app's role editor is unchanged and calls no route, so a narrowing made there still does not reach the server; custom roles, other domains, deletion and delegated editing stay client-only, and owner-only editing is flagged for owner confirmation (DA-85, narrowed; DA-124). **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2** connects the shop app's role screen to the server for the built-in roles' Customers level, in code only ([ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): it signs in to the tenant boundary, reads the four built-in roles' levels through the new owner-only `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` (`edit_role_levels`, no recent sign-in) and changes `manager`, `technician` and `sales_staff` through R1's PUT, calling a change saved only after an exact 200 and a matching same-tenant read-back. R1's "the shop app's role editor is unchanged and calls no route" is superseded for built-in Customers levels. Owner-only editing stays flagged for owner confirmation, contested and unresolved; a manager's read is refused 403 and the panel shows no levels. Custom roles, other domains, deletion and delegated editing stay client-only (DA-85, narrowed; DA-124). **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 (Part 1)** makes the owner's level edit conditional, in code only ([ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): the PUT asserts the tenant and the per-role version of the read the owner reviewed, and migration 021's edit routine judges both inside the committing transaction. It first takes the authority locks (016's standing hold, the session and mint-ledger rows and the issuer key FOR SHARE). It then runs the unlocked checks, the tenant check and a preliminary version check, before any role row is inserted or locked. Only then does it take the role row FOR UPDATE and make the authoritative version check on the locked version row. It writes nothing in either tenant when either assertion fails (an unsealed 409 `write_conflict`). Both are preconditions, never selectors: the tenant written is still the one the presenting session's grant names. `tmpos_app` no longer executes 020's unconditional edit. This closes DA-125 for the built-in Customers server-backed editor; the owner rule, owner-only editing (DA-124), custom roles, other domains, deletion and delegated editing are unchanged. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2** adds a third route under `view_customers`, in code only ([ADR-38](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`. The authorizer decides it on the role's current level, and migration 022's session-bound routine decides it again in the database, with 017's rechecks; no permission or role default changes. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5** gives the three customer routes their first shop client, in code only ([ADR-39](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): a separate Customer directory page lists the server's customers, opens one by id and adds one by name over the shop's existing tenant session, and sends no tenant, store, role or actor. No permission, role default or route changes: the server decides `view_customers` and `create_customers` exactly as before, and a role narrowed while the page is open is refused on its next request. The page offers its add form from the browser's local role state, which cannot see the server-side level; that is a hint, and the server's 403 is the answer (DA-140). The gate stays OPEN. *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the new profile read, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile`, which answers a customer's phone and email, is under the existing `view_customers`; no permission or role default changes. Whether contact details need their own permission is an owner decision (DA-153). The gate stays OPEN.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): a fourth tenant route permission, `edit_customers`, the Customers domain at Edit or above, for `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}`. It is decided by the same materializer on the role's effective level, by the authorizer, again by the revalidator in the committing transaction and again by 024's routine; no role default changes (`store_owner` and `manager` hold it, `technician` and `sales_staff` do not), `read_only` denies it, and the load-time threshold set admits exactly View, Create and Edit. The shop offers Edit from its local role hint, and the server's 403 decides (DA-140, amended). The gate stays OPEN.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8, [ADR-43](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the list's optional name search, `GET /api/v1/customers?q=…`, is under the existing `view_customers`; no permission or role default changes. Migration 025's routine decides the list's authority again in the database by restating 022's steps, so the read's derivation now has two copies, held together by the database suites (`tests/db/customerSearch.integration.test.mjs` M7CS-06). The gate stays OPEN.)* *(M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1, [ADR-46](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the store owner can now also store the Invoices level of `manager`, `technician` and `sales_staff`, through the same `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` with domain `invoices` under the same owner rule (`edit_role_levels`), and read it back through the new `GET /api/v1/roles/levels/invoices`, over migration 026's invoices-only routines. No permission key, role default or level ordering changes. The level is configuration only: no served route decides on it, and no invoice operation is authorized or enforced by the server (DA-183, DA-184). Custom roles, every other domain, deletion and delegated editing stay client-only, and owner-only editing stays flagged (DA-124). The gate stays OPEN.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P1, [ADR-47](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the first store-scope authority, in code only. A store route is decided on the actor's own active store grant at exactly the path store (no tenant-to-store inheritance; none or two grants there refuse), with `view_invoice_drafts` and `create_invoice_drafts` on that grant's role at the tenant's owner-edited Invoices and Customers levels, re-decided inside the save's transaction and again by migration 028's routine under locks (`tests/db/invoiceDraftsHttp.integration.test.mjs` IDH-02 to IDH-04, IDH-09; `tests/db/invoiceDraftRoutine.integration.test.mjs` IDR-13). The stored Invoices level decides those three draft operations and nothing else (DA-183). Store grants are provisioned outside the product (DA-188). The gate stays OPEN.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): a third store route permission, `edit_invoice_drafts` (Invoices at Edit or above, no Customers level), decides the draft edit and its edit eligibility; the edit is decided again in its transaction by the revalidator and by migration 029's routine, on the unlocked level and, on the change path only, on the locked role row (DA-204). *(M7B-INVOICES-P2-R1: from migration 030 the edit takes the role's guard key SHARED after the draft header, for manager, technician and sales_staff, and decides the level then; it locks no role row. DA-204 is closed for the runtime path while 030's trigger is enabled.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P1-R3: from migration 031 the draft save takes the same key SHARED after its customer read, for manager, technician and sales_staff, and decides the Invoices level then; it locks no Invoices role row. One route is not status enforcement across the product, and nothing is deployed. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 ([ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)) adds a second: `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` requires limitation `none`, and 020's edit routine admits only an account and tenant status of `active` or `trialing` | HIGH |

| **G-PCI** | Integrated payment capability with no card data touching TM POS2026 | Product/Security | M7b | **DECIDED (direction):** integrated card-present via PCI-certified **semi-integrated terminal/provider** + hosted/tokenized CNP + cash + external-terminal fallback; **TM POS2026 never receives/stores/logs/exposes** raw PAN/CVV/track/PIN/unencrypted CHD or processor creds in browser storage. Provider selected in M7b via documented criteria; auth/capture/settlement/void/refund/dispute/reconciliation; verified webhooks; idempotency + duplicate-charge prevention; durable audit; credential encryption/rotation; provider-outage behavior; terminal/device assignment scoped to tenant/store. **PCI validation category confirmed with provider/acquirer/QSA before production — no SAQ category claimed until integration architecture establishes eligibility.** As a multi-tenant orchestrator holding provider credentials, terminal-device assignments, and payment tokens, the platform's **own PCI service-provider scope (TPSP / Payment-Facilitator classification, provider responsibility matrix, per-tenant AoC obligations)** is validated with the QSA/acquirer alongside the merchant category — not assumed away. **Acceptance also requires: STORE-owned gateway configuration (Store Settings → Payments, not System Owner CP) + explicit canonical store-scoped permission ([04 §2.1](./04-canonical-iam-and-four-user-migration.md)) + credential isolation (OAuth/hosted-preferred, envelope-encrypted, store-scoped reference, no browser secret) + multi-store isolation + verified webhooks + payment lifecycle/reconciliation + validated PCI scope.** | Provider selected + integration architecture built + **store-owned config + canonical store permission + credential/multi-store isolation** + merchant **and** service-provider PCI classification validated + evidence (webhook/idempotency/reconciliation/audit/redaction) PASS | OPEN | BLOCKER |
| **G-PRIVACY** | Privacy & DSAR foundation for multi-tenant PII | Legal/Platform | M1 (contracts) → M5 (authz) → M6 (audit/workflow/retention) → M7a–h (domain export/correct/delete) → M8 (ops) → M9 (evidence + legal sign-off) | **DECIDED (direction): implement, not exclude.** Data inventory/classification; tenant-controller/platform-processor split; identity verification; tenant approval + platform escalation; cross-domain discovery; export/correction/deletion-anonymization (where legally permitted); retention/legal-hold/restriction/portability; consent/notice linkage; provider propagation; immutable audit; deadline/status tracking; failure/partial-completion handling; backup & deletion limitations; documented jurisdiction/legal-review boundaries. **No universal-compliance claim** — final policy/jurisdiction requires owner/legal review. *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): server customers now hold an optional phone and email, personal data stored in clear, read through the profile read under `view_customers` with no audit record (DA-153); their retention and deletion is an explicit pre-live decision (DA-155). The gate stays OPEN.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): an authorized user can now correct or clear a phone or email. Clearing one sets the column to NULL: a field update, not an erasure of earlier values from backups, the write-ahead log or a database log that captured bind parameters (DA-155, amended). Who can read contacts is unchanged (DA-153). The gate stays OPEN.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8, [ADR-43](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): server customers can now be searched by name. The search text travels in the GET request's query: the application never logs it and the shop keeps it out of the address, history and storage, but infrastructure in front of a deployment may record it in an access log, so its redaction is a pre-deployment requirement (DA-167, DA-171). The gate stays OPEN.)* | DSAR export/correction/deletion flows implemented & tested across PII domains + immutable audit + legal-owner sign-off (M9) | OPEN | HIGH |
| **G-ISOLATION** | No tenant/store isolation (`tenant-1` hardcoded) | Platform | M5/M7 | Cross-tenant + cross-store denial tests per domain | Tenant A cannot access B; store role cannot escape store | OPEN — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 (code only, [ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)) creates a customer only in the tenant the database derives from the presenting minted session, compared with the authorizer's trusted tenant, actor and grant before COMMIT. The tenant is never read from the request, and an idempotency key cannot replay across tenants or actors. This is proven on disposable PostgreSQL only. The runtime-role residuals DA-94, DA-97 and DA-115 remain, no store route exists, and nothing is deployed. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 ([ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)) stores an owner's role levels per tenant, and the edit routine derives the tenant from the presenting session as the create does; an owner's edit changes only its own tenant (`tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-06). The levels read answers any tenant's levels to `tmpos_app` with no key (DA-121) *(M7B-INVOICES-P1, [ADR-47](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the first cross-store denial tests: another store of the tenant, another tenant's store, a missing store and a non-canonical segment are one 403 for the draft list, reopen and save, writing nothing, and a foreign draft is the missing one's 404 (`tests/db/invoiceDraftsHttp.integration.test.mjs` IDH-02; `tests/db/invoiceDraftRoutine.integration.test.mjs` IDR-04); composite foreign keys keep a draft in its store's tenant and a line under its draft's scope. One domain, on disposable PostgreSQL only; the gate stays OPEN.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P2, [ADR-48](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the edit answers a missing draft, another store's or tenant's, and a missing or another tenant's customer with one 404 writing nothing (`tests/db/invoiceDraftEditHttp.integration.test.mjs` IEH-07), and migration 029's row trigger keeps a draft's id, tenant, store, creation time and creator fixed on every update path, the table owner's included (`tests/db/invoiceDraftEditRoutine.integration.test.mjs` IDE-04). The gate stays OPEN.)* | BLOCKER |
| **G-INVENTORY-CONSISTENCY** | POS (M7b) may claim stock decrement before durable inventory (M7c) exists | Domain (POS/Inventory) | M7b→M7c (blocking dependency) | **No production sale may claim inventory was decremented unless a durable, transactional, idempotent inventory mutation OR obligation (outbox) exists and reconciliation passes.** The obligation is written **in the same transaction as sale completion**, with a **unique sale/idempotency key**, **durable obligation states** (pending/applied/failed), **replay-safe** M7c reconciliation, and **alerting + a production block on unresolved obligations**. Pre-M7c inventory-tracked sale completion is feature-disabled / non-inventory mode and **no decrement is exposed to the user until M7c reconciliation passes** | Inventory-tracked production POS blocked until M7c consumes the obligation and reconciliation passes; unresolved-obligation alert clean; no silent divergence | OPEN | BLOCKER |
| **G-APICOMPLETE** | UI actions have no backend | API | M3/M7 | UI→API→DB parity map; every user action hits an authorized API | No production UI action relies on mock/in-memory state | OPEN | BLOCKER |
| **G-DBCONSTRAINTS** | Missing FKs/uniqueness/indexes on business data | DBA | M7 | Schema with tenant/store keys, FKs, natural-key uniqueness, indexes | Constraints present & tested per domain | OPEN | HIGH |
| **G-DBROLE** | App connects as owner, bypasses RLS | DBA | M3/M5 | Scoped app role + RLS policies; migration/owner role separated. **Database-owner provisioning belongs to this gate, not to the migration:** the tenant runtime database must have its database-level `TEMPORARY` and `CREATE` grants to `PUBLIC` closed by the **database owner**, because PostgreSQL grants `TEMPORARY` to `PUBLIC` by default, `PUBLIC` reaches every role regardless of that role's own attributes, and a `REVOKE` issued by a non-owner migration principal is a silent no-op that emits only a warning. **M3 S2 evidence:** migration 005 creates the NOLOGIN privilege roles `tmpos_app` / `tmpos_audit_writer`, marks each with an exact migration-ownership comment, and **fails closed** (`insufficient_privilege`) unless the effective database `TEMPORARY` **and** `CREATE` privilege is false for both, checked with `has_database_privilege` so every grant path (direct, inherited, `PUBLIC`) is seen at once — proved against disposable PostgreSQL in both directions, including the RED case where the owner step has not run. Its rollback refuses to remove a role that does not carry that marker, and restores no database-level privilege. **Caller cutover must repeat the same effective-privilege check against the real persistent LOGIN role, and must go further than the migration can:** a direct or inherited grant to that role is invisible to a check that inspects only the privilege roles (proved by mutation, both paths), and on PostgreSQL 16 a membership granted `WITH INHERIT FALSE, SET TRUE` makes `has_database_privilege` report **false** for the member while `SET ROLE` still reaches the privilege (proved live). The NOLOGIN privilege roles hold no membership at all, so migration 005's own gate is sound; a LOGIN principal that can `SET ROLE` is not covered by a single `has_database_privilege` reading and must additionally be shown to hold no SET-able membership. **M6-PG-P7-R1:** the M6 store's runtime connection, `APP_DATABASE_URL` — never `SUPABASE_DATABASE_URL`, and provisioned only once this gate has created the runtime LOGIN (holding `tmpos_app` and `tmpos_audit_writer`) and chosen its endpoint — is classified before any DNS, socket, TLS or SQL and accepted only for the direct database host or the shared session pooler on port 5432 (`server/platform-identity/databaseEndpoint.ts`); a transaction pooler, shared or dedicated (6543), is refused, because the store's kernel resets each connection's session before reuse (DA-21). A session-pooler username keeps the provider's `<role>.<ref>` form exactly as configured; nothing builds it. No login, secret or endpoint has been created. **M5-GIAM-D1-P4 narrows one more path:** the administrative boundary's admission no longer needs the owner connection. It reads through migration 013's `SECURITY DEFINER` routine as `tmpos_app`, which holds USAGE on `tmpos_admission` and EXECUTE on that one read, and no privilege on the version table or the trigger function (013's section 6 refuses otherwise). The transaction root no longer builds the direct 007 resolver. **M6-ENTRY-P1 narrows only the enforced-startup-preflight part of this gate, in code only:** the startup preflight (`server/persistence/postgresStartupPreflight.ts`, `runtimeLogin`) refuses to let the entry listen unless the APP_DATABASE_URL login holds no superuser, BYPASSRLS, CREATEROLE, CREATEDB or replication attribute; exactly the tmpos_app and tmpos_audit_writer memberships (inherited, no ADMIN OPTION), every membership followed whatever its options; no owned objects; and one direct privilege — SELECT on public.schema_migrations, with no grant option — which no migration grants: provisioning it is a G-DBROLE operator action, recorded here as new. It also refuses (`public_privilege_unexpected`) when PUBLIC holds a relation or column privilege, can execute a SECURITY DEFINER routine, or is named by a policy in the application's schemas (`public`, `tmpos_*`); PUBLIC-executable SECURITY INVOKER routines there are allowed, and PUBLIC's standing outside those schemas is not re-read (DA-54). Each privilege role's policy lines pin the command, PERMISSIVE or RESTRICTIVE, and ROW SECURITY while its table enforces it, so a disabled table or a widened command refuses; the predicates are not re-read (DA-55). A privilege role holding any membership (`role_membership_unexpected`), a dependency in another database of the cluster naming the login or a privilege role (`foreign_database_dependency`) and a second ledger row for one version (`migration_ledger_malformed`) also refuse. The preflight runs once, at startup (DA-56), and is proven against disposable PostgreSQL only. What stays open: managed role provisioning — the persistent LOGIN and its endpoint; the tenant context a session cannot forge (DA-29); the runtime role's reach into 007's resolver and 013's read for any provider reference (DA-45, DA-46); the table owner's and a superuser's power over 013's triggers and version rows (DA-48); and the managed database-ACL provisioning (owner-side action, not authorized). | Tenant paths run under RLS as a non-owner role; **and** the runtime LOGIN role holds no effective database-level `TEMPORARY` or `CREATE` — through a direct grant, a role membership, or `PUBLIC` | **OPEN — M6-ENTRY-P1 narrows only the enforced-startup-preflight part (migrations 001–014 at M6-ENTRY-P1, checked at startup; 001–015 since M5-ADMIN-REVOKE-P1; 001–016 since M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1; 001–017 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P1; 001–018 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2; 001–019 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3; 001–020 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1; 001–021 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4; 001–022 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2; 001–023 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6; 001–024 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7; 001–025 since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8): the deployable entry refuses to listen if the app login is outside the approved runtime boundary (the new direct privilege on the ledger table included). The check runs once, at startup, and re-reads neither PUBLIC's standing outside the application's schemas nor policy predicates (DA-54 to DA-56). No caller-side LOGIN provisioning has occurred; managed role provisioning and owner-side database-ACL provisioning remain later operator actions; only disposable PostgreSQL has been contacted. M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1 changes nothing here: no grant or role changes and no migration, and the reauthentication mode reads the presented session through the existing store operations only. M5-ADMIN-REVOKE-P1 adds migration 015 and no grant or role: the preflight's ledger step now requires exactly 001–015, and the approved runtime boundary (`APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS`) is unchanged. M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1 adds migration 016 and two grants, EXECUTE on `tmpos_session.m5_admit_tenant_session_v2` and on `tmpos_session.m5_hold_tenant_standing` to `tmpos_app`, and no role: the ledger step now requires exactly 001–016, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` names both grants. 016's postcondition refuses any wider grant, any membership of its objects' owner, and any runtime role that may UPDATE the status or access version of a store or tenant or DELETE either, UPDATE a tenant's `tenant_id` or a store's `store_id` or `tenant_id`, INSERT a tenant, or TRUNCATE or place a TRIGGER on `public.tenant`, `public.store` or `public.user_membership`. 005's table-wide INSERT on `public.store` stays and reaches the new column, but 016's trigger sets a new store's access version to 1 whatever the INSERT names (DA-80). The hold is a lock the runtime role can take on any actor's standing (DA-82). M7A-CUSTOMERS-P1 adds migration 017 and two grants to `tmpos_app`, USAGE on schema `tmpos_customer` and EXECUTE on `tmpos_customer.m7a_customer_page`, and no role: the ledger step now requires exactly 001–017, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` names both grants. `tmpos_app` holds no privilege on the customer table, which has row security enabled with no policy; 017's postcondition requires row security enabled and not forced on the table, and `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE and USAGE actually held. It refuses any wider grant, any column grant, any policy on the table, any runtime role that is a member of an owner of its schema, table or routine, and any runtime role (`tmpos_app`, `tmpos_audit_writer`, `anon`, `authenticated`) that holds an effective privilege on the table by any path, `pg_read_all_data` included (011's check). The routine runs with `row_security = off`, so a row security later FORCED on the table makes it fail (a 503) rather than answer empty pages. A second tenant check sits in trusted source: the routine returns each row's tenant id, and the adapter refuses (503) any row that does not name the admitted tenant. The routine's grant re-check refuses stale authority but is not a boundary against the runtime credential (DA-83). M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 (commit 1, code only; [ADR-33](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)) adds migration 018 and no role: schema `tmpos_mint` with the issuer key and the mint ledger, on which no runtime role holds a privilege; USAGE on the schema and EXECUTE on `m7a_session_create`, `m7a_customer_page_v2` and `m7a_issuer_key_check` to `tmpos_app`; and two revocations, EXECUTE on 011's `m5_session_create` and on 017's `m7a_customer_page`. The ledger step now requires exactly 001–018, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` drops the two revoked grants and names the four new ones. DA-83 is contained against the runtime credential alone, not closed. Two new prerequisites belong to this gate: the database owner installs the tenant issuer key out of band, by a bound parameter from a session that does not log it (DA-101), and the tenant database's logging must not capture bind parameters (DA-102). Since commit 2 (the tenant entry, code only) that logging prerequisite is checked at tenant startup and as a readiness check (`database_logging_unsafe`), auto_explain's and pgaudit's own settings included once they are loaded, and the tenant entry refuses to listen unless the database answers `match` for its issuer key; `shared_preload_libraries` stays unreadable to the login and a provisioning check (DA-105). Neither is a check of a managed database. Residuals: DA-93 to DA-105, DA-107 and DA-108. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 (code only; [ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)) adds migration 019 and no role: schema `tmpos_customer_write`, with one `VOLATILE SECURITY DEFINER` routine, `m7a_customer_create`, whose only authority argument is a session store key. It grants USAGE and EXECUTE to `tmpos_app` alone, and `tmpos_app` still holds no privilege on the customer table. Its postcondition proves that no runtime role, nor any role it is a member of, holds a privilege on the session table, the mint ledger, the issuer key or the customer table, and that `tmpos_app` and `tmpos_audit_writer` are members of nothing. The ledger step requires exactly 001–019, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` names the two new grants. A new deployment condition: the database's default isolation must be READ COMMITTED (DA-113). Residuals: DA-94, DA-97, DA-113 and DA-115. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 (code only; [ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)) adds migration 020 and no role: schema `tmpos_role`, with the table `tenant_role_level` (row security enabled with no policy, not forced, no runtime-role privilege) and four `SECURITY DEFINER` routines, EXECUTE to `tmpos_app` alone. It revokes `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 019's create and 018's `m7a_customer_page_v2` and USAGE on `tmpos_customer_write`. Its postcondition also proves no runtime role reaches those by any path. The ledger step requires exactly 001–020, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` names USAGE on `tmpos_role` and its four EXECUTEs and drops the three revoked (32 function EXECUTEs). Residuals: DA-118 to DA-121 and DA-123.** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 (Part 1) keeps it OPEN ([ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** migration 021 adds schema `tmpos_role_edit` (one table the runtime role holds no privilege on, two routines `tmpos_app` may execute, and a trigger function granted to nobody) and revokes `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 020's `m7a_role_level_set`; its postcondition proves no runtime role reaches that routine, the trigger function or either table by any path. The ledger step requires exactly 001–021, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` adds USAGE on `tmpos_role_edit` and EXECUTE on `m7a_role_level_states` and `m7a_role_level_set_v2` and drops the revoked one (33 function EXECUTEs). Residuals: DA-119 and DA-121 amended; DA-129 new (the table owner). **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2 keeps it OPEN ([ADR-38](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** migration 022 adds schema `tmpos_customer_detail` with one session-bound `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` read, `m7a_customer_get`; `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE only and still holds no privilege on the customer table. 022's postcondition checks the runtime ROLES (`tmpos_app`, `tmpos_audit_writer`, `anon`, `authenticated`) through membership, as 020's and 021's do. The runtime LOGIN's own shape is enforced by the startup preflight: `tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` M7CD-02b shows an inherited carrier, a SET-only carrier and a direct grant refused. The ledger step requires exactly 001–022, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` adds USAGE on `tmpos_customer_detail` and EXECUTE on `m7a_customer_get` (34 function EXECUTEs). Residuals: DA-132 new (a direct call with a live key). **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 ([ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** migration 023 creates schema `tmpos_customer_contact` with two `SECURITY DEFINER` routines, `m7a_contact_create` and `m7a_contact_profile`; `tmpos_app` gets USAGE on the schema and EXECUTE on both, loses EXECUTE on 020's `tmpos_role.m7a_customer_create`, and still holds no privilege on the customer table or its columns. The ledger step requires exactly 001–023, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` adds that USAGE and the two EXECUTEs and drops 020's create (35 function EXECUTEs). DA-118 is amended (the direct create now goes through 023's routine), not closed. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 ([ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** migration 024 creates schema `tmpos_customer_edit` with two `SECURITY DEFINER` routines, `m7a_customer_update` and `m7a_customer_profile`; `tmpos_app` gets USAGE on the schema and EXECUTE on both, loses EXECUTE on 023's `m7a_contact_profile`, and still holds no privilege on the customer table or its columns; no column grant is made on the audit table. The ledger step requires exactly 001–024, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` adds that USAGE and the two EXECUTEs and drops 023's profile read (36 function EXECUTEs). DA-118 is amended (a direct edit is unaudited too), not closed. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 ([ADR-43](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** migration 025 creates schema `tmpos_customer_search` with one `STABLE` `SECURITY DEFINER` routine, `m7a_customer_search`; `tmpos_app` gets USAGE on the schema and EXECUTE on the routine, nothing else, and still holds no privilege on the customer table or its columns. The ledger step requires exactly 001–025, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` adds that USAGE and that EXECUTE (37 function EXECUTEs). A holder of `tmpos_app` and a live minted key can search that key's tenant directly, the class of the list's and the detail read's direct calls; no other tenant is reachable. *(M7B-INVOICES-P1-R4: the ledger step now requires exactly 001–032 (G-MIGRATE), and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` holds `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 032's `create_v3` in place of 031's `create_v2`.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1: the ledger step now requires exactly 001–033, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` holds `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 033's discard routine, `page_v2` and `get_v2` and its USAGE on schema `tmpos_invoice_discard`, in place of its EXECUTE on 028's page and get.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-P1: the ledger step now requires exactly 001–034, and `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS` adds `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 034's six routines and its USAGE on schema `tmpos_invoice_issue`; no runtime role holds a table privilege on 034's four tables.)* | HIGH |
| **G-MIGRATE** | Migrations never auto-applied; drift risk | Infra | M3 | Migration runner + applied/rolled-back tests in CI | Runner applies/rolls back; no manual-only application | OPEN — M6-PG-P4 adds migration 006, applied to disposable databases only. While 006 is pending, the managed apply of 005 refuses at its unchanged exact-[005] plan gate; planning to a target version, or authorizing 005 and 006 together, is a separate decision. M5-ID-P1 adds 007, M5-GIAM-D1-P1 adds 008, M5-GIAM-D1-P1-R1 adds 009, M5-GIAM-D1-P2 adds 010, M5-GIAM-D1-P2-R1 adds 011, M5-GIAM-D1-P3 adds 012, M5-GIAM-D1-P4 adds 013 and M6-RATE-P2 adds 014, all applied to disposable databases only; 014's rollback refuses first unless it runs at READ COMMITTED (at a stricter level an earlier statement's snapshot could hide a window opened while its lock was awaited), takes its `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` locks as a statement of their own before its check, refuses while any rate-limit bucket is still inside its window, and otherwise drops every bucket and the registered `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` fingerprint. **Owner decision D4 (policy only, recorded M5-GIAM-D1-P1):** a future managed apply, when separately authorized, targets the complete contiguous chain through the newest committed migration — now 001–033 (M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-P1 updates the target to 001–034 and adds its history below; M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1 updates the target to 001–033 and adds its history below; M7B-INVOICES-P1-R4 updates the target to 001–032 and adds its history below; M7B-INVOICES-P1-R3 updates the target to 001–031 and adds its history below; M7B-INVOICES-P2-R1 updates the target to 001–030 and adds its history below; M7B-INVOICES-P2 updates the target to 001–029 and corrects this line, which M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1 and M7B-INVOICES-P1 had left at 001–025 although they added 026, 027 and 028; M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 had updated it to 001–025 and corrected it, which M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 and P7 had left at 001–022 although their summaries name the chain through 023 and 024; 001–022 at M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2; 001–021 at P4 Part 1; 001–020 at M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1) — and 005 is never applied alone while later required migrations are pending. No managed apply is authorized; the exact-[005] gate is unchanged and still refuses the longer plan. M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1 adds no migration: 005–014 are byte-identical to the stage's entry commit, 014 still matches its pins, and no migration file follows 014, and D4 still names the chain through 014. M5-ADMIN-REVOKE-P1 adds 015 (`015_m5_admin_standing_serialization`), applied to disposable databases only. It creates one trigger function and three triggers, grants nothing and moves no data. It must be applied by the role that owns schema `tmpos_admission` (the one that applied 013) and refuses otherwise. Its rollback drops exactly the three triggers and the function, and 013's rollback refuses while 015 is applied. The runtime refuses to start without it: the startup preflight's ledger step requires exactly 001–015. 005–014 are byte-identical to the stage's entry commit, and D4 now names the chain through 015. M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1 adds 016 (`016_m5_tenant_session_ordering`), applied to disposable databases only. It adds one column, one table, two trigger functions, five triggers and two routines, grants EXECUTE on the two routines to `tmpos_app`, and moves no data. It must be applied by the owner of schema `tmpos_session` (the role that applied 011), acting as the owner of the seven tables it places triggers on, locks or reads as a definer, and it refuses without 015. Its up and its rollback each refuse while any tenant session row exists. 011's rollback refuses while 016 is applied, and 010's and 013's rollbacks do not check for it, so the chain must roll back newest first (DA-81). The runtime refuses to start without it: the ledger step requires exactly 001–016. 005–015 are byte-identical to the stage's entry commit, and D4 now names the chain through 016. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P1 adds 017 (`017_m7a_customer_directory`), applied to disposable databases only. It creates its own schema `tmpos_customer` with one table, one index and one routine, grants USAGE on the schema and EXECUTE on the routine to `tmpos_app`, and moves no data.
