It refuses unless 016, 007 and 005's `tmpos_app` are in place, the applier can execute 007's security version, and the applier holds the owner's privileges on `public.tenant`, `public.app_user`, `public.user_membership` and `public.identity_link`, none of them under forced row security. Its rollback refuses (55000) unless it runs under READ COMMITTED. It runs its no-rows guard with `row_security = off`, so for an applier without BYPASSRLS a forced row security on the table makes PostgreSQL refuse the rollback (42501) instead of hiding rows it would drop. It takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on `public.tenant` and then on the customer table, so it blocks every login, admission and command that uses `public.tenant` and must run in a maintenance window. That order avoids a deadlock with page reads only; a customer INSERT (tests only today) locks in the opposite order and can deadlock with the rollback, and PostgreSQL then aborts one side, losing nothing. It refuses (55000) while any customer row exists, so a rollback never deletes business records, and otherwise drops exactly the routine, the table and the schema. While any customer row exists the chain cannot roll back past 017: 005's rollback (which drops `tmpos_app`) and 002's (which drops `public.tenant`) fail while 017's grants and foreign key remain. The runtime refuses to start without it: the ledger step requires exactly 001–017. 005–016 are byte-identical to the stage's entry commit, and D4 now names the chain through 017. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 (commit 1) adds 018 (`018_m7a_tenant_session_mint`), applied to disposable databases only. It creates its own schema `tmpos_mint` with two tables, one index, two helpers and three routines, revokes two EXECUTE grants 011 and 017 gave `tmpos_app`, and moves no data. It refuses unless 017 and `tmpos_app` are in place, the applier can execute 011's create, 016's admission read and 017's page, and the applier holds the privileges of `tmpos_session.session`'s owner with row security not forced on it. Its rollback refuses unless it runs under READ COMMITTED, and while the issuer key or the ledger holds a row; it restores the two grants, which re-opens the DA-83 exposure, and leaves runtime code built for 018 unable to create a session, so code and database roll back together (DA-100). The runtime refuses to start without it: the ledger step requires exactly 001–018. A managed apply also needs the owner's key install (DA-101) and logging that cannot capture bind parameters (DA-102). 001–017 are byte-identical to the stage's entry commit, and D4 now names the chain through 018. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 commit 2 (the tenant entry) adds no migration: 001–018 are byte-identical to commit 1, no 019 exists, and D4 still names the chain through 018. The tenant entry refuses to start on a ledger that is not exactly 001–018 (`migration_state_invalid`) and on logging that could capture bind parameters. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 adds 019 (`019_m7a_customer_create`), applied to disposable databases only: one schema, one routine and two grants, with no table and no change to anything 001–018 created. It refuses unless 018 and `tmpos_app` are in place and the applier holds the owner's privileges on the four tables the routine rests on. Its rollback drops only its own objects and does not refuse while customers exist, but both entries then refuse to restart (`role_privilege_missing`). So 019 rolls back first, and with the code (DA-116). The runtime refuses to start without it: the ledger step requires exactly 001–019. 001–018 are byte-identical to the stage's entry commit, and D4 now names the chain through 019. M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 adds 020 (`020_m7a_tenant_role_levels`, [ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)), applied to disposable databases only: one schema, one table, four routines and three revoked grants, with no change to 001–019's bytes. It is deliberately not expand-contract: the shared startup preflight requires exactly 001–020 and 020's grants, so 020 and the runtime move together (the P3 build refuses a 020 database). Its down refuses while any stored level differs from its role's catalog default. The rollback order is owner reset first, while the 020 runtime serves; then the runtime to the P3 build; then 020 down with its ledger row. No runtime serves between the last two steps (the P3 build refuses a 020 database). The database does not enforce the order, and no order lets the rollback silently re-widen a narrowed role, since 020 down refuses until every stored level equals its default (the owner's reset widens it deliberately, through the audited route): run before the runtime switch, once the levels are reset, the 020 runtime fails closed (503) until replaced; a runtime switched to the P3 build before the owner's reset leaves no runtime able to start until the 020 runtime is rolled forward again; the owner then resets the levels through it, and the order above is followed (DA-122). 001–019 are byte-identical to the stage's entry commit, and D4 now names the chain through 020. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 (Part 1, [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds migration 021, applied, rolled back and re-applied on disposable PostgreSQL only (`tests/db/roleLevelVersionRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M7RV-01, M7RV-12); 001–020 are byte-identical to the entry commit `86c4ce8`. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–021, so 021 and the runtime move together, and the 020 runtime refuses a 021 database. 021 is forward-only in effect: its rollback refuses while any version row exists, does not give `tmpos_app` its EXECUTE on 020's edit back (so the pre-021 runtime refuses to start until an operator re-grants it, which would reopen DA-125), and must run before 020's. A rollback after the owner deliberately deletes the version rows restarts every counter at 0 (DA-128). 021 also invalidates 020's apply-time postcondition (DA-130), and DA-122 is amended for the new order. D4 now names the chain through 021. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2 ([ADR-38](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds migration 022, applied, rolled back and re-applied on disposable PostgreSQL only (`tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` M7CD-01, M7CD-08). The startup preflight requires exactly 001–022. 022 holds no data; its rollback removes only its schema and returns exactly the 001–021 database, independent of 021's fail-closed rollback. The order is 022, then 021, then 020 (DA-133). D4 now names the chain through 022. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 ([ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds migration 023, applied, rolled back and re-applied on disposable PostgreSQL only (`tests/db/customerContact.integration.test.mjs` M7CN-01, M7CN-08). The startup preflight requires exactly 001–023. 023's rollback refuses, dropping nothing, while any customer holds a phone, an email or a creation time (DA-155); otherwise it returns exactly the 001–022 database, 020's create grant included. The order is 023, then 022, 021, 020. D4 now names the chain through 023. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 ([ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds migration 024, applied over populated rows, rolled back and re-applied on disposable PostgreSQL only (`tests/db/customerEdit.integration.test.mjs` M7CE-01, M7CE-02, M7CE-11). The startup preflight requires exactly 001–024. 024's rollback refuses, dropping nothing, while any customer has been edited (`row_version` above 1), any audit record names a customer, any column grant exists on the customer table or reaches `tmpos_app`, outside READ COMMITTED, or when 024 or 023's profile read is missing; otherwise it returns exactly the 001–023 database, 023's profile grant included. The order is 024, then 023, 022, 021, 020. On a shared test database the steps that commit edits must run after every step that rolls 024 back (DA-159). Applying 024 holds ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on the customer and audit tables while it runs (DA-161). D4 now names the chain through 024; no managed migration was run. **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 ([ADR-43](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds migration 025, applied over populated 001–024 rows (an edited customer included), rolled back and re-applied on disposable PostgreSQL only (`tests/db/customerSearch.integration.test.mjs` M7CS-01 to M7CS-03). It refuses to apply without the ICU root collation `und-x-icu` (DA-169). The startup preflight requires exactly 001–025, and the P7 build refuses a 025 database. 025 holds no data, so its rollback has no data guard: it refuses only when 025 is not applied or its postcondition fails (a 001–024 customer routine withdrawn), and otherwise returns exactly the 001–024 database. The order is 025, then 024, 023, 022, 021, 020. Its CI step rolls back 025 alone, never 024 or earlier, so it runs after the customer edit's HTTP step (DA-159). D4 now names the chain through 025; no managed migration was run. M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1 adds 026, M7B-INVOICES-P1 adds 027 and 028, and M7B-INVOICES-P2 adds 029 (`029_m7b_invoice_draft_edit`), all applied to disposable databases only; 029's rollback refuses while any draft's version is above 1. D4 now names the chain through 029; no managed migration was run. M7B-INVOICES-P2-R1 adds 030 (`030_m7b_invoice_edit_authority`), applied to disposable databases only: one schema, one trigger function, one row trigger on the level table, one routine and three privilege changes, with no table, column, index, row, role, membership or policy and no data rewritten. It takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on seven tables before any check (a maintenance window), refuses unless 029 is applied, and its down refuses, changing nothing, only on the catalog or the applier's rights: 030 not applied (its schema, either function or its trigger missing) or a table it locks missing; 029's routine missing; a statement PostgreSQL refuses (another object in 030's schema or depending on one of its objects, `tmpos_app` gone, the applier's rights); or 029's contract found inexact by its postcondition (an extra, missing or altered trigger on the level table, 021's function serving another trigger, 029's ACL not its two entries). It never refuses on data. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–030, and D4 now names the chain through 030; no managed migration was run. M7B-INVOICES-P1-R3 adds 031 (`031_m7b_invoice_create_authority`), applied to disposable databases only: one routine in 030's schema and three privilege changes, with no table, column, index, row, role, membership, policy, trigger or schema and no data rewritten. It takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on 030's seven tables before any check (a maintenance window) and refuses unless 030 is applied exactly; its down never refuses on data, restores the 030-era surface (028's create EXECUTE for `tmpos_app` again) and refuses, changing nothing, only on the catalog or the applier's rights; while 031 is applied, 030's down is refused atomically (2BP01). The startup preflight requires exactly 001–031, and D4 now names the chain through 031; no managed migration was run. M7B-INVOICES-P1-R4 adds 032 (`032_m7b_invoice_create_customers_authority`), applied to disposable databases only: one trigger function, one trigger and one routine in 030's schema and three privilege changes, with no table, column, index, row, role, membership, policy, type or schema and no data rewritten. It takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on 030's seven tables before any check (a maintenance window) and refuses unless 031 and 030 are applied exactly; its down never refuses on data, restores the 031-era surface (`create_v2`'s EXECUTE for `tmpos_app` again) and refuses, changing nothing, only on the catalog or the applier's rights; while 032 is applied, 031's down is refused atomically by its existing postcondition (55000) and 030's (2BP01). The startup preflight requires exactly 001–032, and D4 now names the chain through 032; no managed migration was run. M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1 adds 033 (`033_m7b_invoice_draft_discard`), applied to disposable databases only: one schema (`tmpos_invoice_discard`), six functions, two triggers, one partial index, the draft status CHECK replaced under its own name and six privilege changes, with no table, column, row, role, membership, policy or type and no data rewritten. It takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on 030's seven tables before any check (a maintenance window) and refuses unless 001–032 are applied with their expected catalog; its down refuses, changing nothing, while any draft is discarded (a rollback never deletes a draft or returns it to `draft`), and otherwise restores the 032-era contract; while 033 is applied, the downs of 032, 030, 029 and 028 are refused by its rollback-order anchor's recorded dependencies (2BP01) and 031's by its existing postcondition (55000). The startup preflight requires exactly 001–033, and D4 now names the chain through 033; no managed migration was run. M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-P1 adds 034 (`034_m7b_invoice_issue`), applied to disposable databases only: one schema (`tmpos_invoice_issue`) with four tables, its routines, guards and anchor, the draft status CHECK replaced under its own name, the header terminal trigger rebound and two nullable audit subject columns. It takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on the seven tables 033 locks (its down also on its own four) before any check (a maintenance window) and refuses unless its prerequisites and 033's exact objects are present, and refuses a re-apply; its down refuses, changing nothing, while any issuing settings, number counter, issued invoice, issued draft or audit record naming an issued invoice or a store exists (a rollback never deletes them), and otherwise restores 033's exact catalog; while 034 is applied, 033's down refuses (55000). The startup preflight requires exactly 001–034, and D4 now names the chain through 034; no managed migration was run. The gate stays OPEN | HIGH |
| **G-AUDIT** | No durable audit; owner "audit log" forgeable | Security | **M3/M4 (minimal writer) → M6 (full)** | `audit_event` writer wired into every authz/mutation path; **minimal writer live before M5 provisioning/grant changes** | Durable append-only audit on all decisions/mutations; provisioning/IAM changes audited from M5 | OPEN — M6-OUTBOX-P3 adds a transaction-local audit record that a command commits with its mutation (`server/runtime/commandTransaction.ts`), and M6-PG-P4's PostgreSQL adapter writes it through the existing append-only writer on the same transaction, proven against disposable PostgreSQL only. Since M6-ENTRY-P1 the production entry composes the adapter in code for the two audited administrative mutations (`PATCH /admin/v1/tenants/display-name` and `PATCH /admin/v1/tenants/status`), not deployed; the login/logout audit record is absent, and the gate stays OPEN. M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1 adds no audit record: a reauthentication is a login, and no login is audited yet; a tenant action after it is audited by the unchanged command path *(until M6-ADMIN-AUDIT-P1)*.

**M6-ADMIN-AUDIT-P1 (code only, not deployed; [03 §2b](./03-backend-control-plane-login-session-blueprint.md), [ADR-29](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** audits the administrative boundary's successful login (`platform.admin.session.login`), reauthentication (`platform.admin.session.reauthenticate`) and logout (`platform.admin.session.logout`). Each row is written through the existing writer in the same transaction as its session change. The insert must affect exactly one row. The row's `request_id` is a server-generated operation id, and its metadata holds action-scoped session references, never a session id or key.

Proven in process and against disposable PostgreSQL only (`tests/db/productionEntry.integration.test.mjs` M6AA-01–14 check the persisted rows together with the session results); nothing managed or live. The gate stays OPEN. Still unaudited:
- every other authorization decision and action;
- refused and unverified attempts (bad token or TOTP, stale authentication, rate limit, admission denial, reauthentication refusals);
- the tenant boundary;
- the session revocations no user asked for: an admission denial at login or at revalidation, a changed witness, idle or absolute expiry (DA-65);
- a logout whose audited end failed and fell back to the plain revocation (DA-64).

The database does not couple a session change to its row, because the runtime login may still call the session routines directly (DA-66).

**M5-TENANT-SESSION-P1 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-31](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** extends the same three records to the tenant boundary: `tenant.session.login`, `tenant.session.reauthenticate` and `tenant.session.logout`, each in the same transaction as its session change, at scope `none` with no tenant or store named, the actor being the internal user the tenant admission resolved (none at logout), and the same metadata keys. The "tenant boundary" item above is delivered in code; its refused attempts, unrequested revocations and expiries stay unaudited as on the administrative boundary (DA-65), and its logout has the same fallback (DA-64). No entry composes the tenant boundary. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P1 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-32](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds the first tenant business read, `GET /api/v1/customers`, and by owner decision it writes no audit event: a list of customer names is read unaudited. Its request log line names the route's path (never its query), the status and a bounded reason, and never carries customer data. Whether reads of personal-data lists are audited is an open privacy and audit decision (DA-88). Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 (commit 2) the compiled tenant entry serves that read in code, still unaudited, not deployed. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds the first audited tenant business command. `POST /api/v1/customers` appends one `audit_event` row in the same transaction as the customer and the idempotency completion. The row carries:
- action `tenant.customer.create` and permission `create_customers`;
- scope `tenant`, with the context's tenant and no store;
- the context's app-owned actor;
- `request_id`, the command's correlation id;
- metadata `{}`.

The store now requires exactly one row INSERTed, or it rolls the whole transaction back, for every command. A refusal writes no audit row. The row names neither the customer nor its name (DA-111). Its tenant and actor are proven in TypeScript and by the mutator's comparison with 019's derived identity, not by a database rule (DA-114). The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** audits the first tenant IAM change. `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` appends one `audit_event` row in the same transaction as the stored level and the idempotency completion:
- action `tenant.role.level.update` and permission `edit_role_levels`;
- scope `tenant`, with the context's tenant and no store, and `subject_tenant_id` null;
- the context's app-owned actor;
- reason code `role_<role>_customers_to_<level>` and the owner's trimmed reason (10–500 scalars);
- metadata `{}`.

A refusal writes no audit row, and no outbox event is written. The create now reads the owner's level (migration 020's routine), and its audit record is unchanged. **Role edits and customer creates are audited only through their HTTP routes.** A holder of `tmpos_app` plus a live minted key calls these routines directly with no audit record: 020's customer create `tmpos_role.m7a_customer_create` (DA-118, 019's DA-115 class), and the role edit, a new unaudited IAM-change path (DA-119). The edit was 020's `m7a_role_level_set` at R1; since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 it is 021's `tmpos_role_edit.m7a_role_level_set_v2`, and 020's setter is revoked from every runtime role. Both are G-AUDIT exceptions and pre-deployment blockers. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 1 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** leaves the role edit's audit record unchanged and adds a refusal that writes none: an edit whose asserted tenant or version no longer holds rolls the whole command transaction back, so it writes no level, version, audit record, event or completion (`tests/db/roleLevelPrecondition.integration.test.mjs` P4W-01 to P4W-04 count the edit audits and completions in both tenants). The direct edit available to a holder of `tmpos_app` plus a live store-owner key is now 021's set routine, which must name the session's tenant and the role's current version and still writes no audit record: DA-118 and DA-119 stay OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-38](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds the customer detail read, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`, and like the list it writes no audit event: reading one customer's name is unaudited (DA-131). Its log line names the route class `other` and a bounded reason, never the id or the name. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-39](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds no audit record and changes none: the shop's Customer directory page is a client of the existing routes. A create it sends writes the create's one `tenant.customer.create` record in the create's own transaction, as before; that record names neither the customer nor the name (DA-111). Its list and detail reads write none (DA-88, DA-131). The page writes nothing to the browser console (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-43). DA-118 and DA-119 stay OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** leaves the create's one `tenant.customer.create` record as it was: its metadata stays `{}`, so no phone or email reaches it, and the record names neither the customer nor the name (DA-111). The new profile read, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile`, writes no audit event, like the list and the detail read: reading a customer's phone and email is unaudited (DA-131's class; DA-153). The runtime role's direct create path is now migration 023's `tmpos_customer_contact.m7a_contact_create`, which delegates to 020's create and also writes the contacts, still with no audit record (DA-118, amended, not closed). DA-118 and DA-119 stay OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 (code only, not deployed; [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))** adds one audit record per committed edit that changes a customer, in the edit's own transaction: action `tenant.customer.update`, permission `edit_customers`, the trusted actor and tenant, and the edited customer in the new typed column `public.audit_event.subject_customer_id` (migration 024, 009's precedent; set only by the command transaction, from the aggregate the mutator locked). Its metadata stays `{}`: the changed field names are not recorded (DA-158), and no name, phone or email reaches the record. A no-op, a conflict and a refusal write none, and an audit or completion fault rolls the edit back. The create's record is unchanged and still names no customer (DA-111, amended). A runtime-role holder with a live Edit-capable key can call 024's routine directly and edit customers with no audit record (DA-118, amended, not closed). DA-118 and DA-119 stay OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. The gate stays OPEN. | BLOCKER |
| **G-IDEMPOT** | Idempotency/rate-limit in-memory, process-local; money double-apply on retry | Platform | M6 | Durable idempotency store + distributed rate limits | Retried writes don't double-apply; limits survive restart/multi-instance | **OPEN — M6-RATE-P1 (committed in `dd89ff9`, accepted as a code-only foundation) delivers the rate-limit contract, M6-IDEMPOT-P2 (committed in `a6469f23`, accepted as a code-only foundation) the idempotency contract, and M6-OUTBOX-P3 the command-transaction and outbox contracts, and M6-PG-P4 their PostgreSQL adapter and migration 006, and M6-RATE-P2 the rate-limit contract's PostgreSQL store and migration 014 (both proven against disposable PostgreSQL only), all below; nothing is live. The client address groups IPv4 per address and IPv6 per /64; only IPv4-mapped IPv6 counts as IPv4, and NAT64 and 6to4 addresses are IPv6 clients (corrected in M6-IDEMPOT-P2):**
- **The port.** `DistributedRateLimiter` (`server/runtime/rateLimit.ts`), provider-independent:
  - A bucket is (namespace `runtime`/`tenant`/`admin`/`tenant-login`/`admin-login`, dimension `client`/`account` with `tenant`/`store` reserved, pseudonymous key, limit, window).
  - Its fixed window never extends. A consume is atomic, and one that is refused changes nothing.
  - Answers are explicit: allowed (with the remaining allowance), limited (with a bounded `retryAfterMs`) or unavailable, plus a readiness `probe`.
  - The runtime obeys only a plain record whose outcome and fields are in contract; other fields are ignored. Anything else, a throw, or an overrun of min(port deadline, 500 ms) is a bounded 503 (`rate_limit_unavailable`, `rate_limit_timeout`, `rate_limit_outcome_invalid`).
- **Keys.** Each key is HMAC-SHA256 under `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` over the namespace, dimension and subject: an IPv4 address, an IPv6 /64, or a verified principal's digest. No raw address, UID, token or cookie crosses the port.
- **Limits.** Each request is limited at 300 per 60 s per client per namespace. The login client limit is 10 per 60 s, spent before verification. The login account limit is 5 per 5 min, spent after verification and the evidence policy and before admission.
- **Conformance.** An executable suite (`assertRateLimiterContract`, `server/runtime/rateLimiter.testkit.ts`) covers atomicity across two instances, mixed costs, the fixed window, aborts, and outage with recovery. Positive controls show it fails a non-atomic, per-instance-locked, window-extending, spend-on-refusal, policy-blind, abort-ignoring, fail-open or state-losing adapter.
- **No per-process path.** No per-process limiter remains in the production graph, and no fallback exists. The in-memory limiter is test support outside the artifact.
- **Probes and readiness.** The probes never touch the limiter. Readiness reports its store, with one shared probe per second per instance.

**M6-IDEMPOT-P2 delivers the durable idempotency contract, in code only:**
- **Route policy.** Every route declares `idempotency` (`none` or `required`), and startup refuses a missing or unknown value. `required` registers only on a state-changing route with a verified principal and a declared platform-scope authorization; a tenant- or store-scoped route may not require it until M5 supplies server-derived tenant and store context to bind *(since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3, [ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): a tenant-scope requirement is admitted on a tenant-session `command` route; store scope stays refused)*. Its operation is a `perform` handed only its IdempotentContext. Every current route is `none`, and `required` exists only on synthetic test routes.
- **The step.** It runs after the client limit, CSRF and origin, authentication, authorization and the bounded body read, and just before the operation. A missing, repeated or malformed `Idempotency-Key` (one UUID version 4) is a 400. Another request under the key is a 422 `idempotency_key_reused`, identical whatever differs. One still in progress is a 409 `request_in_progress`. A store timeout, outage or malformed answer, an operation overrun, or a completion the store cannot confirm is a 503; a malformed outcome is a 500.
- **Identity.** A dedicated `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY` keys domain-separated HMACs; the transaction composition root (and the runtime at startup) refuses one equal to `RATE_LIMIT_KEY`, however padded. The record is bound to the client key and the principal, so each principal has its own key namespace; the request is bound to its method, route, session boundary, reserved tenant and store slots, and body *(since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3, contract version 2: the authorizer's trusted tenant and store, and the app-owned actor)*.
- **Port and replay.** `DurableIdempotencyStore` (`server/runtime/idempotency.ts`) offers acquire, a lease-checked complete and a readiness probe, and every answer is strictly validated. A recorded response is sealed (AES-256-GCM, bound to its scope and fingerprint): 200/201 or the approved 409/422 rejections, JSON only, `Location` only, at most 16 KiB. Set-Cookie, Authorization, WWW-Authenticate, CSRF and tracing material are never recorded, and each replay carries its own request ID. Crashes, timeouts and malformed outcomes are never recorded.
- **Conformance.** `assertIdempotencyStoreContract` runs over two instances sharing synthetic state, with 22 positive controls. Static containment keeps the in-process store out of the production graph.

**M6-OUTBOX-P3 delivers the atomic command transaction and the transactional outbox, in code only ([ADR-17](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):**
- **Command routes.** A `required` route may declare a `command`: its contract (kind, create or update, one aggregate type, a change schema, the events it may enqueue), validated at startup against a closed event registry, and a synchronous planner handed only the session boundary, the parsed body and a runtime-generated aggregate ID. The runtime enforces only that the planner returns a plan and no thenable; that it returns nothing it captured or imported, and makes no effect, are source-review rules, so atomicity covers what its plan hands the store, never an effect beside it. `perform` keeps the crash window and is never approved for a production route.
- **The transaction port.** `CommandTransactionPort` (`server/runtime/commandTransaction.ts`) commits, in one transaction, the lease check (the fencing token, checked first), the mutation at its expected version, the sealed completion, the audit record and the events — or nothing. Since **M5-GIAM-D1-P2-R1** there is one further committed shape: a completion ALONE, carrying a deterministic rejection and no business state — answering `committed`, `lease_lost`, `conflict` or `unavailable`, strictly validated. The response is sent only after `committed`. Since **M5-GIAM-D1-P2-R1** the two deterministic post-authorization rejections are *sealed*: a `conflict` (the aggregate is not at the version named) and, for an update, an `invalid_state` (its state does not admit the change) each record their own bounded 409 as the completion, in the very transaction that fenced the lease, revalidated the authority and locked the row — writing no mutation, no audit record and no event. The intent is therefore decided once: an identical retry replays that 409 instead of being replanned (with the one pre-existing ambiguity every commit has — a COMMIT whose acknowledgement is lost answers the first caller 503, and the retry then replays the sealed 409), the same key with any other request is the 422 key reuse, and a key that was refused can never later become a mutation while its record is retained. If the completion does not hold, the whole transaction rolls back to `unavailable` and the lease is left to expire exactly as before. The remaining outcomes are not all 503s: an update's missing aggregate is a bounded 404 and an update whose changes already hold is its recorded 200; only a lost lease, an unavailable or timed-out store and an answer that does not conform map to a bounded 503, which assumes no rollback and releases no lease, so a retry replays a committed record or waits on the lease, and a stale attempt's late commit is fenced out.
- **The outbox.** An immutable envelope — event ID, type and version from a closed registry, aggregate type, ID and version, tenant/store/actor slots (null until M5), a server-generated correlation ID, a bounded flat payload (at most 4 KiB, explicit schema) and the store's transaction timestamp — with delivery metadata kept outside it. `OutboxDeliveryStore` (`server/runtime/outbox.ts`) claims atomically under fencing tokens with store-clock expiry, in batches of at most 32; only the holder acknowledges, retries or dead-letters; retries back off from 1 s to 15 min over at most 20 attempts. `deliverOutboxBatch` is one bounded pass; no worker runs. Delivery is at least once until dead-lettered: consumers deduplicate by event ID, and neither ordering nor exactly-once delivery is claimed.
- **Conformance.** `assertCommandTransactionContract` and `assertOutboxDeliveryContract` (`server/runtime/transactionalOutbox.testkit.ts`) run over two instances sharing synthetic state, with 17 positive controls each. Static containment keeps the in-process adapters, any delivery loop, SQL and database or network clients out of the production graph, and the approved-adapter table stays closed.

**M6-PG-P4 delivers the PostgreSQL adapter and its schema, proven against disposable PostgreSQL only ([ADR-17](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):**
- **Migration 006** (`server/platform-identity/migrations/006_m6_transactional_store.*`): `idempotency_record`, `outbox_event` and `m6_store_clock()` in the internal schema `tmpos_internal`, with database-enforced digest, lease, token, state, attempt, time and size bounds, the outbox transition guard, RLS enabled with no policy, and a reverse that refuses to destroy work. Since M6-PG-P6 `tmpos_app` holds no table privilege at all: it reaches the store only through nine fixed `SECURITY DEFINER` lifecycle routines (DA-18). Applied to disposable databases only; no role is created or modified.
- **The adapter** (`server/persistence/postgresTransactionalStore.ts`) serves the three ports over one database through one bounded transaction kernel, reads the store clock after locking the row it decides about (a claim, which never waits on a lock, reads it once for its whole statement, and migration 006's guard re-checks each claimed row against its own later reading, so a backward step of the database's clock past a chosen row's eligibility instant refuses that claim, which fails closed — the call answers `unavailable` and hands out nothing), never reads an error as a conflict, and rejects as indeterminate only when a COMMIT's fate is unknown. The audit record is written by the existing writer on the same transaction.
- **Evidence** (`tests/db/transactionalStore.integration.test.mjs`, in CI against the job's disposable service): the three unchanged conformance suites over two independent instances, and targeted cases for migration up/down/up, constraints, database time, rollback at every part, fencing, conflicts, reused event IDs, concurrency, a lost acknowledgement, aborts, timeouts, SQLSTATEs, connection loss, claim order and output containment. Since M6-PG-P7-R1 the adapter is composed only by the transaction root (`server/composition/productionTransactions.ts`) and only for a route that requires idempotency — the production inventory's two platform commands, the two audited administrative mutations. Since M6-ENTRY-P1 the production entry composes it for them in code, not deployed.

**M6-RATE-P2 delivers the distributed rate-limit store and composes it in code, proven against disposable PostgreSQL only ([ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):**
- **Migration 014** (`server/platform-identity/migrations/014_m6_distributed_rate_limit.*`): schema `tmpos_rate_limit` with the bucket table (one row per namespace, dimension, pseudonymous key, limit and window, with database-enforced bounds and an expiry index), a one-row key-fingerprint table, and four routines — the consume, the probe and the reclaim, `SECURITY DEFINER`, and the key check the consume and the probe share, granted to nobody. `tmpos_app` holds USAGE and EXECUTE on the consume, the probe and the reclaim, and no table privilege; section 6 also refuses when `tmpos_audit_writer`, `anon` or `authenticated` can execute any of them, by a grant or by membership in `tmpos_app`. RLS is enabled with no policy; no role is created. Its rollback takes its locks before its check and refuses while any bucket is inside its window.
- **The store's contract.** A fixed window from the first successful consume, never extended; a consume atomic across instances through its bucket's row lock, except one the committed state already refuses, which is answered from an unlocked read; a refused consume that changes nothing; a retry-after on the database's clock; policy and namespace isolation through the bucket key. Each call is one transaction on the runtime kernel under a 200 ms lock, 300 ms statement and 400 ms idle-in-transaction bound, below the runtime's 500 ms limiter cap, and the adapter answers at its own 500 ms bound even with COMMIT in flight (an allowance committed after it is answered unavailable: its cost spent, never an allowance unrecorded). Consumes of one bucket are serialized per instance in the adapter, so one client holds at most one kernel connection per bucket it reaches.
- **Fail closed.** An outage, timeout, abort, malformed answer or mismatched key is `unavailable`: a bounded 503 (`rate_limit_unavailable`, `rate_limit_timeout`) and a failing readiness probe, which a read-only session or a standby also fails (`store_read_only`). `/health` is untouched. There is no retry, queue, cache or local fallback.
- **Key skew.** The store registers the fingerprint of the first `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` it sees, atomically, and refuses every other. Nothing erases or replaces it; recovering from a wrong first registration is a manual operator procedure, and rotation needs a coordinated procedure that does not exist (DA-53).
- **Cleanup.** A separate reclaim routine, called by the adapter in its own short transaction: one call owed per consume that opened a window, run one at a time per instance; its failure changes no consume.
- **Composition.** The transaction root builds the adapter (`server/persistence/postgresRateLimiter.ts`) over its one kernel, and the sessions root binds it with the key it parsed itself. Readiness includes the limiter's probe.
- **Evidence**, against disposable PostgreSQL only: the unchanged conformance suite (`assertRateLimiterContract`) over two adapter instances on separate kernels sharing one database, and the stage's targeted database and real-socket suites (recorded in the stage report). Operating limits and measured local limits: "The distributed rate-limit store" below.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 composes the store for the first tenant command, in code only ([ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):**
- **Route policy.** `required` is admitted at tenant scope on a `command` route of the tenant session audience only. A tenant `perform`, a Bearer tenant route and every store-scope route stay refused. `POST /api/v1/customers` is the one such route.
- **Binding.** The chain binds tenant, store and actor from the authorizer's trusted scope, never the request, and refuses (403) a missing context. The fingerprint gains the actor, and `IDEMPOTENCY_CONTRACT_VERSION` is 2, so every record, the administrative ones included, starts afresh. Nothing is deployed, so no drain was needed. The same principal, key and body under another tenant or actor is a 422, never a replay.
- **Composition.** The tenant transaction root composes the PostgreSQL store over the shared kernel with `contextScope: 'tenant'`. That store admits tenant contexts alone, each re-proved in its own transaction by the tenant revalidator. The root requires the tenant process's own `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY`: `idempotency_key_missing`, `idempotency_key_invalid`, and `idempotency_key_shared` (equal to `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` or `TENANT_SESSION_ISSUER_KEY`, however padded). The tenant entry judges the key the same way at configuration, and its readiness adds the store's two probes.
- **Outcomes.** The customer, its audit record and the completion commit together, or nothing does. An in-transaction refusal, a duplicate id and a lost commit acknowledgement are a 503 that releases no lease: a retry replays the sealed 201 or, once the lease expires, plans afresh. A lost COMMIT acknowledgement is answered by the app's command-port deadline first (`transaction_timeout`), and a retry under the same key resolves through the retained idempotency record. Every tenant instance must hold one `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY` (DA-112).
- **Same key while the winner commits.** A same-key request, typically on another instance, that arrives while the winner's command transaction holds the record answers a retryable 503 (`idempotency_unavailable`), not 409: the winner's transaction holds the record row as its fence, and the request's acquire waits on it until its lock bound. 409 `request_in_progress` answers only while no command transaction holds the record, for example a retry within a failed command's lease. A client retrying on either answer gets the winner's replayed 201 once it commits (`tests/db/customerCreate.integration.test.mjs` M7CC-06, M7CC-08).

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 adds a second tenant command, in code only ([ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels`, a tenant-session `command` requiring idempotency at tenant scope under the same policy, binding and composition as the create. Its planner refuses a body its contract refuses with 400 after the record is acquired (DA-117). Its outcomes are the create's: the level, its audit record and the completion commit together or not at all, and an in-transaction refusal or a lost commit acknowledgement is a 503 that releases no lease. A replay answers the recorded 200, and the same key with another body is 422. A create refused inside its transaction because an owner's edit committed first keeps its lease: the same key is 403 at the authorizer while the role stays narrowed (no key touched), 409 `request_in_progress` within the lease once the level is restored, and after the lease the key is decided afresh on the current level. A create refused 403 by the authorizer reserves no record, so the same key sent after a restore is a new command (`tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-02, M7RLH-04).

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2 adds a client of the role-level command, in code only ([ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the shop app's role screen sends R1's `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` with one `Idempotency-Key` per payload (role, level and reason); another payload gets a new key. With no response at all it retries once under the same key. A 409 `request_in_progress`, or a second silence, keeps the key, so the next Save replays it; any other answer settles it. The new `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` is a read with `idempotency: 'none'`: its tenant is the authorizer-selected trusted scope, passed to 020's definer read as a parameter, not bound to the session in the database (DA-121). R1's PUT has no tenant or version precondition (DA-125).

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 1 adds the first unsealed precondition refusal, in code only ([ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the PUT's body now carries `expectedTenant` and `expectedVersion`, and the fingerprint covers them, so the same key with another tenant or version is 422. When either precondition no longer holds, the store rolls the command transaction back, so it records no mutation, audit record, event or completion; the idempotency lease, committed separately beforehand, remains until it expires. The runtime answers 409 `write_conflict` unsealed, unlike the platform commands' version conflict, whose 409 is recorded as the completion (M5-GIAM-D1-P2-R1). The key's lease, acquired in its own transaction, is left to expire under the existing policy: the same key and body answer 409 `request_in_progress` until then and are decided afresh after it (`tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-10). A key that committed replays its sealed 200, whose `version` is the one it committed, even after a later edit. The shop client uses a new key for each new attempt. It holds any attempt whose outcome is unresolved as its key plus the exact change it sent, and a confirmation re-sends exactly that pair. Only a 200 or a `write_conflict` settles the hold. A changed level or reason is an explicit local abandonment of it, not a settlement, and that attempt's server outcome may stay unknown (its key may already have committed). The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2 adds a replayed header, in code only ([ADR-38](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the customer create's 201 now carries `Location: /api/v1/customers/<id>` inside the sealed envelope, so a same-key replay answers the same `Location`. A completion sealed before that build replays without it (DA-134). The new detail read is `idempotency: 'none'`, and a templated route can never require idempotency, since the route table refuses it at registration. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 adds the first client of the customer create, in code only ([ADR-39](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the shop's Customer directory page sends `POST /api/v1/customers` with one `Idempotency-Key` per submitted draft, and holds the key and the exact body string in memory before the request leaves. It never retries on its own. A 409, a 5xx, a 2xx that is not the validated 201, any other status the page does not treat as a refusal, or no answer leaves the attempt unconfirmed. "Check again" re-sends the same key and the same bytes: only by the user's action, only while the page's session CSRF token is the one the attempt was sent with, and only within 23 hours of the first send, a client cutoff under the 24 h retention. Only a validated 201, fresh or replayed, confirms it. A first send answered 400 or 422 ends the attempt, and the next submission takes a new key (DA-117). A reload loses the attempt, and after a session change or the cutoff it can no longer be checked from the shop, though an answer already on its way can still confirm it; adding the name again is a new key that can create a second customer (DA-137, DA-139). The server's contract, lease and retention are unchanged. The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 changes the create's body, not its idempotency contract, in code only ([ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** `POST /api/v1/customers` takes an optional `phone` and `email` beside `name`, at most 8,192 bytes. The fingerprint is still the keyed hash of the exact body bytes, so a name-only body keeps its fingerprint, and any change to the phone or email bytes under one key is the existing `422 idempotency_key_reused`. `IDEMPOTENCY_CONTRACT_VERSION` is unchanged and completions sealed before this build replay as sealed. The shop leaves a blank contact out of the body, so a name-only create keeps its exact bytes; an unconfirmed attempt holds its exact body, contacts included, and is still re-sent only by the user (DA-137 unchanged; DA-154). The gate stays OPEN.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 adds the first templated idempotent command, in code only ([ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}` requires an `Idempotency-Key`. Its fingerprint is contract version 2's array with the canonical path target appended after the body; a literal route builds exactly the array it built before, so `IDEMPOTENCY_CONTRACT_VERSION` stays 2, the create's pinned vector and every sealed completion are unchanged, and no drain is needed. Under one key, a changed body, or the same body sent to another customer, is `422 idempotency_key_reused`. A completed edit's retry replays its sealed answer before anything is evaluated again, even after later edits moved the version, and still needs the current session and authorization. A stale version is a sealed `409 {"error":"write_conflict"}` that commits only its completion, so its retry replays it; a missing or foreign customer is an unsealed 404 whose lease waits out its time. A lost COMMIT answer is a 503 whose same-key retry replays what committed (M7CH-05); the branch where nothing committed and the lease is later reclaimed is not exercised end to end for this route (DA-163). The shop holds an edit whose outcome is unknown, with its exact bytes and key, in memory only, and re-sends it only on the user's "Check again" (DA-154, amended). The gate stays OPEN.

**Still OPEN:**
- No deployed entry composes the limiter. The PostgreSQL store is composed in code (M6-RATE-P2), and since M6-ENTRY-P1 the deployable entry composes the sessions and transaction roots, and the limiter with them, in code only; nothing is deployed, so nothing outside the test suites counts requests in a shared store. Durable idempotency has been the transaction root's since M6-PG-P7-R1 (`IDEMPOTENCY_KEY` moved out of the sessions root): for a route that requires it, that root requires a supported `APP_DATABASE_URL` and a valid `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY`, refuses with bounded codes before any client exists (`app_database_url_missing`, `app_database_url_invalid`, `app_database_endpoint_unsupported`, `database_tls_invalid`, `idempotency_key_missing`, `idempotency_key_invalid`, `idempotency_key_shared`, `command_mutator_unavailable`), and otherwise composes the PostgreSQL store's idempotency store and command-transaction port together, never one alone, with no fallback. The production inventory's two platform commands require idempotency (since M5-GIAM-D1-P1 and -P2), so wherever that root is composed both variables are required. Since M6-ENTRY-P1 the production entry composes it, and requires both, in code for the two audited administrative mutations, not deployed; the login/logout audit record is absent.
- **The crash window.** It is closed by contract for `command` routes only (M6-OUTBOX-P3), and the PostgreSQL adapter that implements that contract (M6-PG-P4) is composed only for a route that requires idempotency — the production inventory's two platform commands — by a root the production entry composes in code since M6-ENTRY-P1, not deployed, and, since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3, the tenant inventory's customer create, composed by the tenant entry in code, not deployed. `perform` keeps it: the reservation, the business write and the completion are separate steps, so an instance that dies, or an operation that commits and then fails, leaves an applied operation that a retry reclaims and runs again once its 60 s lease expires. That path guarantees one holder of an unexpired lease and the replay of a completed operation, never exactly-once execution.
- **Remaining G-IDEMPOT and M6 blockers.** Binding the M6-PG-P4 adapter — a runtime LOGIN holding `tmpos_app` and `tmpos_audit_writer` (G-DBROLE) — behind a real business route with its source-defined mutators and each domain's aggregate tables, composed only through the transaction kernel (`createSupervisedPgClient`), which contains the driver's lost-connection defects (DA-15) — DA-18 (the runtime role's authority over the store's lifecycle), DA-19 (the close window) and DA-20 (savepoints) are closed in M6-PG-P6, and DA-21 (session state carried to the next transaction) in M6-PG-P7, its direct-or-session-mode precondition enforced by the transaction root in M6-PG-P7-R1; the runtime login's `APP_DATABASE_URL` over a direct or session-mode endpoint (G-DBROLE); the managed apply of migrations 005 and 006 — while 006 is pending the managed 005 apply refuses at its exact-[005] plan gate, and target-version planning versus a combined 005+006 authorization is a separate, unmade decision (G-MIGRATE); the limiter's cost on the primary database under real traffic (DA-52) and a `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` rotation procedure (DA-53); a deployable worker and publishers, dead-letter observability, redrive, and retention and purge of terminal rows (M8); an owner-approved deployable entry composing the sessions and transaction roots — its code half delivered by M6-ENTRY-P1 (`server/entry/productionEntry.ts`, code only, not deployed), its deployment and the owner's approval of it still open; money-path idempotency end to end (M7b); audit coverage and actor attribution (G-AUDIT, M5). The four dev-only High advisories remain pre-launch maintenance debt (G-APPSEC).
- A limiter adapter must pass its conformance suite, honour the abort signal, keep no offline queue, time out below 500 ms and reclaim expired buckets: the PostgreSQL limiter (M6-RATE-P2) does, and any later replacement (ADR-26) must. A future idempotency adapter must pass `assertIdempotencyStoreContract`, time out below 1 s and use the store's own clock.
- The deployment must hold one `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` and one `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY` on every instance. Since M6-RATE-P2 the store detects `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` skew: an instance whose key's fingerprint is not the registered one answers every consume unavailable and fails readiness, and rotating that key needs a coordinated procedure that does not exist. The first caller registers, so a wrongly keyed first caller locks out every correctly keyed instance until an operator re-registers (DA-53). No such check exists for `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY`, and since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 none exists among tenant instances either (DA-112). Rotating `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY` or changing the contract version starts every record afresh, so either needs a drain of one retention period (24 h); a retry after the retention is a new operation.
- The deployment half of the proxy contract must present IPv4 clients as IPv4 (or IPv4-mapped) addresses. Under the /64 rule, an ingress that translates them into the NAT64 prefix would put every such client in one bucket (`64:ff9b::/96` lies inside one /64). | **BLOCKER** |
| **G-SSRF** | ~~Open SSRF (`label-proxy` fetches arbitrary URL)~~ — **M3 half DONE** | Security | **M3 (eliminate DEV sidecar) ✅ → M7e/M8 (egress boundary)** | Egress boundary; host allowlist; no request-controlled URL | M3 evidence: `label-proxy` + entire Shipping sidecar DELETED; `server/**` contains zero outbound `fetch(`; containment regression suite green. **Still OPEN** for the M7e/M8 provider egress boundary. | **PARTIAL** | BLOCKER |
| **G-UNAUTH** | Unauthenticated endpoints (~~Shipping API~~ and ~~identity routes~~ eliminated M3); protection re-implemented/omitted per route (folds GAP-25) | Security | **M3** (+M4/M5/M8) | Every endpoint authenticated + authorized; **shared enforced middleware (auth/CSRF/rate-limit/security-headers), not per-route**; unauth write removed | M3 evidence: all 22 unauthenticated Shipping routes eliminated; the unauthenticated identity routes (`POST /identity/resolve`, `GET /identity/by-uid`) DELETED, each now a bounded JSON 404 (`server/platform-identity/identityUnauthenticatedSurfaceElimination.test.ts`); **the M3 shared-middleware slice is delivered** — the M3 runtime admits requests only through ONE enforced chain: a closed central route table in which each route declares `public` or `authenticated` + its authorization requirement (startup fails closed otherwise, and an unauthenticated state-changing route cannot register), then a fixed order of rate limit → exact method/path admission → CSRF/exact-origin (unsafe methods) → authentication → authorization → handler, with a closed security-header policy on every application-layer response — proven over real sockets (`server/runtime/enforcement.test.ts`). **The M3 request-body/media-type slice is delivered:** every route declares `none` or bounded JSON (a source-defined cap of at most 1 MiB, required or optional); a body is judged from headers alone (400/413/415; an Expect on HTTP/1.1 → 417) before CSRF, authentication and authorization, read only after them under a cap on the bytes actually streamed, and parsed as strict UTF-8 JSON (`server/runtime/bodyPolicy.test.ts`). **The M4 login/session foundation is delivered in the runtime (committed in `dd89ff9`; `server/runtime/sessions.test.ts`):** two separate session boundaries — tenant/store `/api/v1/*` and administrative `/admin/v1/*` — each with its own `__Host-` cookie (Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Max-Age equal to the absolute lifetime), trusted origins (never shared between the two), audience, admission, authorizer, store and login limits, exchanged at `POST …/session/login`, `GET …/session` and `POST …/session/logout`; opaque 256-bit identifiers held server-side only as digests, rotated at login, revoked at logout, with absolute and idle expiry; session-bound CSRF (derived from the session, compared in fixed time) on every unsafe session request after the session authenticates and before authorization; a login client limit before verification and an account limit keyed only by the verified principal; and the authenticator and authorizer are handed frozen header-only and route views, never the request. The existing Firebase adapter is adapted to the runtime's authenticator port (`createRuntimeIdentityVerifier`, on its own Admin app over exactly the validated configuration) but was not composed into any deployable entry until M6-ENTRY-P1, and that adaptation fails closed without its configuration. Until M6-ENTRY-P1 the production entry served only the public operational routes. **Since M6-ENTRY-P1 the deployable entry composes, in code only and not deployed, the administrative boundary's authenticator (the Firebase adapter), admission and durable session store; the tenant boundary is composed by no entry.** **M4 session-lifecycle hardening (committed in `dd89ff9`) adds:** every port call bounded (3 s by default) and handed a cancellation signal, an outage a bounded 503 distinct from a 401; admission revalidation with a security-version witness, a denial revoking every session of the principal in that boundary; idle refresh only by a successful authorized request; admin MFA and recent authentication; opaque provider UIDs, digested wherever a storage key is needed; host-distinct origins; and a provider-aware composition root outside the runtime that composes all or nothing and today refuses (`server/composition/productionSessions.test.ts`). **M6-ENTRY-P1 adds the admin Host boundary in the runtime** (`server/runtime/app.ts` `hosts`): every request but the two probes must name the one served authority (from `SESSION_ADMIN_ORIGINS`) in exactly one Host line, or it gets the bounded 404 before the limiter; `X-Forwarded-Host` is never read, so the real edge must pass Host through unchanged and route only the admin hostname to the entry — an edge duty, unproved (DA-9). It keeps this gate `PARTIAL`. **Still OPEN for:** production wiring — the composition root exists outside the runtime and, since M6-ENTRY-P1, the deployable entry composes it in code, but an owner-approved deployment of it is still required, and real-edge proof is one of this gate's open items; a durable, shared session store — delivered in code (migration 011, `postgresSessionStore.ts`) and, since M6-ENTRY-P1, composed by the entry for the admin boundary; its deployment and the runtime LOGIN provisioning (G-DBROLE) stay open; a production admin admission adapter (delivered in code by M5-GIAM-D1-P4: migration 013's definer read as `tmpos_app`, built by the transaction root and bound by the sessions root together with the canonical authorizer; since M6-ENTRY-P1 the deployable entry composes both roots in code, not deployed); tenant/store admission and scope authorization (the tenant plane — migration 005 — and M5); a production admission source to revalidate against (the runtime re-checks admission on every protected request of both boundaries — the tenant since M5-GIAM-D1-P2, the admin since M5-GIAM-D1-P4 — and revokes on a denial or a changed witness; both admission adapters exist in code, over migrations 011 and 013; since M6-ENTRY-P1 the deployable entry composes the administrative one in code, not deployed, and since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P2 the compiled tenant entry composes the tenant one in code, not deployed; a provider-side disablement reaches a live session only through that admission); a durable audit record of logins and logouts (G-AUDIT — the live writer needs migration 005); the deployment half of the trusted-proxy contract — the contract and its real-socket suite are delivered in code (M6-RATE-P1, `server/runtime/clientAddress.ts`: the socket peer is the client unless it lies in an explicit trusted CIDR; only then is one `X-Forwarded-For` line walked right to left to the first untrusted hop; universal and near-universal ranges, overlaps, more than 32 ranges or two /8s of IPv4, zones, ports, hostnames and overlong chains are refused), but the production `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` (the composition root refuses it missing, invalid, or `none` under `NODE_ENV=production`), trusted ranges that hold proxy egress addresses only, proxies that append to or overwrite `X-Forwarded-For` and are the runtime's only way in, a trusted set covering every hop, and an end-to-end check through the real edge are deployment work — and HSTS at TLS termination; a deployed distributed limiter (G-IDEMPOT — since M6-RATE-P2 the PostgreSQL store over migration 014 is composed in code, [ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md), and since M6-ENTRY-P1 the deployable entry composes it in code, not deployed; no per-process limiter exists to fall back to); a re-authentication flow for step-up and provisioning the admin hostname (G-CPLOGIN — admin MFA and recent authentication are enforced at login, and since M5-GIAM-D1-P2 a command route may require recent authentication and refuse without it, and host distinctness at composition; since M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1 the re-authentication flow exists in code — the login route's same-principal reauthentication mode and the console's recent-sign-in dialog — not deployed, and the admin hostname is still unprovisioned); the legacy DEV identity/BCP Express app, which is not behind the shared chain; and the pass rule — every deployed endpoint authenticated + authorized (M5/M8). The forward obligation stays tracked as `TMPOS-UNAUTH-IDENTITY-RESOLVE` in `m2-security-gate-manifest.json`. | **PARTIAL** | BLOCKER |

The checker runs **before** `npm ci` in every installing job, mandatory (no `--if-present`, no `\|\| true`), Node built-ins only. Lockfile changes reproduced by a clean-cache public-registry `npm ci`. DAST is M8. **Prod SCA residual at M2 (verified against the recovered lockfile): 0 Critical / 4 High transitive — `@grpc/grpc-js`, `path-to-regexp`, `picomatch`, `ws`; none was resolved by the lockfile recovery, and all four were remediated later by package overrides in `0af5744`.** The advisory set is byte-for-byte identical to the pre-recovery baseline (22 advisories before and after; 0 added, 0 removed), so the recovery neither introduced nor silently suppressed a finding. `npm audit signatures` now verifies 1002 registry signatures — only possible against the public registry.
