| DA-120 | **Lock holding and starvation on a role row.** (1) A direct caller holding `tmpos_app` plus a live key whose role passes the create's pre-read holds its role row FOR SHARE for as long as it keeps its transaction open (the database ends no transaction at session expiry), so the owner's edit of that role in that tenant waits until its lock or statement timeout and answers 503 (`tests/db/tenantRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M7RL-14). A live store-owner key holding the row FOR UPDATE through the edit delays every create of that role in that tenant the same way. (2) Through the HTTP route too: a share locker does not queue behind a waiting FOR UPDATE, so overlapping creates of one role by two or more principals can keep the owner's edit of that role waiting into a 503. Availability only: every create is decided on the level committed when it locked the row. (3) The first create of a role in a tenant inserts its lock row, so concurrent first creates of that role serialize behind it *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4, [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): 021's edit takes the same role row FOR UPDATE and then the role's version row, which only a holder of the role row ever locks or writes, so the version row adds no wait of its own. A direct caller holding 021's edit open holds both until its lock or statement timeout, the same class)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): (4) the same class on a customer row: a direct caller holding 024's edit open keeps the role row FOR SHARE and the customer row FOR UPDATE until its lock or statement timeout, so an owner's level edit of that role, and every other edit of that customer, waits into a 503. Through the HTTP route a no-op holds the row until its completion is sealed and committed, as a change does.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P2: the same class for the draft edit — a holder that calls 029's routine keeps the draft header `FOR UPDATE`, the customer `FOR KEY SHARE` and, on the change path, the role row `FOR SHARE` for as long as it keeps its transaction open; availability only (029's header).)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P2-R1, [ADR-48](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the note above no longer describes the edit's locks. The edit holds the draft header `FOR UPDATE`, the customer `FOR KEY SHARE` and, for manager, technician and sales_staff, the role's Invoices guard key SHARED from after the header until COMMIT; it locks no role row. The setter takes the key EXCLUSIVE after its role and version rows, and a direct owner write takes it at the write. The class widens: any database login can take a guard key, in transaction or session form, because the advisory functions are PUBLIC. An EXCLUSIVE holder makes that tenant's and role's edits answer 503 while it holds the key, and a SHARED holder makes its level writes wait; nobody can release another session's key. A writer waiting for the key holds its role row `FOR UPDATE` up to the lock timeout, so that role's creates queue behind it (for runtime command transactions, bounded and ending in 503; a raw table-owner write has no such bound and may wait until the holder ends), and creates can still delay the writer (2). Two (tenant, role) pairs whose hashes collide are serialized. Availability only. (M7B-INVOICES-P2-R1, dated note: after migration 030 a direct caller of the edit routine that keeps its transaction open holds the header `FOR UPDATE` and, on the paths that reach the customer check, the customer `FOR KEY SHARE`; it takes the role's guard key `SHARED` only for manager, technician and sales_staff (store_owner takes none), and no role row; `invalid_state` and `conflict` return before the customer check and take no customer lock. All of it lasts until that transaction ends. A transaction that holds the key SHARED and then locks an Invoices role or version row, or a table-owner transaction that writes several pairs or locks another pair's version row, a customer or a draft's lines before a level, breaks THE RULE in 030's header and can deadlock (40P01, one side aborted, no wrong authority answer); availability only.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P1-R3: the draft save no longer locks an Invoices role row. From migration 031 it holds, for manager, technician and sales_staff, the role's guard key SHARED from after its customer read until COMMIT and, on the applied path, its Customers role row `FOR SHARE`; an Invoices level write waits for that key. A writer waiting for the key behind a save still holds its Invoices role row `FOR UPDATE`, and a later save of that role now queues on the key behind the writer, not on the row (bounded by the command transaction's lock timeout, ending in the existing 503). A table-owner transaction that locks a Customers role row or a customer and then writes an Invoices level can deadlock with a save (40P01; no wrong authority answer). Availability only.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-P1-R4: the save no longer anchors or locks its Customers role row; from migration 032 it holds, for manager, technician and sales_staff, the role's guard key SHARED from after its customer read until COMMIT and locks no role or version row of either domain. Every write of a Customers level now takes the same key EXCLUSIVE (032's trigger), so the class widens to the Customers domain: a Customers level write waits for in-flight draft saves and edits of that tenant and role (an edit decides only the Invoices level yet shares the key), Customers and Invoices setters of one pair serialize on the key, and a Customers setter waiting for the key holds its Customers role row `FOR UPDATE`, so that role's customer creates and customer edits queue on the row behind it and a later save or edit of the role queues on the key behind it (for runtime command transactions bounded by the lock and statement timeouts and ending in the existing 503). A login holding the key now delays Customers level writes too, and a hash collision serializes the Customers writes of two pairs. A table-owner transaction that writes several pairs, or both domains of one pair in separate statements, or locks a customer and then writes a level, the owner's direct call of 031's `create_v2`, and a direct `tmpos_app` transaction that holds the key SHARED and then runs the customer create or 024's customer edit can deadlock (40P01; no wrong authority answer). Availability only.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1: the draft discard holds the draft header `FOR UPDATE` and, for manager, technician and sales_staff, the role's guard key SHARED from after the header until COMMIT — the edit's order without the customer and the lines — and locks no role, version, customer or line row, so it adds no runtime cycle; a direct caller holding its transaction open holds both until it ends, the same class. Migration 033's line trigger reads a line's header `FOR SHARE`, so an owner's direct UPDATE or DELETE of a line, which locks the line before its trigger reads the header, can deadlock with an edit of the same open draft that holds the header `FOR UPDATE` before it replaces the lines (40P01, one side aborted, no wrong answer). Table-owner paths only: the runtime writes lines only through the definer routines, which take the header first. Availability only (033's header).)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 | Recorded, availability only; G-DBROLE, with DA-82 and DA-115 |
| DA-121 | **The levels read discloses any tenant's stored levels to the runtime role.** `tmpos_role.m7a_role_levels(p_tenant_id, p_role_id)` answers a tenant's stored Customers levels to `tmpos_app`, keyed by a tenant id and a role id, with no session key. It is the read every HTTP decision makes, keyed by the admission answer's tenant and role. It discloses how an owner configured roles, not customer data. Same class as 016's admission read of any principal's grants (DA-97). *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2, [ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the owner's read-back, `GET /api/v1/roles/levels`, calls the same `m7a_role_levels(uuid, text)` with the authorizer-selected trusted tenant. Isolation stays app-enforced: the table has row security enabled, a foreign key to `public.tenant` and every table privilege revoked from the runtime roles; the gap is the definer routine taking a bare tenant id.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4, [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the class widens by one routine. The read-back now calls 021's `tmpos_role_edit.m7a_role_level_states(uuid)`, which answers a tenant's three editable levels AND their versions to `tmpos_app`, keyed by a bare tenant id with no session key. The versions are what 021's edit asserts, so a runtime-role holder learns the precondition values it would need for a direct edit with a live owner key (DA-119). The authorizer's per-decision read is still 020's `m7a_role_levels`. The version table has row security enabled, no foreign key and no runtime-role privilege.)* *(M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1, [ADR-46](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the class widens by one more routine. `GET /api/v1/roles/levels/invoices` calls 026's `tmpos_role_invoice.m7a_invoice_role_level_states(uuid)`, which answers a tenant's three Invoices levels and their versions to `tmpos_app`, keyed by a bare tenant id with no session key, and 020's `m7a_role_levels` now also answers stored Invoices rows. It still discloses role configuration, not customer or invoice data.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 | G-DBROLE, with DA-97 |
| DA-122 | **The database does not enforce 020's rollback order.** 020 and the runtime move together: the shared startup preflight requires exactly 001–020 and 020's grants, and the P3 build refuses a 020 database. The documented order must be followed. First, while the 020 runtime still serves, the owner resets every edited role to its default through `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` and the operator confirms the down file's own check finds no non-default level. Then the runtime goes back to the P3 build. Then 020's down runs with its ledger row, which refuses while any stored level differs from its role's default. No runtime serves between the last two steps, since the P3 build refuses a 020 database. No order lets the rollback silently re-widen a narrowed role, because 020's down refuses until every stored level equals its default (the owner's reset widens it deliberately, through the audited route). Running 020's down before the runtime switch, once the levels are reset, also converges: the 020 runtime then fails closed (503) until it is replaced. Switching the runtime to the P3 build before the owner's reset leaves no runtime able to start until the 020 runtime is rolled forward again; the owner then resets the levels through it, and the order above is followed. A PL/pgSQL body records no dependency, so 016's, 017's, 018's or 019's rollback run while 020 is applied succeeds; 020's routines then raise, and 020's down refuses until the chain is whole again. `tests/db/tenantRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M7RL-16 witnesses the down's level guard, its READ COMMITTED refusal, its refusals of a widened grant, the exact restoration of 019's and 018's grants, and a clean re-apply; no test runs the out-of-order 016–019 rollback or starts a runtime across the rollback steps *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4, [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): with 021 applied, 021 is rolled back first. Its down refuses while any version row exists (DA-128) and does not give `tmpos_app` its EXECUTE on 020's set back, so the pre-021 runtime refuses to start until an operator deliberately re-grants it, and that re-grant reopens DA-125. Only then does the order above apply. The database does not enforce this order either: 020's down run while 021 is applied succeeds, dropping the trigger with 020's table; 021's routines then raise, and 021's down refuses until 020 is applied again. `tests/db/tenantRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` now runs on 001–020, rolling 021 back first when it is applied and re-applying it at the end)* *(M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1, [ADR-46](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): with 026 applied, 026 is rolled back before 025 and the rest of the chain, and the runtime moves with it (the preflight requires exactly 001–026; the P10 build refuses a 026 database and this build a 025 one). 026's down runs only under READ COMMITTED and refuses while ANY `invoices` row exists in 020's level table or 021's version table. An owner's reset to the default is an UPDATE and leaves the row, so after any Invoices edit the rollback needs the table owner to delete those rows deliberately, discarding the owner's Invoices configuration and its version history (DA-128's restart applies to them on a re-apply); no audit record is touched. `tests/db/invoiceRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M5IRL-09 witnesses the refusals, the clean down once the rows are deleted, 020's and 021's routines and grants left as they were, both CHECKs restored to `customers` only, and a clean re-apply)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 | G-MIGRATE: roll 026 back first (after a deliberate deletion of every Invoices row), then 021 (DA-128), then 020 after the owner's reset, each with the code |
| DA-123 | **The runtime's decision and the database's are two reads.** The authorizer and revalidator read the stored level in their own statements; 020's create and read read it again. A narrowing committed between them is caught by the create's locked re-read. A widening committed between them can make the runtime refuse a create the database would now allow: it fails closed, and the client retries | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-124 | **Owner-only editing and the unwired shop-app editor, flagged for owner decision.** (1) Only the store owner edits (the owner rule, `edit_role_levels`). The client's delegated editing (a manager holding `manage_role_permissions`) is refused on the server: that grant is owner-editable only in client memory, and doc 04's server-side parity and cap rules are undefined. Independent review contested the owner-only reading and argued for STOP instead; recorded, not resolved. (2) The shop app's role editor is unchanged and calls no route, and the SPA has no tenant-server session, so an owner narrowing a role in the shop app still does not reach the server. Connecting it is a named dependency of a later slice. (3) Custom roles, other domains and role deletion stay client-only. *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2, [ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): (2) is resolved for built-in Customers levels: the shop editor holds a tenant session and calls `GET` and `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels`. (1) stands: owner-only editing is still flagged for owner confirmation, contested and unresolved. A manager's read is refused 403, so the panel shows no levels and the note "Server-enforced Customers access can only be viewed and changed by the store owner. Changes in the table below affect this browser only." (3) stands: custom roles, other domains and deletion stay client-only.)* *(M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1, [ADR-46](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): (2) is also resolved for the built-in roles' Invoices level, as configuration only (DA-183): a second panel, "Server-saved Invoices level", reads `GET /api/v1/roles/levels/invoices` and saves through the same PUT. (1) stands: the same owner rule applies, and a manager's read is refused 403. (3) stands for custom roles, the remaining domains and deletion.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 | Owner decision; G-IAM, with DA-85 |
| DA-125 | **R1's `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` has no tenant or version precondition.** The last writer wins across tabs and owners: a save from a tab showing an older level overwrites a newer one. A save whose session tenant changed lands on the new tenant and is detected only by the read-back, which names that tenant: the panel restarts and keeps the notice "Your last change may have been applied to a different store. Review the levels below." until the next user change. Not changed in M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2. *(Entry witnesses on `86c4ce8`, `tests/db/roleLevelPrecondition.integration.test.mjs`: P4W-01, an A draft sent on the same owner's new B session, answered 200 and changed B with an edit audit and a completion; P4W-03, a stale tab on a second instance, answered 200 and restored Create over None; P4W-04, a draft read before an edit and a reset to the default, answered 200 and overwrote the reset. P4W-02, a save already in flight under A when the switch happened, wrote nothing in B in either ordering: it answered 503 and left only its lease.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2 | **CLOSED for the built-in Customers server-backed editor — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 1 (`e4ba510`; [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** What closed it: migration 021's `m7a_role_level_set_v2`, which the PUT now calls, writes only when the session's own tenant is the asserted `expectedTenant` (checked before any insert, locking no role row in either tenant) and the role's version is the asserted `expectedVersion` (checked unlocked, then again under the role row FOR UPDATE); 021's trigger advances the version on every UPDATE of a level row by any path, a reset to the default included, so no A → B → A cycle recreates a version; a refusal rolls back everything and is an unsealed 409 `write_conflict` with no level, version, audit record, event or completion; `tmpos_app` no longer executes 020's unconditional edit; and the shop panel sends the tenant and version of the read each draft came from and never resubmits a conflicting draft silently. Evidence: P4W-01, P4W-03 and P4W-04 red on `86c4ce8` and green on the final bytes, P4W-02 green on both (entry run on `86c4ce8`: 4 tests, 1 pass (P4W-02), 3 fail; final bytes 4/4); `tests/db/roleLevelVersionRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M7RV-05 (tenant mismatch), M7RV-06 (version conflict), M7RV-08 (reset to default), M7RV-10 (two edits from one read), M7RV-14 (a 020 edit parked across 021's apply), M7RV-15 (every other update path) and M7RV-16 (an update that moves a row to another key advances both keys); `tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-10; and `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SRL-08, SRL-08T, SRL-10, SRL-11 and SRL-12 (local gate on the `e4ba510` bytes: disposable-database gate 26 suites 525/525, shared-service CI replay 26 steps 479/479, shop browser gate 14/14 under the Firestore emulator, frontend 442/442, run-tests 159/159, 0 sentinel dials; `build:check` and the admin browser build were killed locally (SIGKILL) and are not counted as passed; CI run 36616730497, green on attempt 1). **Not closed by it:** custom roles and every other permission domain stay local-only; owner-only editing (DA-124); a direct edit by a runtime-role holder with a live owner key (DA-119); the table owner's unversioned writes (DA-129); and the counter restart after a deliberate rollback (DA-128). *(M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1, [ADR-46](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the Invoices level has the same precondition from the start: 026's `m7a_invoice_role_level_set` is 021's edit with the domain fixed to `invoices`, so a stale version or another tenant is the same unsealed 409 `write_conflict` writing nothing, and a reset to the default advances the version (`tests/db/invoiceRoleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M5IRLH-06 and M5IRLH-07; `tests/db/invoiceRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M5IRL-05 and M5IRL-06; `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SRL-14).)* |
| DA-126 | **Shop users need a PostgreSQL identity and exactly one active tenant grant.** The tenant login issues a session only when 007's resolver finds the Firebase UID's one `platform_identity` row (migration 001), an `app_user` account that is neither suspended nor pending activation, and an admitted grant (a login with no grant is 401); the role-level and customer routes then require exactly one active tenant grant (`user_membership`), since the tenant authorizer selects exactly one grant at the route's scope and refuses none or several with 403. No production provisioning exists for shop accounts; the browser gate inserts synthetic rows | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2 | Before deployment: provision the identity, account and grant rows for every shop account (G-IAM) |
| DA-127 | **The shop has no sign-out control in its tenant layout.** A tenant-server logout happens only on a Firebase sign-out (in any tab) or a user switch. So a tenant session can outlive the shop tab until its idle or absolute expiry (30 min idle / 8 h absolute) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2 | Product decision before deployment: a shop sign-out control |
| DA-128 | **021's rollback and re-apply restart the version counter.** 021's down refuses while any version row exists, so no version is discarded by accident. But once the table owner deliberately deletes those rows and rolls 021 back, a re-apply starts every role at version 0 again. A draft still open in a shop tab from before the rollback, at version N, can then match a version N issued after the re-apply against a different level, and its save succeeds (an ABA across the rollback). The down does not re-grant `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 020's edit: while rolled back the runtime role cannot edit a level at all, and the pre-021 runtime refuses to start until an operator re-grants it, which reopens DA-125. `tests/db/roleLevelVersionRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M7RV-12 witnesses the refusal with a version row, the refusal outside READ COMMITTED, the removal once the rows are deleted, no re-grant, and a clean re-apply | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-MIGRATE: a rollback of 021 is an owner-accepted risk for every draft still open; no runbook exists to end or invalidate open drafts first |
| DA-129 | **A table owner can change a level without moving its version.** Who can bypass: the owner of 020's `tmpos_role.tenant_role_level`; the owner of 021's schema and `tmpos_role_edit.role_level_version` (whoever applied 021 — 021 accepts an applier who only inherits the owner of 020's table, so the two owners can be different roles); any member of either owner role; and superusers. Four of their writes change a role's effective level and leave the version where it was: an INSERT of a non-NULL level (the insert branch of an upsert and `COPY FROM` included), a DELETE (a return to the default), a TRUNCATE, and disabling or dropping 021's trigger (after which updates move nothing; 021's edit then raises rather than apply). A draft read at that version is then accepted against a level it never saw. The version table's owner can also write or delete version rows directly: runtime and application writes reach that table only through the trigger, but its owner is not so limited. Running 020's `m7a_role_level_set`, which no runtime role can still execute, is not among them: it updates the row, so the trigger advances the version. No runtime role reaches any of this: `tmpos_app` holds no privilege on either table, and 021's postcondition proves it by every path *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the same class for 024's customer `row_version`: the customer table's owner, a member of that role or a superuser can rewrite a customer by a raw UPDATE without moving its version, so an editor's version stays current over a change it never saw. No runtime role reaches the table (024's postcondition).)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-DBROLE: hold the owning principal to an owner's standard (as DA-41 and DA-48); owner operations must not write, delete or truncate these tables or disable the trigger outside a migration |
| DA-130 | **020's apply-time postcondition no longer holds once 021 is applied.** 020's postcondition admits no non-internal trigger in schema `tmpos_role` and requires `tmpos_app` to hold EXECUTE on `m7a_role_level_set`. 021 places `trg_tenant_role_level_version_advance` on 020's table and revokes that EXECUTE. 020's file is byte-identical and its check is an apply-time proof, so nothing re-runs it; but a check that re-evaluated 020's postcondition against a 021 database would fail. The standing check is the startup preflight's `APPROVED_ROLE_GRANTS`, which reflects 021 | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | Recorded. Any future live role-topology check must use the newest migration's postcondition, not 020's |
| DA-131 | **The customer detail read writes no audit record.** `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` returns one customer's id and name and appends no `audit_event`, following the list's owner decision (DA-88). Migration 022's routine writes nothing (`tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` M7CD-07: no audit, outbox, idempotency, customer, session or level row moves) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-AUDIT stays open; decide with DA-88 whether reads of personal data are audited |
| DA-132 | **A direct call of the detail routine.** A holder of the runtime login with `tmpos_app` membership (`tmpos_app` itself is a NOLOGIN role) plus a live minted tenant session key can call `tmpos_customer_detail.m7a_customer_get` directly. It answers only that key's own tenant, under the same standing, level and status rechecks as the route, and a foreign tenant asserted is `denied`. This is the class of 020's `m7a_customer_page_v3` direct call and of DA-119's direct edit, adding no exposure the list's routine does not already give; ids are random, so enumeration is bounded by identifier entropy and session-bound authorization. The per-client rate limiter does not apply here: it applies only to HTTP requests. 022's postcondition checks the runtime ROLES (`tmpos_app`, `tmpos_audit_writer`, `anon`, `authenticated`) through membership, as 020's and 021's do; the runtime LOGIN's shape is enforced by the startup preflight, which M7CD-02b shows refusing an inherited carrier, a SET-only carrier and a direct grant | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-DBROLE, with DA-83, DA-93 and DA-119 |
| DA-133 | **The database does not enforce 022's rollback order.** The supported order is 022, then 021, then 020, the ledger's newest-first order. 022's rollback is independent of 021's fail-closed one (M7CD-08). If 020 is rolled back out of order while 022 is applied, 020's down succeeds, because a PL/pgSQL body records no dependency. Then detail reads by `manager`, `technician` and `sales_staff` raise in the routine, which reads 020's dropped table for their level, and the route answers 503: fail closed. `store_owner` reads keep working, because a store owner's Customers access is always Full and the routine never reads 020's table for it (`022_m7a_customer_detail.up.sql:224`, the `store_owner` branch). That is correct authorization, not a leak. 022's own rollback then refuses (its postcondition requires 020's `m7a_customer_page_v3`) until 020 is applied again. `tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` M7CD-09 exercises the out-of-order case for a `manager` only; the `store_owner` statement rests on the routine's code, not on that test | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | G-MIGRATE: roll back 022, then 021 (DA-128), then 020 (DA-122), each with its code |
| DA-134 | **Create responses sealed before this build replay without `Location`.** The create's plan before M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2 always sealed `headers: {}`. A same-key replay answers exactly what was sealed, so such a completion replays the 201 without `Location` for the rest of its 24 h retention; the body already carries `customer.id`. Nothing has been deployed, so such records exist only in disposable databases (`server/runtime/customerCreate.test.ts` replays a literal pre-change envelope) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | Recorded; none expected in any deployed environment |
| DA-135 | **Foreign and missing ids are not equalized in time.** The detail routine answers another tenant's id and a missing id with one call, the same SQL statement and the same outcome branch and row. The route answers both with the same status (404), the same error code and body shape and the same bounded log reason. Per-request metadata differs: the body's `requestId`, `X-Request-Id`, `Date` and similar headers. Their timing is not equalized, and the residual is unbounded by design: a foreign id's primary-key probe can find a row that the tenant filter then rejects, and a missing id's cannot. `tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs` M7CD-05 is a sanity check, not a constant-time proof: it logs the cold and warm medians and asserts they stay within max(2 ms, 25%). Local runs observed medians of about 0.8–1.2 ms for both, as observations only. 022 does not change 017's table, and a tenant-first index would not by itself remove the difference (design review C2.4). Over HTTP, enumeration is limited by the random id space and the per-client rate limiter. A direct routine call (DA-132) is limited only by session-bound authorization and identifier entropy | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-136 | **The route surface under `/api/v1/customers/` widened.** Before M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2, `/api/v1/customers/<anything>` was the terminal 404. Now `GET /api/v1/customers/<one raw segment of 1–128 characters without />` matches the detail template. Template lookup is keyed by method, so every other method on such a path is still the terminal 404. It is an authenticated route: the chain answers 401 or 403 first, then a non-canonical segment is 400 `invalid_request`. An empty segment, a trailing slash, an extra segment or a segment of 129 characters or more is still the terminal 404. The router admits this one template by name and refuses every other template, and every literal path directly below its prefix, at registration. *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the router now admits exactly two templates by name, this one and `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile`, the same segment followed by exactly `/profile`; lookup is an exact literal first, then the template ending in the id, then the template with the id before its suffix. So `GET /api/v1/customers/<segment>/profile` is an authenticated route too: 401 or 403 first, then 400 for a non-canonical segment. Any other extra segment, other method, empty segment or trailing slash is still the terminal 404, and every other template is still refused at registration.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): a third template, `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}`, on the same segment; so `PATCH /api/v1/customers/<segment>` is an authenticated route too, 401 or 403 first, then 400 for a non-canonical segment. Every other method, segment shape and template is unchanged.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 | Recorded (ADR-38 decision 3); amended by M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 and M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 |
| DA-137 | **An unconfirmed customer create is lost on a reload, and after a session change it can no longer be checked from the shop.** The shop's Customer directory page holds an undecided create (its key, its exact body, its name, the session CSRF token it was sent with and a send time) in memory only, and re-sends it only under that same token and within 23 hours. A reload discards the hold. Any new sign-in gives the page another token, and the attempt is then detached for good: after a session expiry (30 min idle / 8 h absolute, DA-127), after the shop is opened in a second tab of the same browser (every page load that opens a server-backed screen signs in, and a login ends the session presented to it: DA-143), or after the account's grant moves. A detached attempt has no "Check again"; its card says it "cannot be checked from here" and that "a row with the same name is not proof". An answer already on its way can still confirm it, shown as `"<name>" was saved on the server under the earlier connection.` (SCD-32, SCD-47); without one, the user is told only that the server may have saved it, and adding the name again uses a new key and can create a second customer. The outcome of a create that settles after its page has gone is kept in the same in-memory store until a page shows it (SCD-46), so it too is lost on a reload. Witnesses: `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-26, SCD-27, SCD-32 and SCD-33, and `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SCB-06. *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1, [ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): a reloaded page, or the shop opened in a second tab, now usually reuses the same session and token (DA-143, closed in code), so the second-tab case above no longer detaches an attempt by itself. An unconfirmed create is still lost on a reload, because the hold is in memory only, but a reload no longer by itself changes the session. Expiry, a moved grant and a different user's sign-in still give the page another token.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed; amended by M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 ([ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)), which leaves this behaviour as it was. Later-stage proposal: a server-provided tenant witness on the session read (the session's `subject` names its user, not its tenant), or a read of an attempt's outcome by its key, so the shop can confirm an attempt across sessions |
| DA-138 | **The customer GET requests are not bound to the session the page just checked.** The directory page reads the session before every request, but the list and detail GETs carry no session token: they are answered under whichever session cookie the browser holds when they arrive. If another tab replaces that cookie between the check and the read (the account signing in again after its grant moved, or another user signing in before this tab sees the change of user), the page can show the other session's rows until its next session check, which comes with its next request or a window focus. It is display only: every create carries the session CSRF token, so the server refuses (403) a create whose cookie session is not the page's, and the page sends no create on a token it has not just re-checked (SCD-31) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed. Later-stage proposal: a server change that binds the reads to the checked session (ADR-39 alternative (c)). **CLOSED IN CODE — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 ([ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Both customer GETs, cursor pages included, now require `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` equal to the presenting session's token and are otherwise refused 403 `forbidden` before the authorizer or any read, and the shop sends the token its session check just answered. Evidence: RB-01 to RB-06 (`tests/db/customerDirectory.integration.test.mjs`) and SCB-08 (`tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`); commit `5479d1e` (after the dependency commit `df3fc4e`), CI 36791433899 |
| DA-139 | **"Stop tracking" forgets an unconfirmed create.** The button and its warning are offered only while the attempt is shown as not confirmed, not while a first send or a "Check again" is out (SCD-16, SCD-23); a rate-limit wait does not disable it. The warning reads "Stop tracking does not cancel the request, and adding the same name again can create a second customer." It removes the held key and body, so that attempt can never be checked again, and reads the first page; during a rate-limit wait that read is held back until the wait ends (SCD-51). If the server did commit it, the customer is in the directory, and re-adding the name under a new key creates a second one (SCD-34) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed: a deliberate user action with an explicit warning |
| DA-140 | **The add form is offered from the browser's local role state.** The directory page shows its form when the shop's local role state holds Customers at Create or above, and the page itself is reached through the local `customers` feature guard. Neither can see the owner's server-side level (DA-85, DA-124). A user the server does not allow sees the form and gets "Refused by the server. This account may not add customers to this store." with nothing written (SCD-40, SCB-04); a user the server allows but the local state does not is not offered the form, and sees "Your role in this store does not include adding customers." Separately, and unchanged by this stage: the Customers screen's own "+ New Customer" creates a record in the browser's memory only, sends no request and is not checked against the server-side level (`src/components/Customers.localBanner.test.tsx` LCB-02) *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the directory page's Edit is offered the same way, when the local role state holds Customers at Edit or above; a user the server does not allow gets 403 with nothing written, and the form closes and its draft is cleared.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P9, [ADR-44](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the invoice draft's server-customer picker is offered the same way, when the plan includes the Customers feature (the directory route's own guard) and the local role state holds Customers at View or above, inside a Create Invoice dialog that needs Invoices at Create; a selection binds only while all three still hold. All three are checks in the browser: the server's 403 is the authority for the customer read alone, and no server route creates or authorizes an invoice. The owner's server-side level can disagree with the local Customers View hint. A user the server refuses gets the picker's access-denied state after one fresh session check, and a server customer already in the draft is dropped with "You can't view the customers saved on the server, so the server customer was removed from this draft."; a user the server allows but the local state does not sees "Server customers aren't available with your role or your store's plan." and no picker. Nothing is written either way.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10, [ADR-45](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the new repair ticket form offers the same field the same way, and a selection binds only while Repairs at Create also holds, a browser check like Invoices at Create; the 403 governs customer reads only and authorizes nothing about repairs. The P9 clause above first left Invoices at Create under "the server's 403 is the authority"; corrected.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed; the server's 403 is the authority for the customer routes only (no server route handles invoices or repair tickets). G-IAM, with DA-85 and DA-124 |
| DA-141 | **The shop's tenant clients enforce their response-size cap only after the whole body has been buffered, and do not cancel a non-JSON body.** The customers client (cap 524,288 characters, `src/tenant/customersClient.ts`) and the role-levels client (cap 16,384, `src/tenant/roleLevelsClient.ts`) read a JSON answer in full and compare its length afterwards, so an oversized body is downloaded and held in memory before it is refused (`src/tenant/customersClient.test.ts` CDC-06 witnesses the refusal, not an early stop). The session client (`src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.ts`) parses a 200 JSON answer with no cap of its own. None of the three cancels a body it does not read, such as an answer that is not JSON. What bounds it: a body that is read is cut off by the client's 15-second request timer, and every request is same-origin with redirects refused, so the body comes from the shop's own origin. Reading the body as a stream, to stop at the cap, was considered and not adopted (ADR-39 alternative (f)) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-142 | **A rate-limit wait is forgotten when the connection changes or the page is re-opened.** The directory page disables its read, add and check buttons for the seconds a 429 asked for. While a wait runs, a later deadline wins (a shorter `Retry-After` never shortens it), a wait text states the wait running when it is written (SCD-51), and nothing is read: a customer opened then shows the wait and sends nothing (SCD-52), and a window focus sends no session check (SCD-60). A first-page read the wait blocked (after a confirmed create, a refused create, "Stop tracking" or a cursor reload) is remembered and runs once when the wait ends; it is a read, never a create (SCD-56). But the wait is page state: a new connection resets it and reads again at once, and a page left and re-opened starts without it. Requests can then leave before the server's delay has passed: the first-page read and, on a customer's address, that customer's read. The server's limiter still decides them, so these are wasted requests, not a bypass. Two display limits, with no test: a wait text is not revised when a later answer lengthens the wait, so a customer view can name the shorter wait while its button stays disabled for the longer one; and when two blocked first-page reads fall in one wait, only the later one's note is kept, so "The list changed on the server, so it was loaded again from the start." can be lost | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-143 | **Every page load by a signed-in user that opens a server-backed shop screen signs in to the tenant boundary again, against a login limit of 5 per account per 5 minutes.** The shop's session client remembers a session only in the page's memory. So each page load that opens the role screen's server panel or the Customer directory sends one login, which the two share within a page. The tenant login's account limit is 5 per 5 minutes (G-IDEMPOT row, Limits; `server/runtime/rateLimit.ts`), so a sixth such load inside the window is answered 429. The directory page then shows the wait and no rows; SCD-39 witnesses how the page handles a rate-limited sign-in, not the limit itself. Each login also ends the session the browser presented, which a page already open in another tab sees as a changed connection (DA-137). The role screen has had the same sign-in behaviour since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2; the directory page is its second consumer | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed. Before deployment: decide whether a page load should first read the current session, and whether the account limit suits shop use. **CLOSED IN CODE — M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 ([ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** A page load now reads the current session first and reuses it, with no login, when the session 200's new `subject` equals the digest of the signed-in user's UID, so reloads and reopens within the window send no further login; the account limit itself is unchanged and still applies to real sign-ins. Evidence: SCB-07 (one login and no 429 across ten loads in one window) and TSC-15 to TSC-24; commit `5479d1e` (after the dependency commit `df3fc4e`), CI 36791433899 |
| DA-144 | **The Customers screen's records and the server directory are not linked, and nothing is migrated.** The shop now shows customers in two places. The Customers screen holds contact details, loyalty, history and its own create and duplicate rules only in the browser's memory, and they are lost when the page is reloaded. The Customer directory shows the server's id and name. No record is matched by name, no local record gains a server id, and nothing is imported, migrated or erased; a customer added in one place does not appear in the other. Each page says so (the Customers banner, `src/components/Customers.localBanner.test.tsx` LCB-01; the directory's sub-lines, SCD-01; and SCD-43). *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the directory now also holds an optional phone and email and a creation time per server customer, shown on a customer's page. The Customers screen and its records are unchanged; nothing is imported, no local record gains a server id, and the two are still not linked.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P9, [ADR-44](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): a new invoice draft can now name a server customer by its server id, but cannot be created with one (DA-175). No local record gains a server id, nothing is matched by name, phone or email, and no existing invoice, repair or order reference changes (DA-176).)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Product decision (G-PERSIST, G-PRIVACY): the customer record's server fields, their classification and retention, and the cutover from browser memory. G-PERSIST stays OPEN |
| DA-145 | **Locked sidebar items have no programmatic "unavailable" state.** Before activation the shop's sidebar shows the items a store's plan will unlock as plain elements with a lock icon and a hover title, "Complete activation to unlock" (`src/layouts/TenantLayout.tsx`). They carry no role or disabled state that assistive technology can read, so a screen-reader user hears a label with nothing saying it is unavailable. Pre-existing: the Customer Directory entry joins that list under the same rule as every other item (`src/layouts/TenantLayout.customerDirectory.test.tsx` NAV-03) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed. Accessibility review (G-A11Y) |
| DA-146 | **The entry-point suite's bare-import case has a 5 s limit that a cold start on the development host can exceed.** `server/entry/main.test.ts` case 1 imports `main.ts` in a child process with a 5 s limit. On the 8 GB development host it measured 0.5 to 0.6 s warm and 5.1 s cold, killed by the limit. In this stage's final local gate the node lane (`node scripts/run-tests.mjs`, 160 suites) failed on it in two of three runs and passed in the third; alone it passes. The first of those runs, on a loaded host, also failed one case of `server/composition/productionTransactions.test.ts`, which did not recur and whose name was not captured. Neither file is changed by this stage. So a local red on that case is not evidence about a change, and a local green does not show the limit is comfortable; exact-commit CI is the deciding run | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 | Recorded, not changed. Test-reliability review before launch |
| DA-147 | **The role panel's `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` is not bound to the session it checked.** M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 binds only the routes that declare `sessionBinding: 'required'`, the two customer GETs *(three since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6: the profile read declares `sessionBinding: 'required'` too)*. The role screen's server panel reads the levels with no session CSRF token, so the read is answered under whichever session cookie the browser holds when it arrives. If another tab replaced that cookie after the panel's session check, the panel can show that session's levels. The answer names its tenant, and the panel compares it with the tenant its drafts came from (a read naming another tenant clears every draft and says the tenant changed, [ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)). Every PUT carries the session CSRF token and its `expectedTenant`, so nothing is written to the other session's tenant. It is display only, and only an owner can read the levels | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 | Recorded, not changed. Later-stage proposal: the same binding for the levels read |
| DA-148 | **The tenant session's `subject` is a stable per-user identifier that the session holder can read.** Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 the tenant session 200s carry `subject`, SHA-256 over a fixed label, the provider and the Firebase UID. It is the same for every session of that user, in every tenant and over time, and it is not keyed: anyone who knows a user's UID can compute it and recognise that user's sessions. Any script on the tenant origin that can call `GET /api/v1/session` with the browser's cookie can read it, as it can already read the session CSRF token. It names no tenant and grants nothing. It is sent only in those two 200 bodies, which every response's `Cache-Control: no-store` covers, and it is never logged | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 | Recorded, not changed. Review with G-PRIVACY: whether a keyed or per-session subject is needed |
| DA-149 | **Session reuse rests on the shop's digest of the Firebase UID, and a reused session is not re-verified with a fresh ID token.** The server does not know which user the page believes is signed in: the shop reuses a session when its `subject` equals the digest it computes from the Firebase UID it holds, and the result is only as current as the page's Firebase state. The server still authorizes every request on the session's own principal, so a wrong comparison can show one identity's data under another's sign-in but grants nothing the cookie does not. A user switch or sign-out still ends the previous session through the shop's auth-state handling ([ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md) decision 1). Before this stage each page load's login verified a fresh ID token; a reused session now lasts until a logout, its idle or absolute expiry (DA-127) or a server-side revocation, so a change made only at the identity provider (a disabled account, for example) reaches it only through those | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 | Recorded, not changed. Before deployment: decide whether a reused session needs a periodic ID-token re-verification |
| DA-150 | **A reload or a new page whose cookie names an expired session still signs in on its own; only an end seen by an open page waits for Reconnect.** Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 the shop's session client answers `ended`, with no ID token asked for and no login sent, only for a 401 after it has confirmed this user's session during the page's life. That memory is the page's own and nothing is stored, and the server's 401 does not say whether a session expired, was revoked or never existed. So a reload, a new tab or a page opened later, whose cookie names an ended session, treats the 401 as a first establishment and signs in once through the login and its limits, as before (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-78; TSC-28). A person whose access was removed is still refused by that login (admission), so it re-establishes only what a fresh sign-in would *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the customer edit keeps this: Save and "Check again" take the plain step, which signs in only when no session is confirmed yet for this user or the cookie names another identity's session; after a confirmed end only Reconnect does. An edit whose outcome is unknown is held in the page's memory only, so a reload loses it, as it loses an unconfirmed create.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8, [ADR-43](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): Search, Clear, Refresh and Load more under a search follow the same plain rule, and an ended session clears the rows, the cursor and the search.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P9, [ADR-44](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the invoice draft's server-customer picker keeps this too: its reads and its one plain session check on a window focus or a return to the tab never sign in after an end, only its Reconnect does, whose token then counts as the dialog's first (no "connection changed" notice); a server customer chosen under a session that ended is dropped from the draft with a notice.)* *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10, [ADR-45](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the new repair ticket draft's field follows the same rule; after a confirmed end a window focus, a return to the tab and a reopened dialog send no login, and only the Reconnect click passes `reconnect` (SCP-50). The P7 and P8 clauses above first said those steps "never sign in"; corrected.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Recorded, not changed. Later-stage proposal: a server-provided reason on the session read, or a stored marker, if a reload must also wait for Reconnect |
| DA-151 | **Lone surrogates and NUL in a phone or email are refused by the runtime only.** The runtime (`REFUSED_CHARACTER_RE`, `server/runtime/customerDirectory.ts`) and the shop client refuse a C0 control including NUL and a lone surrogate. Migration 023's CHECKs and its create cannot express either: PostgreSQL text cannot hold NUL, and a UTF-8 database cannot hold a lone surrogate, which the driver sends as U+FFFD, a character the CHECKs admit (`tests/db/customerContact.integration.test.mjs` header; `server/runtime/customerCreate.test.ts`). So a direct caller holding `tmpos_app` and a live key (DA-118) can store U+FFFD where the HTTP route would have refused the request. The same split applies to names since 017 *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the same split applies to 024's edit routine, which restates 017's and 023's CHECK rules: a direct caller can store U+FFFD in a name, phone or email by an edit where the HTTP route refuses a lone surrogate.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-152 | **Customers created before migration 023 have no creation time.** 023 adds `created_at` with no default, so every existing row keeps NULL; the default `now()` applies only to rows inserted afterwards, and the migration's time is never written as a creation time. The profile read answers `createdAt: null` and the shop shows "Not recorded" (M7CN-02, M7CN-07; SCD-87). No trustworthy source for those times is recorded: 017 kept no creation time, and the create's audit record names no customer (DA-111), so it cannot be joined to one. Nothing has been deployed, so such rows exist only in disposable databases *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): an edit never writes `created_at`: a legacy customer's NULL stays NULL through an edit (`tests/db/customerEdit.integration.test.mjs` M7CE-04; M7CH-01), and the shop still shows "Not recorded".)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Owner decision: any backfill, and from what source. None is proposed |
| DA-153 | **Any role at Customers View or above reads a customer's phone and email.** The profile read is under `view_customers`, the list's and the detail read's permission, so every role whose Customers level is View or above, for example `technician` by default, and any such role under a `read_only` account or tenant (which caps tenant route permissions at View, not below it), can read every customer's phone and email in its tenant. That is the stage's design (the same authority as the detail read), but contact details are personal data, and the read writes no audit record (DA-131's class) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Owner decision (privacy, G-PRIVACY, G-IAM): whether contact details need a separate permission, a narrower default, or an audited read |
| DA-154 | **An unresolved create's held body, with its phone and email, stays in that tab's memory until the directory page next renders.** The directory page holds an unconfirmed create's exact body, contacts included, in memory only, so that "Check again" can re-send the same bytes (DA-137). Once the attempt can never be sent again, the body is emptied unless it was name-only: at once on a detach (a connection change), and past the 23-hour check-again cutoff on the page's next render (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-92). An idle page, or one the user has navigated away from, keeps the body in that tab's memory until a render or a reload. It is never displayed (the card shows no contact) and never re-sent *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the same holds for an edit whose outcome is unknown: its exact body, with any phone or email it sets, is held in memory only (at most one per user) so that "Check again" can re-send the same bytes. It is dropped with its bytes on another token, an ended session, a user change or a sign-out, and past the 23-hour cutoff its body is emptied on the directory page's next render. The hold outlives the page across in-app navigation, so an idle tab keeps the body until a render or a reload. It is never displayed and never re-sent on its own.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-155 | **No retention or deletion policy for customer contact data.** Phones and emails are stored in clear on 017's table and kept until a deletion decision exists; there is no delete, edit or export command for them, and 023's rollback refuses while any customer holds a phone, an email or a creation time, so a rollback is never the way to remove them. Bind parameters in a database log expose contacts as they already expose session keys (G-DBROLE). Retention and deletion stay an explicit pre-live decision (owner brief) *(M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): there is now an edit command: an authorized user can correct or clear a phone or email. Clearing sets the column to NULL. That is a field update, not an erasure: earlier values may remain in backups, the write-ahead log and a database log that captured bind parameters, and the edit's sealed completion holds its answer's name (never a contact) for the key's retention. Retention and deletion stay open.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 | Owner decision before any live data (G-PRIVACY, G-PERSIST) |
| DA-156 | **`name` is required in every customer edit (a stated deviation from the owner brief).** The brief made `name`, `phone` and `email` each optional in `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}`, with omitted meaning unchanged. The command kernel seals the 200 from the plan before the commit (`server/runtime/commandTransaction.ts`), and only values cross the store port, so a planner cannot know a stored name it was not sent, while the 200 must carry the name as stored. Requiring `name` keeps the answer exact without widening the kernel; a body without it is the bounded 400. `phone` and `email` stay optional. The shop always sends the name (`customerEditBody`) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded for the owner's confirmation. A later kernel change (a response sealed from the store's answer) may make `name` optional additively |
| DA-157 | **A stored customer id outside the outbox's aggregate-id shape can be read but not edited.** The edit's target must be a canonical lowercase UUID (else 400 before the body is read) and must also match the outbox's `UUID_RE`, a versioned, RFC-variant UUID (else the same 400 after the key is acquired). Every id this runtime mints is a v4, but 017 lets a direct writer choose any uuid, so a customer inserted outside the runtime with, for example, a nil or non-RFC-variant id is listed and read but never edited (`server/runtime/customerUpdate.test.ts`) | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded, not changed; nothing deployed holds such an id |
| DA-158 | **A customer edit's audit record does not say which fields changed.** The brief asks for the changed field names if supported. The audit writer's metadata allow-list has no key for them, so the record's metadata stays `{}`: it names the actor, the tenant, the action, the permission and the edited customer (`subject_customer_id`), and never which of name, phone and email changed, or any value | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | G-AUDIT: an allow-listed field-name key is a widening of the shared audit shape, an owner decision |
| DA-159 | **On a shared database, steps that commit customer edits must run after every step that rolls 024 back.** Every change committed through the route leaves the customer at `row_version` 2 or above and an audit record naming it, and a change by a direct routine call leaves the version above 1 (a no-op or a conflict leaves neither); audit records are append-only, and 024's rollback refuses while either exists. In CI the HTTP edit suite and the browser step (SCB-11 and SCB-12 commit edits) are the last two database steps, after every suite that rolls 024 back (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). Re-running a rollback suite locally against a persistent database after them refuses at 024's guard; the default disposable cluster, created and destroyed per run, avoids it *(M7B-INVOICES-P2: they are no longer the last two database steps — later stages added database steps after them, and the draft edit's HTTP step now follows the browser step — but no later step rolls 024 back, so the rule still holds; the draft edit's own twin is DA-207.)* | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded; a CI ordering rule for every later step that commits an edit |
| DA-160 | **A direct call with an expected version outside bigint is refused by PostgreSQL's argument cast, whose message may repeat the number.** `tmpos_customer_edit.m7a_customer_update` judges its arguments with fixed messages that name no value, but a version outside the 64-bit range never reaches it: the bind's cast raises 22003, and PostgreSQL's out-of-range message can repeat the number sent (`tests/db/customerEdit.integration.test.mjs` M7CE-05 records which). It is a version number, never personal data, and the HTTP route bounds `expectedVersion` to 1 to 2^53 − 2 before anything reaches the database | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-161 | **Applying 024 takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on the customer and audit tables for the apply's duration.** `ALTER TABLE` adds the version column (a constant default: no rewrite) and its CHECK, which scans the customer table once, and adds the audit subject column and builds the partial index `idx_audit_event_subject_customer_occurred` without `CONCURRENTLY` (009's precedent), over the whole audit table. Every read and write of either table waits meanwhile. Nothing is deployed, so no live table is affected | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Availability note for any managed apply (G-MIGRATE): size the audit table and plan the window first |
| DA-162 | **Each applied customer edit records a multixact on its row.** The edit routine locks the customer row FOR UPDATE and then updates it inside a PL/pgSQL exception block, a subtransaction, so PostgreSQL records the row's locker as a multixact. That costs multixact space and vacuum work per applied edit; it changes no answer | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded, operational only (G-DBROLE monitoring) |
| DA-163 | **The lost-COMMIT branch where nothing committed is not exercised end to end for the customer edit.** A COMMIT whose answer is lost is a 503 with an unknown outcome. The branch where the edit did commit is proven over HTTP: the same-key retry replays the committed 200, never a second change (`tests/db/customerEditHttp.integration.test.mjs` M7CH-05). The branch where it did not commit, whose retry must wait for the lease to expire, reclaim it and then evaluate the edit afresh, relies on the shared idempotency kernel's reclaim and is not driven for this route | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded; a later test-hardening item |
| DA-164 | **The shop reads a 413 or 415 answer to an edit as a refused edit.** As for the create, `customersClient.update` maps 400, 413 and 415 to `invalid`, and a first send answered so drops the attempt with nothing held. The server's own 413 and 415 come before any key is acquired, so nothing was done; an intermediary's 413 or 415 would be read the same way | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded (create parity), not changed |
| DA-165 | **Two of the shop edit's ordering guards are proved by code structure, not by behaviour.** SCD-144 checks, by reading the source of `ServerCustomerDirectory.tsx`, that the route is taken in the layout phase together with the edit clear, so a settlement that lands after the commit is never erased by a later route clear; it does not drive that race in a browser, because a behavioural test of it depends on React commit timing and would be flaky. The guard that stops an edit-state update after the page has unmounted is not exercised by any test. Neither is behavioural browser evidence | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded; a behavioural test is deferred |
| DA-166 | **A history-only secret-scan exception is matched by rule, file and line, not by commit.** `scripts/check-security-gates.mjs` allows a `historyOnlyFindings` entry wherever a history finding has the same gitleaks rule, path and line. The two owner-approved entries for commit `8e0c775` (`server/runtime/customerUpdate.test.ts:197`, `tests/db/customerEditHttp.integration.test.mjs:1023`) would therefore also allow a different literal at exactly that line in a later commit. The current-tree scan still requires the exact expected inventory, so such a literal fails the gate unless one push adds it in one commit and removes it in a later one | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded; binding the entries to a commit needs a reconciler change, not made |
| DA-167 | **A search's text can reach request logs outside the application; redacting it is a pre-deployment requirement.** `GET /api/v1/customers?q=…` carries the search text, typically part of a customer's name, in its request target. The application logs the path only, never `q`, and no refusal or error repeats it (M7CS-U06, M7CS-08); the shop keeps the text out of the address bar, history and storage. But a load balancer, proxy, CDN or platform router in front of a deployment may record the whole request target in its access log. Those layers were not inspected or changed in this stage, and nothing is deployed | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 | **Pre-deployment requirement:** before the tenant entry is deployed, access-log and edge configuration must redact `q` (and the search `cursor`, DA-171) on this path, or not log the query; G-PRIVACY, G-OBSERVE |
| DA-168 | **The name search's cost grows with the tenant's customers, or with the whole table under a sequential plan; no installed index evaluates its substring predicate.** The predicate is `upper(name collate "und-x-icu") collate "C" LIKE` an escaped `'%text%'` pattern, filtered to the derived tenant. The customer table's indexes are `customer_pkey` (`customer_id`) and `customer_tenant_page_idx` (`tenant_id, name, customer_id`): the page index keeps a search to the tenant's range in page order and serves a continuation's keyset position, but no installed index evaluates the uppercase substring predicate itself. The planner walks the tenant's range of the page index in page order until its page is full, or scans the whole table and sorts the matches, so a rare or absent text uppercases every name in the tenant's range or, under the sequential plan, in the table: its cost grows linearly. On a local disposable PostgreSQL 16.10 cluster with 25,000 synthetic customers (a tenant of 20,000): no match 8.459 ms and a rare match 8.279 ms with literal parameters, each by a sequential scan of all 25,000 rows; about 13 ms over the tenant's 20,000 index entries under a generic plan; a common match 0.121 ms and a continuation page 0.309 ms, both through the page index. Modest local synthetic evidence, not a benchmark. Each search is bounded by the read's 2.7 s statement limit (then a 503) and, over HTTP, by the per-client rate limit | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 | Recorded, not a defect fixed here. No substring accelerator is installed: a trigram index would need the `pg_trgm` extension, which this work does not add. An owner decision when tenant sizes require it |
| DA-169 | **Which names a search matches depends on ICU.** 025 refuses to apply where the ICU root collation `pg_catalog."und-x-icu"` is absent, so a server without ICU never answers a different match. The uppercase mapping comes from the ICU library PostgreSQL is built with: an ICU change (a server or operating-system upgrade, or a replica or restore on a server built with another ICU) can change which names a text matches. No index or stored value depends on the mapping, so nothing is corrupted, but answers can change. The root locale applies no language rule: Turkish dotless `ı` uppercases to `I`, so it matches `i` and `I`, while a dotted capital `İ` stays itself and is not found by `i`; Greek letters keep their accents, and final and medial sigma both uppercase to `Σ`. It is a full uppercase mapping (`ß` matches `SS`), not case folding | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 | Recorded; record the ICU version with any managed database and re-check search answers after an ICU change (G-MIGRATE) |
| DA-170 | **The name search applies no Unicode normalization and no accent folding.** A name stored decomposed (NFD, for example `e` followed by a combining acute accent) does not match a search typed composed (NFC, `é`), or the reverse; `e` does not match `é`, so `jose` does not find `José`. Names are stored exactly as created (doc 05: no normalization), and the search compares UTF-8 bytes after uppercasing. A search cursor's digest is of the text's own bytes too, so an NFC and an NFD spelling are two different searches (`server/runtime/customerDirectory.test.ts` M7CS-U03). The database behaviour is stated by 025's design; the suite proves case-insensitive non-ASCII matching (`É`/`é`, `ß`/`SS`), not an NFD name | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 | Recorded for a product decision: accent-insensitive or normalized matching changes answers |
| DA-171 | **A search cursor carries an unkeyed digest of the search text.** Its last 16 bytes are the first 16 bytes of the SHA-256 of the normalized text, with no key, so whoever sees a search cursor (for example in an access log that redacts `q` but not `cursor`) can confirm a guessed search text by hashing it. The shop holds a cursor in the page's memory only and sends it only to read the next page | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 | Recorded; covered by DA-167's redaction (redact `cursor` with `q` on this path). A keyed digest is a later option |
| DA-172 | **Cancelling a conflicted customer edit during a rate-limit wait leaves the customer's page showing the wait.** Cancel on a form in conflict reads the profile again, so that Edit starts from the current version (`cancelEdit`, `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.tsx`). While a 429 wait runs, that read is answered in the page with the rate-limited state, so the page shows the wait, with the last known details, instead of the profile until the user reads it again after the wait. Nothing is sent or lost. Reported at M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7's review and recorded here once | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-173 | **An edit whose outcome is unknown, held under a session that has ended, is dropped after Reconnect.** A held edit is bound to the session token it was sent with (doc 05). Reconnect establishes a new session with a new token, so the held edit is dropped, never sent, with the page's note that the change could not be confirmed, and "Check again" is no longer offered for it; the user opens the customer to see its current details, as after a reload (DA-137 for a create). Reported at M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7's review and recorded here once | M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 | Recorded, not changed |
| DA-174 | **The audit writer's static diagnostic fails three of its checks, before and after M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7.** `scripts/diagnostics-audit-event-writer-static-check.ts` reports 35/38 on the current tree: C4 (the writer imports `getRuntimeDb` from `./db`), C5 (the writer reuses the audit-event contract) and C7 (every INSERT targets `audit_event` only) fail, because their source-pattern checks do not match the writer's current source. The same three fail on the tree before `8e0c775`, so P7 did not cause them. The new case "DA-182: a 422 whose correlator spells "555" meets the refusal contract; a refusal that reflects a contact, or a foreign correlator, does not" sends a fixed correlator containing `555` and proves both directions. The same class in `server/composition/productionTransactions.test.ts`'s audit contact scan, whose bound values include random UUIDs (the event id among them), now excludes any value that is a whole canonical UUID and nothing else, which cannot carry a phone or an email. There is no modified-at column. | DA-187 | **Draft retention and privacy are undecided, and nothing removes a draft.** A draft's lines hold free-text descriptions a team member types, and the draft names a customer by id. No route, routine or job deletes, redacts or ages out a draft, a line or the audit record that names it; migration 028's rollback refuses while any of them exists, so after the first save a rollback needs the table owner to remove them deliberately. A database log that captures bind parameters exposes line descriptions and session keys (028's header; DA-102) *(M7B-INVOICES-P2: each applied edit adds one draft-naming audit record (scope `store`, action `store.invoice_draft.update`) and one `invoice_draft.updated` outbox row carrying the store id, customer id and line count (no contact, no line); nothing removes them. An edit deletes the replaced lines; their old descriptions survive only where database history or a parameter log keeps them (DA-102). 029's rollback refuses while any draft's version is above 1 and leaves the audit and outbox evidence in place; 028's rollback still refuses while any audit record names a draft. Still undecided before live use.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1: a discard removes nothing. It keeps the header, its lines and their descriptions, the customer reference and every audit record, and adds one draft-naming audit record (scope `store`, action `store.invoice_draft.discard`, metadata `{}`) and no outbox row. The product's reads leave a discarded draft out, but the table owner can still read it, through 028's page and get too (only `tmpos_app` lost their EXECUTE). A discard is not erasure and adopts no retention period; still undecided before live use.)* *(M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-P1, [ADR-50](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): the same open decision now also covers issued invoices — whose immutable snapshots keep the customer's name, the line descriptions and the store's seller details — the stores' issuing settings, and the audit records that name them.
