| DA-198 | **A sign-out scrubs nothing from the Server drafts hold store: the signed-out user's session token can stay in the shop page's memory.** The Server drafts section keeps the session token on each save attempt in its module-level hold store, whatever the attempt's state (unresolved, created or refused): to re-send an unresolved one ("Check again") and to read a created one back only under that session. The scrub runs in `ServerInvoiceDrafts` (`src/tenant/ServerInvoiceDrafts.tsx`, its `detachOthers` effect) when an Invoices page mounts or renders a UID, and takes the token only from OTHER users' attempts: a user change scrubs the previous user's attempts once the new user's Invoices page renders. A sign-out never renders the page signed out: the session is cleared and the route guard (`src/components/AccessGuard.tsx`) redirects to sign-in, unmounting the page (a DEV session is exempt), so nothing is scrubbed then. The signed-out user's token, and an unconfirmed attempt's key and body, stay in page memory until another user's Invoices page renders, the attempt is detached (an unconfirmed one once the same user's new session is seen), read back or removed (a created one then says it was saved under an earlier connection; a refused one stays until Dismiss or Stop tracking), or the page reloads. It is in memory only, never in browser storage, a URL, the DOM or a log. The logout is best effort: `src/context/AccessContext.tsx` starts `tenantSession.logout()` without waiting for it, and `src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.ts` catches any failure; a successful logout makes the server refuse the token, while a failed one leaves that server session until its idle timeout **Closed in M7B-INVOICES-P1 (closing round, commit `e79251d`) for the Server drafts section and the picker:** a sign-out is now heard where it starts. `src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.ts` gains `onSessionEnd(listener)` and `sessionEndsSoFar()`: at the start of every `logout()` and `reset()`, before anything is sent or awaited, the end count is bumped and every listener runs synchronously; one that throws stops neither the sign-out nor the other listeners, and its error is rethrown unchanged in a queued microtask once all have run. Two listeners are registered at module load and hear it whether or not a page is mounted: the drafts hold store (`bindDraftHoldsToSessionEnd`, which runs `detachOthers(null)`) takes the token from every user's attempt, settled ones included, and an unconfirmed one's body too, so it can never be sent again; and the picker forgets its module-scope (UID, token) pair, while `sessionContextOf(uid, csrf, since)` mints a context only for a session confirmed since the last end and every comparison uses the non-mutating `isContextOf` (`hasSessionPair` only reads whether a pair is kept). While mounted, the drafts section and the open picker dialog listen too and, synchronously, start a token-less generation, cancel their reads (with them the name queue and the reopen's read), end a rate limit's wait and show the ended session; the section also closes the new-draft dialog, whose state held the token. Nothing is read and nobody is signed in because of it. Save, Check again and a 403's fresh check compare the end count across their session await and take nothing from an answer asked for before a sign-out began. Both module registrations release on a hot reload's dispose. Evidence: jsdom `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceDrafts.test.tsx` SID-43 to SID-45, SID-49 and SID-51 to SID-53, `src/tenant/ServerCustomerPicker.test.tsx` SCP-59 to SCP-63 and `src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.test.ts` TSC-32 to TSC-34 (TSC-33 also requires the client's header to name every module that listens); in Chromium, SDB-05 and SDB-08 (DA-193). Evidence limits: the hot-dispose release is untested (development only, absent from builds), and the new-draft dialog's hand-over, which captures `since` before its own session check, has no sign-out race case of its own. The logout itself stays best effort: a failed one leaves that server session to its idle timeout. The Customer directory page, deliberately unchanged, does not hear `onSessionEnd`; its residual moves to DA-200 *(M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2, commit `68bc927`: the hand-over's sign-out race now has cases of its own. The new-draft dialog keeps the section's generation (its count and token) and `sessionEndsSoFar()` from when it opened, and checks them before it asks for its session and again before it takes the answer: an overtaken check applies nothing (no customer, no context, no notice), sends nothing and signs nobody in. jsdom SID-55 (the section adopts a new generation before the old answer arrives) and SID-56 (the section finds the session ended while the check is out) fail on the entry source and pass; SID-54 (a sign-out while the check is out) and SID-57 (StrictMode) pass on both and are regression guards; in Chromium, SSE-03 is a regression guard of the session client's epoch only, since an answer the harness holds at the network arrives after the logout began. A third module-load listener now hears every sign-out, the Customer directory's create hold store (DA-200). The hot-dispose release is still untested.)* | M7B-INVOICES-P1 | Closed (M7B-INVOICES-P1 closing round) for the Server drafts section and the picker; the Customer directory page's residual is DA-200 |
| DA-199 | **Outside its dialogs, the Server drafts section lets focus fall to the page body when a focused control disappears.** In `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceDrafts.tsx` four buttons can disappear while they hold focus, and nothing takes focus then, so it falls to `<body>`: "Show server drafts" is removed by its own press; "Load more" goes when its result removes it (the last page, or a failed continuation); the stores' Try again or Reconnect goes as soon as its retry starts (the stores read shows its loading state); and the drafts list's Try again goes as soon as its retry starts (the first page is read again). Inside the two dialogs (the reopen and "New server draft") and from the attempt's card, focus is managed and taken back. An accessibility gap, outside the dialogs only **Closed in M7B-INVOICES-P1 (closing round, commit `e79251d`):** one recovery for the section, outside its dialogs. The section tracks the control that last took focus in it (`trackFocus`); after every commit, a render that removed or disabled that control while focus was still on it, or had fallen from it to `<body>`, hands focus to the place named for it, by default the "Server drafts" heading (`drafts-title`, `tabIndex` −1). So "Show server drafts", the stores' Try again or Reconnect and the list's Try again land on the heading, as does a render that takes the focused control away through a new generation, a session end, a rate limit's wait, a page one that drops rows or the local role hint taking New away; the attempt card's Check again, Stop tracking and Dismiss hand focus to the heading themselves. "Load more" is `aria-disabled`, never `disabled`, during its read (a press meanwhile reads nothing), so it keeps focus while more pages remain; the last page hands focus to the first appended row's Open, and a failed continuation to the heading. Focus on another control or in a dialog is never taken, and a pointer press outside the tracked control ends the tracking, so focus the user left on `<body>` stays theirs. Evidence: jsdom SID-46 to SID-48 (each named site, not every possible render) and SID-50; in Chromium, SDB-06 (Show server drafts, both Try again buttons and Reconnect to the heading; Load more through its read, on the last page and on a lost read) *(M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2, commit `33972c0`: "the local role hint taking New away" above is now the eligibility state taking New away (a store change while the new store is asked, a refusal, a wait); the eligibility's own Try again joins the sites; SID-48 and SID-50 were reworked for it.)* *(Correction, M7B-INVOICES-P2: SID-50 passes on its entry source and is a regression guard; SID-48 fails there. | DA-200 | **A sign-out leaves the session token in the Customer directory page's create hold store.** `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.tsx` (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 to P8), deliberately unchanged in the closing round, does not hear `onSessionEnd`. Its refs (the generation's token, `gen.current.csrf`) go with the page, which a sign-out unmounts (the route guard, `src/components/AccessGuard.tsx`; a DEV-only session excepted) and which another user's page replaces (one page per UID), on a direct switch only once that user's profile has loaded. Its edit hold store is bound to the sign-in state once a directory page has mounted (`bindEditHoldsToAuth`, `keepOnly`), so a sign-out or another user drops every other user's held edit and edit outcome with their tokens. Its module-level create hold store (`holdStore`) keeps the token after a sign-out in two places: on a held create (`HeldCreate.csrf`, while it is sending, unresolved or checking), until that user's directory page sees another token (the create is detached: the token cleared and a body carrying a contact emptied), the create ends (confirmed, refused, or Stop tracking) or the page reloads; and on a "saved" outcome (`HoldOutcome` of kind `saved`, a validated 201 that arrived after its page had gone), until that user's page takes it once it holds a session token, a newer outcome of that user replaces it, or the page reloads. Another user's page never touches either. It is in memory only, never in browser storage, a URL, the DOM or a log, and a successful logout makes the server refuse the token. Not exercised in a real browser **Closed in M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2 (commit `68bc927`) for the create hold store:** the store gains `detachAll()` and is bound to `onSessionEnd` at its module's load (`bindHoldsToSessionEnd`, released on a hot reload's dispose), so it hears every sign-out whether or not a directory page is mounted. At the start of every logout and reset, every user's held create loses its token and its whole body (a name-only body too) and is marked ended, and every recorded outcome loses its token. The key, state, send time and name stay, only so a late answer settles its attempt by key and a 201 is validated: a late 201, refusal or undecided answer settles in the store and is said, in a later session of the same user, only as an unnamed notice (a customer sent before the server session ended), with no read-back, list or detail read, wait, hold note or resend, and the attempt never moves to the new session. A mounted page listens too: a new generation with no token, its reads (a Reconnect's included) cancelled, a wait ended, the form's name, phone and email, the hold note and the create notices cleared, and the ended state shown. Add, Check again, the list and detail reads, the recheck's adoption and the focus check take nothing from an answer asked for before a sign-out began (`sessionEndsSoFar`); Check again keeps only the key across its session check, then re-reads the hold and sends the same key and bytes once only if it is not ended, holds the token just confirmed and a body, and is inside 23 hours. An ended session a read finds detaches the hold the same way. Evidence: jsdom `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` SCD-166 to SCD-176 (each fails on the entry source and passes), SCD-25, SCD-26 and SCD-75 and the browser scenarios SCB-06 and SCB-09 changed for the observed end's detach, and `src/tenant/tenantSessionClient.test.ts` TSC-33 (the client's header names the directory as a listener); in Chromium, SSE-01 (it fails on the entry source): an owner's create with a phone and an email whose answer is lost, then a sign-out in the same document, after which the page's JavaScript heap holds neither the session CSRF token nor the exact body sent; signed in again the attempt is detached and unnamed, with no Check again, through a navigation away and back; a second create's 201 released after the sign-out starts no read or send and is said later without its name; two POSTs in the case. Evidence limits: this clears the application's own holders; it is no heap erasure, and bytes already sent in a request in flight are not recalled. SSE-01 asserts the token and the exact body absent from the heap only: the phone and email strings stay reachable through detached inputs that a handle of the test harness keeps, and no erasure of the contact is claimed. The session-end broadcast names no user and is published only by this browser's own logout or reset, so a remote expiry or revocation is learned only when a read or a check asks (`ensure`). The path where a session check answers just before a sign-out begins is proven in jsdom only (SCD-169): in a real browser the client's epoch already discards an answer held at the network. The edit hold store still follows the sign-in state (`bindEditHoldsToAuth`), not `onSessionEnd`: a sign-out or another user drops every other user's held edit, and a held edit of the same user keeps its token until a mounted page of that user hears a session end, sees another token or an ended session, or the page reloads **Closed in M7B-INVOICES-P2 (commit `dc48862`) for the edit hold store:** it now hears `onSessionEnd` from its module's load (`bindEditHoldsToSessionEnd` in `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.tsx`), mounted page or not, and the Firebase sign-in binding (`bindEditHoldsToAuth`) is removed. A sign-out — which also stands for a user change, because the shop logs the previous user out first — drops every user's held edit and every recorded edit outcome whole: target, body with phone and email, expected version, display name, key and session token. Only a per-user mark is kept, for one unnamed "could not be confirmed" notice on the page that heard the end or on that user's next generation (never another user's page; consumed once). A late answer for a dropped edit finds nothing to settle (no name, read-back, resend or login). Save and Check again note `sessionEndsSoFar()` before their session check and send nothing once a sign-out began; Check again keeps only the key across that check. A page's user change still runs `keepOnly`. Evidence kinds, kept apart: in jsdom, SCD-178, SCD-181 (its two "page gone, answered before the sign-out" variants), SCD-182, SCD-183, SCD-184 and SCD-185 fail on the entry source and pass; SCD-114 and SCD-177 are new API contracts (the export did not exist); SCD-179, SCD-180, SCD-186 and SCD-181's other six variants pass on entry and are regression guards, as is SCD-139 (it failed in the full-file entry run on a missing element and passed in an isolated probe run). In Chromium, SSE-04 is a regression guard (it passes on the entry source too), with a positive heap control before the sign-out: no case in a real browser discriminates the fix. Limits: no erasure is claimed (bytes already sent, closures of requests still out until they settle or time out, engine copies and objects a test harness keeps); a mounted page's drop of its form, draft and Cancel-closed profile is stated as released, not asserted; the per-user marks stay until consumed or the page reloads; the session-end broadcast is still local only (a remote expiry is learned at the next check); in development only, a provider remount that switches users without a logout leaves a held edit until a page mounts, and a hot-reload disposal orphans the old store. | M7B-INVOICES-P1 (closing round) | Closed (M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2 for the create hold store; M7B-INVOICES-P2 for the edit hold store) |

A 403 gets the reads' one fresh session check: the same token makes it a refusal, said as an alert while it stands, and another token starts a new generation; a 401 ends the session as any read's does; a 429 starts the section's wait, which holds New, Try again and Save back and is said once. The applied path re-decides Customers on its role row held `FOR SHARE`. 028 had the same. This is shown by reading 031 (its header, "WHAT THIS DOES NOT DEFEND"); no test reproduces it. The save's existence signal is DA-189. Closing it would need every write of a Customers level to meet a guard that the save holds before it answers `customer_not_found`, as 030 did for Invoices, in a later forward migration, or an owner decision that the residual is accepted. **Closed in M7B-INVOICES-P1-R4 (commit `cdd1ff1`) for the runtime save while migration 032's trigger `trg_tenant_role_level_customers_guard` is enabled (DA-210's closure of the Invoices level still needs 030's trigger). Preserving the existing Customers requirement through its deciding transaction needed no owner-policy decision: the requirement (Invoices >= Create and Customers >= View), the catalog defaults and store_owner's fixed Full are unchanged.** Reproduced on the entry source `30828fc` (two tenant entries over one disposable database, the create and the owner's change by different actors): a create paused at its customer read, after its unlocked step-8 decision and before any key; the owner's PUT narrowing the role's Customers level to None committed; released, the create answered the sealed 422 `customer_not_found` and its completion committed, with no draft, line, audit record or event (`tests/db/invoiceDraftCreateAuthorityHttp.integration.test.mjs` CCA-01a(a), manager, an existing Edit override; CCA-01b(a), sales_staff, the role's first Customers row replacing its default Create; two runs, each failing on that safety assertion). With an existing customer the same ordering was already refused by 031's applied-path re-decision (the (b) variants: the 503, the lease kept). The adapter now calls migration 032's `tmpos_invoice_edit.m7b_invoice_draft_create_v3`, which takes 030's guard key (530030, the same tenant and role hash) SHARED for manager, technician and sales_staff after the customer read, reads the database clock and both the Invoices and the Customers level again under it by plain SELECTs, and decides both sealed answers (`customer_not_found` and `applied`) with the key held to COMMIT; 031's Customers NULL anchor and `FOR SHARE` are gone, so the save creates no configuration row and a role without a stored Customers level is decided on its catalog default; store_owner takes no key and no read. Every write of a Customers level takes the key EXCLUSIVE through 032's trigger: a first effective override, an update or reset, a change to or from NULL, a delete of a non-NULL row, and a key or domain move (the complete old/new key set, ascending, before 030's trigger); the INSERT or DELETE of a NULL anchor takes nothing. A Customers narrowing committed after the save's unlocked checks and before the key is granted refuses the save with the existing transaction-time 503 (nothing sealed, the lease kept, no completion, draft, line, audit record, event or role row); one committed earlier is refused by those earlier checks (the authorizer's 403, or the revalidation's or step 8's `refused`, the generic 503); one attempted while the save holds the key waits for its COMMIT or rollback (evidence: `tests/db/invoiceDraftCreateAuthorityHttp.integration.test.mjs` CCA-01a(a) and CCA-01b(a), each red on `30828fc` in two runs, with CCA-02, CCA-03a, CCA-03b, CCA-09 and CCA-10; `tests/db/invoiceDraftCreateAuthority.integration.test.mjs` CCA-04 to CCA-08, CCA-11, CCA-12 and two negative controls). Scope: the runtime path through the save's adapter. Outside it: Owner DDL on the level table (`DISABLE TRIGGER` of 032's or 030's trigger, an altered or replaced trigger function, `DROP`); the owner's direct call of 031's `create_v2` or 028's create, neither of which decides the Customers level under the key (`create_v2`, which takes the key and then its Customers anchor `FOR SHARE`, can now deadlock with a Customers setter, 40P01); a call of the save routine outside the runtime's command transaction, and a direct `tmpos_app` caller of 021's setter that holds its transaction on the key past its session's expiry (no recent sign-in re-check, G-AUDIT); a table-owner transaction that writes several (tenant, role) pairs, or both domains of one pair in separate statements, or locks a customer and then writes a level, and a direct `tmpos_app` transaction that holds the key SHARED and then runs the customer create or 024's customer edit, each of which can deadlock (40P01, availability, no wrong authority answer; DA-120); any login holding the key, and two pairs whose hashes collide (availability); any database where 032 is not applied, every managed database included; a rollback of 032, which restores this window; and customer editing: 024's customer edit is frozen and unchanged (its Customers NULL anchor and `FOR SHARE`, no key), and DA-205's status is unchanged | M7B-INVOICES-P1-R3 | Closed (M7B-INVOICES-P1-R4, for the runtime save while 032's trigger is enabled; the items listed as outside stay open) |
| DA-212 | **The shop holds a discard's unknown outcome only while its confirmation stays open.** In `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceDrafts.tsx` (commit `6048bfb`) a discard's attempt — its key, exact body bytes, store, draft, expected version and generation — lives in memory with the confirmation dialog, not in a hold store, browser storage, a URL or a log (owner decision D-DIS-5, [ADR-49](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)). While the attempt is unresolved (a 503, no answer, or 409 `request_in_progress`), "Discard draft" is gone from the dialog, so it never mints a second key, and "Check again" re-sends the same key and identical bytes only when pressed, in the same generation under the same session token, never during a wait, and within 23 hours (the server keeps a key for 24). Closing the confirmation only stops tracking the request, which its Close control says first ("Closing stops tracking this discard; the server may have discarded the draft anyway."); it also closes the reopen dialog and reads page one again (during a rate limit's wait the list stays, marked last known, with Try again), and it says neither "cancelled" nor "discarded". A sign-out, a session end, a user change, a store change and a new generation drop the attempt and both dialogs at once; a reload ends tracking too. After that "Check again" is no longer offered, and an answer that arrives finds no attempt and changes nothing, although the POST may have committed; a draft missing from a later read, or a later 404, does not show that this attempt was the one that discarded it. A new discard of the same draft needs a fresh eligibility answer, the version of a fresh reopen read and a new key; if the earlier send landed, it is answered the sealed 409 `invalid_state`, so a discard is never applied twice. Evidence ([11](./11-m7b-invoice-draft-discard-decision-pack.md) §10): DSC-13 — IDC-25, IDC-26; SID-119, SID-125, SID-126, SID-127, SID-128, SID-140, SID-141, SID-142, SID-143, SID-144; SDB-20. DSC-14 — SID-122, SID-124, SID-128, SID-129, SID-130, SID-131, SID-134, SID-136, SID-145; SDB-21. No erasure is claimed: bytes already sent, closures of requests still out and copies the JavaScript engine keeps are not erased. DA-208's wider question, whether an unknown outcome should outlive its dialog or editor, stays open for both | M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1 | Recorded (a recovery limit the owner accepted, D-DIS-5); a later stage may keep the attempt in a hold store if the owner wants it recoverable after the confirmation closes |
| DA-213 | **A data-only restore or replay into a schema at migration 033 meets the terminal guards.** Migration 033's triggers `trg_invoice_draft_terminal` and `trg_invoice_draft_line_terminal` are ENABLE ALWAYS, so they fire for a session running as a replica too: a header may be inserted only as an open draft, and a line may not be written under a header that is not `draft`. A data-only restore or replay, or a logical replication table copy, into a schema already at 033 is therefore refused at its first discarded header, and the line table's load then fails too (its header is not readable) — as migration 006's outbox guard already refuses a non-pending outbox row. Retained drafts load through a full-schema restore (the triggers are created after the data), or with both triggers disabled by the table owner for the load and each re-enabled explicitly as ENABLE ALWAYS: a restore's own re-enable is a plain ENABLE, which fires for origin sessions only, and the startup preflight does not read a trigger's enabled state. Otherwise the guards bind every row write by every role while they are enabled; TRUNCATE, an owner's `DISABLE TRIGGER` or `DROP TRIGGER`, a replaced function and a superuser are outside them (033's header). Recorded from 033's header and the stage's review; no restore was rehearsed *(M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-P1, [ADR-50](./10-architecture-decision-records.md): migration 034 adds ENABLE ALWAYS guards on its four tables — issued headers and lines refuse every UPDATE, DELETE and TRUNCATE, a header insert requires its source draft still `draft` at the reviewed version and its number equal to the counter's value plus one, a counter update moves it by exactly one, and a deferred check runs at COMMIT — and rebinds the header terminal trigger, still ENABLE ALWAYS, to admit `draft` → `issued` only while the draft's issued invoice exists. A data-only restore or replay into a schema at 034 meets them as it meets 033's: an issued header, for one, is refused under a source draft that is already `issued`. Recorded from 034 and the stage's review; no restore was rehearsed.)* | M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1 | Input to G-BACKUP, which stays OPEN: a restore rehearsal must load retained discarded drafts and their lines and leave both triggers ENABLE ALWAYS; since M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-P1 it must also load issued invoices, their lines, issuing settings and number counters and leave 034's guards ENABLE ALWAYS |

### Authorization findings carried from M5-GAP11-P1-R1 (recorded M5-GAP11-P2; not fixed)

Found by the M5-GAP11-P1-R1 review, outside that stage's scope, and recorded here once. None is fixed, and none blocks the D2 candidate. Each needs an owner decision on whether it must be fixed before the GAP-11 cutover or can be carried to the pre-launch review. *(Retagged M5-GIAM-D1-P3: that cutover was superseded when M5-GAP11-P5 closed GAP-11 at repository level. "Pre-cutover" below now means before the first tenant-plane production route, owner decision D1 under G-IAM.)*

| # | Finding | Where | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| AZ-1 | Supervisor refund authorization accepts one hard-coded PIN for every role, and the supervisor it records is a role, not an identified person. As recorded in M5-GAP11-P5, an approval was also kept until the refund completed or was cancelled, was not re-checked when the owner revoked the grant or the POS operator changed, and the POS refund path did not consult the read-only write block. **Resolved in M5-GAP11-P5-R1:** the POS decides a refund again from current state when it executes it (the active operator, that operator's store role, the Refunds level, `approve_refunds`, the plan, the tenant and read-only mode — the write block, or a `read_only` or `suspended` tenant). A supervisor approval covers one refund request and is bound to the signed-in user, the active operator and that operator's role, the tenant and plan, and the supervisor role configuration it was decided on, so an operator change, a revoked grant or an edited Refunds level voids it, and changing it back (re-granting included) does not revive it. Read-only mode denies both the approval and the refund, and the operator's own path needs the explicit `approve_refunds` grant, not only Process Refunds. **Still open:** the shared hard-coded PIN; a per-person supervisor credential; approvals live in memory only, with no durable or audited approval record; every check is client-side, because no server refund command route exists. | `requestSupervisorRefundAuth` and `canExecutePosRefund`, `src/context/AccessContext.tsx`; `decidePosRefundExecution`, `src/context/tenantAccessDecisions.ts`; `src/components/POS.tsx` | Partly resolved (M5-GAP11-P5-R1); open for the PIN, a durable approval record and server enforcement — decision required: pre-cutover or pre-launch |

ADR-05 moves them to server storage. | `src/owner/platformPermissionsConfig.ts` | Open — decision required: pre-cutover or pre-launch |

### G-DBROLE — the live role-topology check for the transactional store (recorded in M6-PG-P5; not run)

Migration 006 proves only the direct object grants on its own objects, and only when it runs. Before the adapter is bound, the owner-authorized G-DBROLE step must show — read-only, against the real database and the real runtime LOGIN (`<login>` below) — that effective reach still matches those grants. Run every query in a session that first executes `set search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, so no operator or function planted in another schema answers in place of pg_catalog's. Each query must return exactly the stated result; anything else stops the binding.

1. **The login.** `select rolsuper, rolinherit, rolcreaterole, rolcreatedb, rolreplication, rolbypassrls, rolcanlogin from pg_catalog.pg_roles where rolname = '<login>'` → `f, t, f, f, f, f, t`.
2. **Its memberships.** `select m.roleid::regrole::text, m.inherit_option, m.admin_option from pg_catalog.pg_auth_members m where m.member = '<login>'::regrole order by 1` → exactly `tmpos_app` and `tmpos_audit_writer`, each `inherit t, admin f`. Then every role it reaches, at any depth: `select r.rolname, r.rolsuper, r.rolcreaterole, r.rolcreatedb, r.rolreplication, r.rolbypassrls from pg_catalog.pg_roles r where pg_catalog.pg_has_role('<login>', r.oid, 'MEMBER') order by 1` → exactly `<login>`, `tmpos_app` and `tmpos_audit_writer`, every attribute `f`. (A store role granted, say, `pg_execute_server_program` — shell commands through `COPY … PROGRAM` — would pass every other step.)
3. **No path to an owner or a superuser.** `select count(*) from pg_catalog.pg_roles r where (r.rolsuper or r.rolbypassrls or r.oid in (select nspowner from pg_catalog.pg_namespace where nspname in ('tmpos_internal', 'public') union select relowner from pg_catalog.pg_class where relnamespace = 'tmpos_internal'::regnamespace or oid = 'public.audit_event'::regclass union select proowner from pg_catalog.pg_proc where pronamespace = 'tmpos_internal'::regnamespace or (pronamespace = 'public'::regnamespace and proname in ('reject_audit_event_mutation', 'audit_metadata_is_flat')))) and pg_catalog.pg_has_role('<login>', r.oid, 'MEMBER')` → `0`. (`MEMBER` counts every path: inherited, `SET`-only and nested. A member of an owner could replace the guard, the clock or the audit functions, or `ALTER` a table.)
4. **Who else reaches the store roles.** `select r.rolname from pg_catalog.pg_roles r where r.oid not in ('tmpos_app'::regrole, 'tmpos_audit_writer'::regrole) and not r.rolsuper and (pg_catalog.pg_has_role(r.oid, 'tmpos_app'::regrole, 'MEMBER') or pg_catalog.pg_has_role(r.oid, 'tmpos_audit_writer'::regrole, 'MEMBER')) order by 1` → only the runtime LOGIN(s) the owner approved, plus at most the principal that created the two roles. For that creator, `select m.roleid::regrole::text, m.admin_option, m.inherit_option, m.set_option from pg_catalog.pg_auth_members m where m.member = '<creator>'::regrole and m.roleid in ('tmpos_app'::regrole, 'tmpos_audit_writer'::regrole) order by 1` → each row `admin t, inherit f, set f`, the `ADMIN`-only membership PostgreSQL 16 gives a non-superuser creator while `createrole_self_grant` is empty (the default). `ADMIN` still lets it grant itself `INHERIT` or `SET` at any time, so the creator is held to an owner's standard: the principal that applies migrations, never an application login, and unreachable from `<login>` (step 2).
5. **Effective reach, for every role.** For each `r` in `pg_catalog.pg_roles` and for `public`, read `has_schema_privilege` on `tmpos_internal` (`USAGE`, `CREATE`) and on `public` (`CREATE`); `has_table_privilege` on each of the two tables (`SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE`, `REFERENCES`, `TRIGGER`) and `has_column_privilege(r, table, column, p)` for every column and each of `SELECT`, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`; `has_function_privilege` (`EXECUTE`) on every function in `tmpos_internal` — the nine lifecycle routines, `m6_store_clock()` and `outbox_event_transition_guard()`; and `has_table_privilege` on `public.audit_event` (`INSERT`, `SELECT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE`). → `<login>` holds exactly `USAGE` on `tmpos_internal`, `EXECUTE` on the nine routines and `INSERT` on `public.audit_event`, and nothing else — no table or column privilege and no `EXECUTE` on the clock or the guard (since M6-PG-P6); `tmpos_app` holds the same without that `INSERT`, `tmpos_audit_writer` only that `INSERT`, and any other login step 4 approved no more than `<login>`. The built-in roles `pg_read_all_data` and `pg_write_all_data` report, by design, `USAGE` on `tmpos_internal` and `SELECT` — respectively `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` — on every table and column here, so only superusers may be their members: `select r.rolname from pg_catalog.pg_roles r where not r.rolsuper and r.oid not in ('pg_read_all_data'::regrole, 'pg_write_all_data'::regrole) and (pg_catalog.pg_has_role(r.oid, 'pg_read_all_data'::regrole, 'MEMBER') or pg_catalog.pg_has_role(r.oid, 'pg_write_all_data'::regrole, 'MEMBER'))` → no row. No other role holds any of them, except the owners and superusers (step 3's concern), and no role but an owner or a superuser holds `CREATE` on `public` or `tmpos_internal`.
6. **Section 6, re-run live.** Migration 006's three section-6 selects, read-only (the `with objects … aclexplode …` grants, the functions' owner, definer attribute and pinned path, and the project roles' membership in the owner) → no row each: nothing has been granted, redefined or joined to the owner since the apply.
7. **The guard and row security are in force.** `select tgenabled from pg_catalog.pg_trigger where tgrelid = 'tmpos_internal.outbox_event'::regclass and tgname = 'outbox_event_transition_guard'` → `A`; both tables have `relrowsecurity = t`; `pg_policies` for `tmpos_internal` → no row (since M6-PG-P6 no role but the owner, through the routines, reaches a row).
8. **The login cannot switch the guard off.** `select pg_catalog.has_parameter_privilege('<login>', 'session_replication_role', 'SET')` → `f`. (The trigger is `ENABLE ALWAYS`, so replica mode would not skip it anyway; this checks that the right was not granted.) Step 3 already shows it owns no table it could `ALTER`.

Why steps 2 to 5 read membership and effective reach, not grants alone, is proved against disposable PostgreSQL (M6-PG-20): the `has_*_privilege` functions follow inherited membership (two levels deep) but report `false` for a membership granted `WITH INHERIT FALSE` that `SET ROLE` still reaches; they report `true` for any member of the owner and for a superuser; and a direct table or function grant without USAGE on the schema reaches nothing.

### Runtime database-endpoint classification (recorded M6-PG-P7-R1; persisted M5-ID-P1)

Four clarifications about `classifyRuntimeDatabaseUrl` (`server/platform-identity/databaseEndpoint.ts`), the gate that decides whether an `APP_DATABASE_URL` may be used at all. They record behaviour that already exists; none changes it.

1. **Precedence, in order.** A missing value; a space or control character; an unparseable URL, then a wrong protocol; the host, then the port; a routing (`host`, `hostaddr`, `port`) or pooling (`pgbouncer`, `pool_mode`) query parameter, which is `app_database_endpoint_unsupported`; any *other* `?` or `#`, which is `app_database_url_invalid`; the authority's spelling; the credentials and database name; a pooler username missing its project reference; and last, a username and host naming different projects. So an endpoint refused as unsupported is never also reported as invalid, and the pooling refusal is reached before the generic query-parameter one.
2. **Identical to the migration executor's managed-DSN validator:** the postgres scheme; the direct and pooler host patterns, and the refusal of any other `db.` host; port 5432 only; the `pgbouncer` and `pool_mode` rules; the role pattern; the refusal of a username/host project mismatch; and a required password.
3. **Stricter at runtime than the executor:** the value is judged as written, so any whitespace or control character is refused; `hostaddr` and `port` are refused as well as `host`; *every* occurrence of a pooling parameter is checked, not just the first; any other query parameter or fragment is refused outright; the authority must be spelled exactly (`:05432`, an empty `:`, or two `@` are refused); the database name must have a valid shape; and a NUL in the password is refused. The executor alone additionally checks agreement with the project URL and the expected database name — a managed-target concern the runtime has no equivalent of.
4. **Composition is route-ready; the deployable entry is routeless (until M6-ENTRY-P1).** `server/composition/productionTransactions.ts` classifies the endpoint before anything is built, but its production inventory holds no route, so nothing is read, classified or connected today; the deployable entry (`server/runtime/server.ts`) serves the probes and the bounded fallback only. *Since M6-ENTRY-P1, `server/runtime/server.ts` is deleted and the deployable entry (`server/entry/productionEntry.ts`) serves exactly the approved inventory — the two probes and the six administrative routes, then the bounded 404 — and the transaction root classifies `APP_DATABASE_URL` for those routes before anything is built; in code only, not deployed.* Separately, the unclassified `getRuntimeDb()` client — which skips this classifier — has exactly three callers, all DEV-only and none reachable from a deployable entry: `db.ts` (defines it), `authorizationRepository.ts` (the DEV control plane) and `platform-identity/server.ts` (the DEV sidecar's readiness probe, which binds a listener only under `npm run identity:api`). The audit writer's former fallback to it was **removed in M5-ID-P1**, and `tests/quality/db-client-containment.test.mjs` now pins that caller set. *Since M6-ENTRY-P1, `db.ts` is in the entry's graph (the entry imports it), so of those three it alone is now reachable from a deployable entry, and its two DEV client factories, `getDb()` and `getRuntimeDb()`, ship uncalled (DA-58).*

### The distributed rate-limit store — operating limits (recorded M6-RATE-P2; [ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md))

What migration 014 and `server/persistence/postgresRateLimiter.ts` do in operation, and what a deployment must still provide. None of it is live: no deployable entry composes the store *(until M6-ENTRY-P1; since then the deployable entry, `server/entry/productionEntry.ts`, composes it through the transaction root in code only, not deployed)*.

1. **Failure.** Any outage, timeout (lock 200 ms, statement 300 ms, idle-in-transaction 400 ms, and the 500 ms limiter cap), abort, malformed answer or key mismatch refuses the request with a bounded 503, logged as `rate_limit_unavailable` or `rate_limit_timeout`, and never allows it. The adapter answers at its 500 ms bound even with COMMIT in flight; an allowance committed after it is answered unavailable, so its cost is spent and no allowance goes unrecorded. The same conditions make the probe false, and so does a read-only session or a standby (`store_read_only`), so `/readiness` answers 503; `/health` never touches the limiter. There is no local fallback while PostgreSQL is down.
2. **Load.** Every protected request is at least one short transaction on the primary database, which writes whenever the consume is allowed; a login is up to three. A consume the committed state already refuses (a live window whose count plus the cost exceeds the limit) is answered from an unlocked read: no row lock and, once the key fingerprint is registered, no transaction ID and no WAL; a refusal racing the consume that spends the last allowance can still take the locked path. These transactions share the runtime kernel's ten one-connection pools (`RUNTIME_POOL_MAX` in server/platform-identity/db.ts, at the supervised client's `MAX_POOLS` ceiling) with sessions, admission and commands. The adapter serializes consumes per bucket per instance, so one source holds at most one connection per bucket it reaches, however many requests it sends — a source that reaches several buckets can still hold several connections, and many sources can exhaust the pool (DA-52); at most 64 consumes of one bucket wait per instance, the one in flight included (`RATE_LIMIT_LANE_DEPTH`), and past that a consume of that bucket is a 503 at once. A consume aborted before its COMMIT leaves its transaction open on the server until the server sees the retired session end, so the next consume of that bucket can wait on the row and is a 503 past its 200 ms lock bound (DA-52). Many sources can still exhaust the ten connections, which the edge must absorb (DA-52).
3. **Cleanup.** Never inside a consume. After an allowed consume that opened a window, the adapter calls `m6_rate_limit_reclaim()` in the background, in its own transaction under 50 ms lock, statement and idle bounds. Each such consume the adapter answers owes one call (one whose window opened only after its caller was answered unavailable owes none); owed calls run one at a time per instance, the next only after one deleted a full four, and one that deletes fewer, or fails, clears what is owed. Each call deletes at most four expired buckets, oldest first, through the expiry index, skipping locked rows, each by its primary key; its failure changes no consume. Its lock hold is bounded far below a consume's 200 ms lock wait, so a consume of an expired bucket being reclaimed still succeeds. The probe never cleans, and there is no timer or worker. So after a burst each window opened is matched by up to four reclaimed while a backlog remains and reclaims succeed; during a burst of new clients windows can open faster than one reclaim at a time clears them, and with no traffic the backlog stays. Under a long-held database snapshot (a long transaction, a replication slot, `pg_dump`), vacuum cannot clear the dead entries that window reopens (non-HOT updates) and reclaims leave at the low end of the expiry index; with about 100,000 of them (a one-off check on a throwaway local PostgreSQL 16 cluster, not part of the committed tests) both reclaims tried failed at the 50 ms statement bound (SQLSTATE 57014) until the snapshot ended. Storage grows meanwhile; consumes, which use the primary key, are unaffected. Monitor reclaim failures with SQLSTATE 57014, long transactions and the xmin horizon's age, and the table's bloat (DA-52).
4. **The key.** `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` must be identical on every instance. The first consume or probe registers its fingerprint in the database; an instance with any other key answers every protected request 503 and fails readiness. If a wrongly keyed instance calls first after 014 is applied, every correctly keyed instance is locked out that way. A holder of the runtime database credential can do the same on purpose without the key, by calling the consume or the probe first with a fingerprint of its choosing between 014's apply (or a re-apply after rollback) and the first correctly keyed call; that locks every instance out, fails closed and grants no allowance (DA-53). Runbook step: right after applying 014, have a correctly keyed instance call (its readiness probe registers the key) before anything else holds the `tmpos_app` credential, or have the owner confirm that the registered fingerprint equals HMAC-SHA256 keyed with the bytes `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` decodes to (not its text) over the ASCII string `tmpos-rate-limit-key-fingerprint:v1`, as `rateLimitKeyFingerprint` computes it. Recovery is an operator procedure: stop the wrongly keyed instances, confirm the intended key's fingerprint, delete the one `key_fingerprint` row as the owner in a maintenance window, and let a correctly keyed instance register. No routine does it, and nothing does it automatically. Rotation needs a coordinated procedure that does not exist (DA-53). 014's rollback also removes the fingerprint, drops every bucket with it, refuses first unless its transaction is READ COMMITTED (at a stricter level an earlier statement's snapshot could hide a window opened while its lock was awaited), takes its locks before its check, and refuses while any window is live.
5. **Trusted proxies (deployment work, unchanged by this stage).** `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` must list exactly the real edge's egress ranges, covering every hop and nothing wider. The edge must append to or overwrite `X-Forwarded-For` and be the runtime's only way in, and must present IPv4 clients as IPv4 or IPv4-mapped addresses (see G-IDEMPOT on NAT64). No real edge or proxy has been exercised, so that contract is unproved (G-UNAUTH, DA-9).
6. **Measured local limits.** A bounded concurrency check on a local disposable PostgreSQL 16.10 cluster over a Unix socket, run twice: with durable commits (`fsync` and `synchronous_commit` on, as CI's stock server) and with `fsync` off. It is not a production throughput claim: it says nothing about a managed database, network latency, pool contention with real sessions and commands, or failover.
   - Concurrency: 2 limiter instances on 2 kernels of 6 connections each, so 12 transactions in flight (32 concurrent callers).
   - Consumes: 2,000 — 600 on one hot bucket (limit 500) and 1,400 over 400 cold keys (limit 3).
   - Duration: durable commits 2,536 ms (789 consumes/s), latency p50 6.96 / p95 159.48 / p99 171.64 / max 193.59 ms; `fsync` off 1,352 ms (1,480/s), p50 4.81 / p95 75.82 / p99 89.45 / max 101.34 ms.
   - Errors and timeouts: in both runs 1,340 allowed, 660 limited, 0 unavailable, 0 database errors, 0 faults; background reclaims 162 (durable) and 95 started, at most 1 in flight per instance, 0 failed.
   - Exact accounting: held in both runs — 0 buckets over-allowed, 0 under-counted. One bucket, 120 concurrent consumes over 4 instances with limit 40 under durable commits: exactly 40 allowed, 80 limited, 0 unavailable, in 211–277 ms across runs.
   - One hot bucket's ceiling: an earlier durable run of 200 concurrent consumes of one bucket (limit 100, before refusals became lock-free) lost 76 to the 500 ms deadline. With durable commits one bucket grants on the order of a hundred allowances per 500 ms, since each allowance holds the row through its WAL flush.

## Summary

- **Total gates:** 37. **BLOCKER:** 21 · **HIGH:** 14 · **MED:** 2. — unchanged since M1. **Lockfile portability is a mandatory subcriterion of G-APPSEC, not a new gate:** G-APPSEC already owns the dependency (SCA) audit, and that audit reads `package-lock.json`, so lock fidelity is a precondition of G-APPSEC's own pass rule rather than a separate risk. No existing gate had claimed lock integrity, clean installation, or supply-chain reproducibility, and none needed to be stretched to accept it. The pre-install checker is enforced regardless of this accounting; only the governance ownership is consolidated. Its non-waivability is a **rule**, not an assertion — see the non-waivable carve-out below.
- **Current state:** all `OPEN` at M1 (M1 is planning; it opens the register, it does not close gates). M2 moves G-EMU, G-APPSEC and G-COVERAGE to `PARTIAL`. **Phase 4.0 M3 (Shipping sidecar elimination) additionally moves G-SSRF and G-SECRETS to `PARTIAL`** — each had a distinct M3 half (eliminate the request-controlled fetch; remove the in-memory unauthenticated credential store) that is now done with regression evidence, while the remainder (M7e/M8 egress boundary; M6/M8 encrypted rotatable secrets) stays open. **Phase 4.0 M3 (shared enforced runtime middleware) moves G-UNAUTH to `PARTIAL`**: the Shipping and identity routes are eliminated and the M3 runtime admits requests only through one shared enforced chain. **The M3 body/media-type policy and the M4 login/session foundation (both committed in `dd89ff9`) keep G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL` and leave G-CPLOGIN `OPEN`:** the body policy is delivered, and the runtime now carries both session boundaries, session-bound CSRF, login limits and the Firebase adapter adapted to the authenticator's port shape (composed only by the provider-aware composition root, which refuses) — but the deployable artifact composes no authenticator, admission source or session store, so production wiring (a deployable build of the composition root, a durable session store, a production admin admission adapter — G-DBROLE — and tenant/store admission — migration 005 + M5), a trusted-proxy contract and HSTS, distributed rate limits (M6), step-up and admin-hostname provisioning, the legacy DEV identity/BCP app and the deployed-endpoint pass rule (M5/M8) stay open. **M4 session-lifecycle hardening (committed in `dd89ff9`) keeps G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL` and G-CPLOGIN `OPEN`:** the provider-aware composition root, bounded port calls, admission revalidation, per-principal revocation, admin MFA and recent authentication, and host-distinct origins are delivered in code, but production login is not active — the composition root refuses until a durable session store and the admission and authorizer adapters exist (G-DBROLE; migration 005 unexecuted; M5). **M4-ADMIN-UI-P1 (committed in `dd89ff9`) keeps G-CPLOGIN `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`:** the admin sign-in and read-only control-panel shell are delivered in the browser, but production login is not active and no endpoint was added; the concurrent admission-revalidation requirement (G-CPLOGIN row) now blocks every sensitive admin action and production session activation. **M4-ADMIN-UI-P2 (committed in `dd89ff9`) moves G-WEBHARDEN to `PARTIAL` (admin half):** the admin header policy, the listener and the routing boundary exist in code, but no admin-host deployment exists and the tenant SPA is M8. It keeps G-CPLOGIN `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL` — it adds no unauthenticated route, and the read-only Command Center route is session-authenticated, authorized and composed nowhere — and leaves G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN`, with migration 005 unexecuted and not modified by this stage. **M6-RATE-P1 (committed in `dd89ff9`) keeps G-IDEMPOT `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`:** the trusted-proxy contract and the distributed limiter port, its conformance suite and the runtime integration exist in code, but no shared store is approved or composed, the deployment half of the proxy contract is not done, and durable idempotency is not started. **M6-IDEMPOT-P2 (`a6469f23`) and M6-OUTBOX-P3 keep G-IDEMPOT and G-AUDIT `OPEN`:** the idempotency, command-transaction and outbox contracts exist in code with their conformance suites, but no adapter, schema migration or worker exists and nothing is composed. **M6-PG-P4 keeps G-IDEMPOT, G-AUDIT, G-MIGRATE and G-DBROLE `OPEN`:** the PostgreSQL adapter and migration 006 exist and pass the three conformance suites against disposable PostgreSQL, but nothing is composed, 006 is applied to no managed database, and the managed apply of 005 refuses while 006 is pending. **M6-PG-P5 and M6-PG-P6 keep them `OPEN`:** they harden the adapter and close DA-16 through DA-20 — the runtime role reaches the store only through 006's fixed lifecycle routines, and every store transaction runs through the application's own transaction kernel — but nothing is composed, 006 is applied to no managed database, and no runtime LOGIN exists. M4's production blockers (the G-CPLOGIN admission race, G-DBROLE, migration 005) are separate and unchanged. **M5-ID-P1 keeps G-DBROLE, G-CPLOGIN, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT and G-MIGRATE `OPEN`, and advances a named half of three of them.** Migration 007 creates its own schema, `tmpos_identity`, holding three routines (corrected in M5-GAP11-P1-R1: an earlier revision said 006's `tmpos_internal`) — no table, no column, no role, no second credential — through which the runtime resolves a **provider-verified** reference to the app-owned `internal_user_id`, its status, a security version and its usable membership grants, and re-reads that authority inside a committing transaction. **G-DBROLE:** the identity-read half is closed in code — `tmpos_app` holds no privilege on `platform_identity`, `app_user` or `identity_link` and reaches them only through those `SECURITY DEFINER` routines, so the path a production route will take never needs the OWNER connection that `authorizationRepository.ts` uses today; the credential half (a runtime LOGIN role and its endpoint) is untouched and still blocks. Note precisely what did NOT happen: **no existing caller was converted.** `authorizationRepository.ts` still reads those relations through the OWNER connection, and it is untouched by this stage — it is the DEV control plane, reachable from no deployable entry, and replacing it is later M5 work. What 007 establishes is that the path a production route WILL take never needs that connection, and a containment test now refuses a direct identity read from `server/runtime`, `server/persistence` or `server/composition`. **G-CPLOGIN:** the pre-operation requirement of this register (a sensitive handler re-verifying the principal's security version inside its own write transaction) is delivered for the command path — after the idempotency fence, before any business state, every row read `FOR SHARE` in one fixed order so a racing revocation is deterministic, and a failure commits no mutation, completion, audit record or event; the admission-revalidation race, step-up, the admin hostname and production login are untouched and still block. **G-AUDIT:** actor attribution is delivered for a command carrying a trusted context — its AUDIT row names the app-owned actor, never a caller-supplied value — while full coverage remains M6/M5. An outbox event still names no actor, tenant or store: migration 006 types those columns as keyed digests rather than identifiers, and this stage keys none. Nothing is composed into a deployable entry, migration 007 is applied to **no** managed database (disposable PostgreSQL only, as 005 and 006 are), **no business route exists and none is live**, and tenant- and store-scope permissions stay undecidable until GAP-11's ordering unification and its six mandatory safeguards land ([04 §3](./04-canonical-iam-and-four-user-migration.md)). **Correction (M5-GAP11-P1):** an earlier revision of this bullet said two of those safeguards "are the owner's". Read against the source, only **safeguard #3** is: 04 §3 says the diff "is reviewed and explicitly owner-approved before cutover". **Safeguard #1**, the per-action re-pin, is an *open policy choice* — 04 §3 offers two ways to satisfy it ("require `≥ manage` **or** an explicit per-role grant") and picks neither; it never assigns the choice to the owner. Calling it an owner decision overstated the source document. It is tracked here as decision **D2** because someone must choose, not because 04 §3 says the owner must. **M5-GAP11-P1 keeps G-IAMUNIFY and G-IAM `OPEN`, and delivers the evidence-and-observation half of GAP-11.** It commits the generated before/after effective-grant diff ([evidence/gap11-ordering-flip-grant-diff.md](./evidence/gap11-ordering-flip-grant-diff.md)): 13 of 1,659 canonical tuples change (12 widened, 1 narrowed), every one a tenant domain-threshold row; no named sub-permission and no platform row moves. It adds a dual-read shadow comparator that returns the caller's decision unchanged and is wired into no decision path, and an exhaustive authorization-matrix suite. G-IAMUNIFY's own pass rule ("One ordering; no effective grant silently changed; tests prove parity") is untouched: the tenant engine still ranks `manage < approve`, nothing was re-pinned (safeguard #1, decision D2) and nothing is approved (safeguard #3, decision D3). The diff is the evidence that pass rule needs; it is not the unification. One safeguard-#4 gap is recorded rather than fixed: the shipped comparators (`rankIn` in `permissionCatalog.ts`, `levelIndex` in `permissionDecision.ts`) rank an unknown *required* level as `none`, so every holder clears it. That is unreachable today — no catalog-defined requirement is unknown and route requirements are typed constants — but the cutover must close it. **M5-GAP11-P1-R1 keeps G-IAMUNIFY and G-IAM `OPEN` and closes that safeguard-#4 gap:** every shipped authorization entry point — the catalog's comparators and materializers, the DEV decision spine (`permissionDecision.ts`, `protectedAction.ts`), the durable-state resolver, the M5 route catalog, the BCP pilot guard, and the client engines — now denies an unknown or malformed role, plane, feature, domain, action, required or held level, explicit grant value, status or ambiguous configured authority, each with direct CI-run evidence, and every valid canonical decision is unchanged. It also corrects P1's taxonomy: `approveGated` only ever meant that a tuple's decisive level is `approve`; it is now `requiresApproveLevel`, and a separate D2 classification marks a tuple a money action only where an authoritative document says so — 17 tuples, none of which the unified ordering changes. The twelve widened rows are `approve`-level but `unresolved`, not D2 rows. D1–D4 remain undecided, and GAP-11 stays open at least until safeguards #1 (D2) and #3 (D3) land — the ordering cutover itself, #5's switch of authority and #6's intended-outcome assertions are also still required. **M5-GAP11-P2 and M5-GAP11-P3 keep G-IAMUNIFY and G-IAM `OPEN`:** owner decisions D2 (explicit per-role money-action grants) and D3 (reject all thirteen ordering changes, and conditionally approve the resulting zero-diff artifact) are carried in the non-authoritative candidate only. That candidate now answers exactly as the shipped authority on all 1,659 tuples, but the tenant engine still ranks `manage < approve` and no authority has been switched, so the pass rule stays untouched until a separately authorized cutover; D1 and D4 remain undecided. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED) — no gate was added, removed, downgraded, or closed.** **No gate is `PASS`.** **M5-GAP11-P5 closes GAP-11 and G-IAMUNIFY at repository level ([ADR-19](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)):** the global-ordering cutover is superseded; the store and platform families keep their own orderings behind one family contract, every comparison names its family, and money approval is an explicit editable grant. The D2 table becomes the built-in money-grant defaults, the thirteen D3 pins are retired from active evaluation, and the P2/P3 records stay as history. G-IAMUNIFY is now the only `PASS`, at repository level (exact-commit CI is recorded in the stage report); the gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED). G-IAM stays `OPEN`, D1 and D4 remain undecided, durable role-editor persistence is still open, and AZ-1 to AZ-8 are not remediated. **M5-GAP11-P5-R1 completes the money-action enforcement G-IAMUNIFY's `PASS` rests on:** the POS decides a refund again when it executes it, binds a supervisor approval to one request and to what it was decided on, and refuses both in read-only mode; every existing billing refund and credit entry point consumes `approve_billing_actions` when offered and again in its handler. That resolves the stale-approval and read-only part of AZ-1 and the `approve_billing_actions` part of AZ-6. The rest of AZ-1 (the shared PIN, a durable approval record), the other 16 sub-permissions of AZ-6, and AZ-2 to AZ-5, AZ-7 and AZ-8 stay open. These money controls are client-side only until a server command route exists; durable role and approval storage stay open; G-IAM stays `OPEN`. **M5-GIAM-D1-P1 records owner decision D1 and implements the first server-authorized business command ([ADR-20](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)): a tenant's display name.** `PATCH /admin/v1/tenants/display-name` — the brief's `/api/platform/tenants/:tenantId/display-name` in the form the route conventions call for (platform routes live under the admin boundary's `/admin/v1`, and paths are literal, so the tenant travels in the body) — runs the one enforced chain: framing, the client limit, CSRF and origin, the admin session, then the canonical platform authorizer, which resolves the session's principal to its app-owned actor through migration 007, selects that actor's one platform grant and decides `edit_tenant_profile` (Tenants at Edit or above) from the role's definition, then the bounded body, then the UUID-v4 idempotency key, then the body's closed validation (a refused body is a 400 that records nothing), then one transaction: the lease fence, the actor's revalidation, migration 008's fixed operation (a row lock, the version before the name), the audit record and one `tenant.display_name_changed` v1 event — or none of them. A name already in place is a 200 that writes nothing but the idempotency completion. **G-IAM stays `OPEN`** — one platform command is its first server-enforced slice, not canonical IAM. It advances named halves of three gates: **G-IDEMPOT** (the first business route over the durable store and transaction port, proven against disposable PostgreSQL), **G-AUDIT** (a real business mutation audited with its resolved actor and linked by correlation to its event) and **G-CPLOGIN** (the command-path revalidation now guards a real write). Each stays `OPEN`, and so do G-DBROLE (no runtime LOGIN or endpoint), G-MIGRATE (008 and 009 disposable-only; D4 recorded above, now through 009) and G-UNAUTH. Nothing is composed into a deployable entry, the admin boundary's production session store and admission still do not exist, **no route is live**, and no client binds the command yet: no display-name editor exists, so the UI binding is the next product stage. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-GIAM-D1-P2 adds the second server-authorized command — tenant suspension and reactivation — and closes DA-31 ([ADR-22](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** `PATCH /admin/v1/tenants/status` moves a tenant between `active` and `suspended` and nothing else: a target equal to the current status is an invalid transition (409), never a no-op, and the other four values of 002's status domain are neither reachable nor a state a transition may start from. It requires `change_tenant_status` (tenant_management at Manage or above, sensitive — a permission the canonical catalog already defined and no route had used), a bounded 10-500 scalar operator reason recorded on the durable audit row through its existing reason pair, and a TRUSTED RECENT AUTHENTICATION: the provider's own verified authentication time, bound to the session at login, no older than ten minutes, refused as a bounded `step_up_required` and re-judged inside the committing transaction against the DATABASE's clock. Migration 010 adds `tenant.access_version` — the witness a tenant/store session must be admitted against, moved by exactly one per transition and never back, and READ BY NOTHING yet, since the admission adapter that must report it does not exist (DA-32) — and one fixed SECURITY DEFINER operation; **nothing is revoked, because nothing ever reached those columns**: no role these migrations own holds a privilege on `tenant.status`, and the new column is granted to nobody, which 010's verify proves rather than asserts (direct, inherited and SET-only paths all tested). The runtime gains ONE tenant-state decision (GAP-13) — active continue, read-only deny writes, suspended deny, anything unrecognised deny — which scope selection now applies, so a suspended or unknown-status tenant selects no scope at all; and the tenant boundary re-asks admission on every protected request, so once an admission adapter exists a suspension will have no grace period. **G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-CPLOGIN and GAP-13 all stay `OPEN`:** the tenant/store session boundary is composed nowhere (DA-32), so every session effect is proved in code and against disposable PostgreSQL and exercised by no live session; migration 010 is applied to no managed database (D4 now names the contiguous chain through 010); no route is live; and no client binds either command (DA-34 — the UI binding is the immediate next product stage). **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **Phase 4.0 M3 S2/S2.2 (database principal separation) leaves G-DBROLE `OPEN`, deliberately:** it added the NOLOGIN privilege roles, the tenant/store RLS policies, the least-privilege grant matrix, migration-owned role lifecycle, and a fail-closed effective-privilege gate — but it converted **no** caller, provisioned **no** LOGIN role, and applied to **no** managed database, so the gate's own pass rule (tenant paths actually running under RLS as a non-owner role) is untouched by it. **M5-GIAM-D1-P2-R1 keeps G-DBROLE, G-CPLOGIN, G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-MIGRATE and GAP-13 `OPEN` and closes DA-32.** The tenant boundary now HAS the two adapters it never had — a durable PostgreSQL session store and a PostgreSQL admission that derives, from the actor's whole standing including each tenant's access version, the one opaque witness the boundary binds a session to and re-reads on every protected request — and the production transaction root builds both over the client it already owns. Against disposable PostgreSQL and real local sockets, a suspension refuses the very next request and revokes the session, a login while suspended is refused, and after reactivation the witness differs from the pre-suspension one while the principal's own security version is identical. Three things are stated rather than claimed away: **no production tenant business route exists** (every tenant-scope permission is undecidable until owner decision D1, and the transaction root refuses to start with one), so the proof runs against a witness route defined only in the integration harness and refused in production source and in the artifact by containment tests (DA-39); both session boundaries share one table and one runtime role (DA-37); and a protected request costs two admission reads (DA-38). No route is live, 011 is applied to no managed database, and the admin boundary's own ADMISSION adapter still does not exist — it is bound to the same durable store as the tenant boundary, which is DA-37, not a store of its own. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-GIAM-D1-P3 keeps G-IAM, G-CPLOGIN, G-DBROLE, G-AUDIT and G-MIGRATE `OPEN`, and binds the two platform commands to a client ([ADR-24](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds one platform read, `GET /admin/v1/tenants`, under `view_tenants` with a required trusted context. The request carries no body and no idempotency key, and its query is strict: any other key, a repeat or an encoding is a 400 before any read. The route reads through migration 012's one `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` routine in its own schema `tmpos_directory`, which confirms the named platform grant in the same snapshot as a keyset page ordered by `(created_at, tenant_id)`. The admin console's Tenant Management workspace now lists, shows, renames, suspends and reactivates tenants through the server alone, with no optimistic state and one idempotency key per intent (DA-34). **Recorded deviation:** the stage brief said a schema migration was not expected. 012 exists because `tmpos_app` sees `public.tenant` only under migration 005's tenant/store-scoped row policy, so no platform listing can be a query the runtime runs; the owner was asked and replied "continue". 012 creates no table, no role and no write, is applied to no managed database (disposable PostgreSQL only), and D4 now names the contiguous chain through 012; 005–011 are byte-identical. What stays open is stated rather than claimed away. The tenant plane still has no production route (G-IAM/D1). No step-up flow exists: a `step_up_required` refusal asks the person to sign in again. The store lifecycle stays unexposed because of DA-41, and DA-42 to DA-45 are recorded. Nothing is deployed or composed into a deployable entry, and no route is live. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-GIAM-D1-P4 keeps G-IAM, G-CPLOGIN, G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`, and makes the administrative boundary's admission and authorization durable and production-composed in code ([ADR-25](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Migration 013 adds schema `tmpos_admission`: a per-actor platform authority version that only increases and has no foreign key, moved by four `ENABLE ALWAYS` row triggers on one definer function — on `platform_identity` (anchor columns only), `app_user` and `identity_link` (any column but `updated_at`) and `user_membership` (platform scope only, any column but `updated_at`) — and one `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` admission read, which answers 007's resolution narrowed to platform grants, plus that version, in one snapshot. Its rollback and a re-application each refuse while any administrative session exists, because a login racing the rollback can commit its session after it and the counters restart at zero. It exists because 007's version excludes memberships, its timestamp digest can be reproduced (an exported row re-inserted, a replica-mode write) and does not cover the identity anchor, and a witness built from the grant row returns when the grant is disabled and re-enabled. The transaction root builds the PostgreSQL platform admission over its one kernel, and the sessions root binds the admission and the canonical authorizer together. Handed that bundle, neither `admin_admission_unavailable` nor `admin_authorizer_unavailable` arises. `rate_limit_store_unavailable` remains, so production composition still refuses *(until M6-RATE-P2, below)*, no production entry composes either root, and nothing is deployable. Administrative sessions now re-admit on every protected request (it was a 1-minute window) against a `p1:` witness; a denial revokes the principal's administrative sessions only, a witness change revokes the session presented, an outage revokes nothing, and a restoration of the standing the witness covers revives nothing (a code-defined role definition is outside it: DA-47). Readiness includes the administrative admission's probe; the tenant admission's is still not wired. 013 is applied to no managed database (disposable PostgreSQL only), 005–012 are byte-identical, and D4 now names the contiguous chain through 013. GAP-11 stays closed at repository level. DA-34 and DA-41 to DA-45 are carried unchanged; DA-46 to DA-51 are recorded. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M6-RATE-P2 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-IDEMPOT, G-IAM, G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`, and adds the distributed rate-limit store ([ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Migration 014 adds schema `tmpos_rate_limit`: shared fixed-window buckets keyed by pseudonymous limiter keys, the registered fingerprint of `RATE_LIMIT_KEY`, and consume, probe and reclaim routines, the only routines `tmpos_app` may execute there; it holds no table privilege, and no other runtime-facing role may execute any of them. The PostgreSQL adapter implements the unchanged port, and the unchanged conformance suite passes against it on disposable PostgreSQL. The transaction root builds it over its one kernel, and the sessions root binds it with its own parsed key, so a composition handed that bundle and a valid key no longer names `rate_limit_store_unavailable`. That narrows G-CPLOGIN's rate-limit part and G-IDEMPOT's limiter half, and closes neither: no production entry composes either root (the deployable entry is the next composition task), and nothing is deployable. It fails closed — a store outage or a mismatched key is a 503 and a failing readiness probe, never an allowance — and it adds write load to the primary database (DA-52). `RATE_LIMIT_KEY` can be neither rotated nor, after a wrongly keyed instance registers first, re-registered without an operator procedure (DA-53). The real edge and proxy contract is unproved (G-UNAUTH, DA-9). 014 is applied to no managed database (disposable PostgreSQL only), 001–013 are unchanged, and D4 now names the contiguous chain through 014. DA-46 to DA-51 are carried unchanged, with DA-47 restated explicitly; DA-9 is updated; DA-52 and DA-53 are recorded. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M6-ENTRY-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-IAM, G-AUDIT and G-IDEMPOT `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`, and adds the deployable production entry in code ([02 §2.1](./02-target-production-architecture.md), [ADR-27](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** The entry (`server/entry/productionEntry.ts`) composes the sessions and transaction roots with the administrative boundary alone — no entry composes the tenant boundary — and serves exactly the 8 shipping routes, then the bounded 404; every route but the two probes answers only on the admin host, whose Host the runtime now checks before the limiter (the real edge must route that hostname alone and pass Host through unaltered: DA-9, unproved). It listens only after 10 bounded startup steps, among them a read-only preflight of the runtime login's standing and of the migration ledger (exactly 001–014; nothing is applied, rolled back or repaired). That narrows G-CPLOGIN's deployable-entry part and G-DBROLE's enforced-startup-preflight part, and closes neither: no admin-host deployment exists, the step-up experience (DA-34) and the login/logout audit record are not delivered, and the pre-operation security requirement is not re-judged; the persistent runtime LOGIN and its endpoint, its new direct SELECT on `public.schema_migrations` (which no migration grants) and the owner-side database-ACL provisioning are operator actions not taken. G-AUDIT and G-IDEMPOT gain only the entry's in-code composition of the existing adapters for the two audited administrative mutations. No migration is added (001–014 are unchanged, and D4 still names the chain through 014), nothing is deployed, and only disposable PostgreSQL was contacted. DA-46 to DA-53 are carried unchanged; DA-9 gains a dated clause for the admin Host edge check; DA-54 to DA-58 are recorded. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-ADMIN-STEPUP-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE and G-IDEMPOT `OPEN` and G-UNAUTH `PARTIAL`, and delivers the step-up experience in code ([03 §2a](./03-backend-control-plane-login-session-blueprint.md), [ADR-28](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** The existing admin login route gains an opt-in same-principal reauthentication mode (`X-TMPOS-Reauthenticate: 1`), and the console's Tenant Management page a recent-sign-in dialog that uses it and resumes nothing without a new confirmation, reason and key. Nothing is deployed. DA-59 and DA-60 record the two M6-ENTRY-P1 findings for targeted pre-deployment follow-up; DA-61 and DA-62 record this stage's residuals. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M6-ADMIN-AUDIT-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN and G-AUDIT `OPEN` ([03 §2b](./03-backend-control-plane-login-session-blueprint.md), [ADR-29](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** The administrative boundary's successful login, reauthentication and logout now write their `audit_event` row in the same transaction as the session change, in code only. No route, migration, grant or routine is added. DA-61 is rewritten, DA-35's decision is recorded, and DA-63 to DA-69 are new. Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M5-ADMIN-REVOKE-P1 keeps G-CPLOGIN, G-DBROLE and G-MIGRATE `OPEN` ([ADR-30](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** Every administrative change now takes effect when its transaction commits, except DA-76. The administrative login, the recent sign-in and the tenant-management commands are ordered against it in their own transactions, and migration 015 (one trigger function, three triggers, no grant) makes the changes 009 cannot lock wait. That implements G-CPLOGIN's pre-operation create condition and in-transaction re-verification for the administrative boundary, in code only. Its stamp-update clause is not implemented (updates are conditional on liveness only) and is an open owner decision (DA-77), and the requirement stays unmet for the tenant boundary. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–015, and D4 names the chain through 015. DA-61(b) is closed, DA-63 is resolved as a classification, DA-67 is unchanged, and DA-70 to DA-77 are new. The tenant login and recent sign-in are one `establish` transaction that holds the actor's account, links, grants, tenants and stores and re-reads the standing, and the tenant login, recent sign-in and logout are audited. Migration 016 (one column, one table, two trigger functions, five triggers, two routines, two grants to `tmpos_app`) gives stores an access version, moves tenant and store versions on every status change and a store's on a move to another tenant, refuses any change of a tenant's or a store's key, starts every new store at 1, and counts every tenant- and store-scope grant change and every delete of a tenant or store such a grant names. That implements G-CPLOGIN's create condition for the tenant boundary in code; no entry composes that boundary, and no store route or permission is in production. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–016, and D4 names the chain through 016. DA-41 is closed in code, DA-61(b) is closed for the tenant boundary, DA-65 is delivered for the tenant audience, and DA-78 to DA-82 are new (DA-80 closed in code). Migration 017 adds schema `tmpos_customer`: the minimal customer identity (id, owning tenant, name), which no runtime role may read directly, and one definer read that re-confirms the named tenant grant in the same snapshot as a bounded keyset page; `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE only. The read is unaudited by owner decision. The route is served by no deployment, since no entry composes a tenant host. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–017, and D4 names the chain through 017. DA-39 is updated, and DA-83 to DA-92 are new (DA-89 to DA-91 record pre-existing or adjacent findings).
