DA-83 is contained against the runtime credential alone, not closed. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–018, and D4 names the chain through 018. DA-66, DA-83 and DA-87 are updated, and DA-93 to DA-104 are new (DA-103 records a pre-existing finding). The tenant entry, which composes the key, is the stage's second commit. Nothing is deployed, and deployment is not authorized until the owner decides issuer-key custody and installs the key. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-34](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds the first tenant business command in code, `POST /api/v1/customers`. It runs under the second decidable tenant permission, `create_customers`: the owner's decision for that route, the Customers domain at Create or above, at tenant scope only, denied under `read_only`, with no role default changed. It is the first tenant-scope idempotent route, bound to the authorizer's trusted tenant and actor (contract version 2). It commits the customer, its audit record and the completion in one transaction, which holds the presenting session and the actor's standing and decides the permission again. Migration 019 adds schema `tmpos_customer_write` with one session-bound definer routine, and `tmpos_app` still holds no privilege on the customer table. The tenant entry now requires its own `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY`. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–019, and D4 names the chain through 019. DA-29, DA-83, DA-85, DA-87, DA-92, DA-93, DA-97, DA-101, DA-102 and DA-106 are updated, and DA-110 to DA-117 are new (DA-110 records a pre-existing driver finding). Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-35](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It makes both customer permissions follow an owner's edit, in code: migration 020 stores the Customers level of the three editable built-in roles per tenant, the store owner alone edits it through `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` (`edit_role_levels`, owner-only, with a recent sign-in, a reason, an audit record and tenant-scope idempotency; flagged for owner confirmation), and the authorizer and 020's routines decide `view_customers` and `create_customers` on it, and the revalidator decides the create again in its committing transaction (the read has none). `tmpos_app` no longer executes 019's create or 018's `m7a_customer_page_v2`. The shop app's editor is unchanged and does not reach the server. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–020, and D4 names the chain through 020. It touches G-IAM, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION, narrows DA-85, amends DA-97 and DA-115, and adds DA-118 to DA-124; DA-119 (a direct, unaudited role edit) is a new pre-deployment blocker and G-AUDIT exception. Nothing is deployed. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-36](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It connects the shop app's role screen to the tenant server for the built-in roles' Customers level, in code: the screen signs in through the M5 tenant-session login, reads the four built-in roles' levels through the new owner-only `GET /api/v1/roles/levels` (no recent sign-in; its tenant is the authorizer-selected trusted scope passed to 020's definer read, not bound to the session in the database) and changes `manager`, `technician` and `sales_staff` through R1's PUT, calling a change saved only after an exact 200 and a matching same-tenant read-back. The existing customer list and create, `GET` and `POST /api/v1/customers`, decide on the stored level. Custom roles and other domains stay local-only, and owner-only editing stays flagged for owner confirmation. The tenant entry now serves 9 routes: `/health`, `/readiness`, the session login, current session and logout, `GET` and `POST /api/v1/customers`, and `PUT` and `GET /api/v1/roles/levels`. There is no new migration: 001–020 and the four frozen operator paths (`scripts/supabase-migrate.ts`, `scripts/managed-baseline-launcher.mjs`, `scripts/managed-m005-launcher.mjs`, `server/platform-identity/migrationExecutor.ts`) are byte-identical, and the startup preflight still requires exactly 001–020. The next customer slice is `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-37](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It makes the owner's Customers level edit conditional, in code: `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` asserts the tenant and the per-role version of the read the owner reviewed, and migration 021 (schema `tmpos_role_edit`: a per-row version counter, a one-snapshot read of levels and versions, the conditional edit, and a trigger that advances the version on every update of a level row) judges both inside the committing transaction: the tenant and a preliminary version check unlocked, after the authority locks and before any role-row lock, then the authoritative version check under the role row FOR UPDATE. It writes nothing in either tenant when either fails. The refusal is an unsealed 409 `write_conflict`: no mutation, audit record, event or completion, and the separately committed idempotency lease stays until it expires. `tmpos_app` no longer executes 020's unconditional edit. The shop panel sends each draft's tenant and version, turns a stale draft into an explicit conflict, holds an unresolved attempt as its key plus its exact change until a 200 or a `write_conflict` settles it, and shows a change as saved only on an exact-match read-back. No route is added. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–021, and D4 names the chain through 021. 001–020 and the four frozen operator paths (`scripts/supabase-migrate.ts`, `scripts/managed-baseline-launcher.mjs`, `scripts/managed-m005-launcher.mjs`, `server/platform-identity/migrationExecutor.ts`) are byte-identical to the entry commit. DA-125 is closed for the built-in Customers server-backed editor; custom roles and other domains stay local-only. DA-119, DA-120, DA-121 and DA-122 are amended, and DA-128 to DA-130 are new. The customer detail read, `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}`, is the stage's second part and is not delivered here. **The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).** **M7A-CUSTOMERS-P4 Part 2 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-38](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` to the tenant entry, in code:
- **Permission and path.** The route is under `view_customers`, and it is the route table's one template, admitted by name. *(Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 the table admits exactly two templates by name; the second is `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile`, [ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md).)* *(Since M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 three; the third is `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}`, [ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md).)*
- **Order of checks.** The chain's 401 and 403 come before the id is judged. Then a non-canonical id is a 400. A missing id and another tenant's id get the same status, error code, body shape and log reason, while per-request metadata differs. Their timing is not equalized, and that residual is recorded (DA-135).
- **Response.** The answer is `200 { customer: { id, name } }` only.
- **Database.** Migration 022 adds schema `tmpos_customer_detail` with one session-bound read routine that re-decides the list's authority on one snapshot. The startup preflight now requires exactly 001–022, and D4 names the chain through 022.
- **Location.** The customer create's 201 now carries `Location`, sealed and replayed identically.
- **Tenant entry.** It serves ten routes. The administrative entry serves no detail path.
- **Register.** DA-131 to DA-136 are new. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **CI.** One new step runs `tests/db/customerDetail.integration.test.mjs`.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-39](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It gives the three customer routes their first shop client, in code:
- **The page.** A separate Customer directory page and sidebar entry in the shop app list the server's customers, open one by id and add one by name. Only an id and a name are shown. The Customers screen keeps its records, which are held only in the browser's memory and lost on reload, and gains a banner and a link; nothing is merged, imported, migrated or erased.
- **The session names the tenant.** The page uses the shop's existing tenant session and sends no tenant, store, role or actor. A changed session token clears what the page shows, reads again and detaches an unconfirmed create for good.
- **Reads.** A malformed success is a failure, never an empty list. A read the server refuses (a 401 or 403, or a sign-in it no longer accepts) clears both the list and the customer shown and never falls back to browser data. Any other failed read, a failed "Load more" included, keeps the rows already confirmed only under a "last known" label; a cursor the server no longer accepts reloads once from the first page instead. A missing id, another tenant's id and a malformed id show one not-found text.
- **Create.** One `Idempotency-Key` per submitted draft. Only a validated 201 is shown as saved; an undecided answer is held as unconfirmed and is re-sent only by the user, with the same key and bytes, on the same session. An answer that arrives after its page has gone is kept in memory and shown by the next page that holds a session.
- **No server change.** No route, permission or migration is added, and the tenant entry still serves ten routes.
- **G-PERSIST is not claimed.** These routes store only a customer's name, and nothing has been deployed or run against a managed database.
- **Register.** DA-137 to DA-146 are new, and DA-84 and DA-107 are amended. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **CI.** The quality job's limit is now 30 minutes (it was 20). The browser step, renamed "Shop role levels and customer directory in a real browser against the tenant entry and disposable PostgreSQL (M7A-CUSTOMERS-P3-R2, M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5)", also runs the directory scenarios SCB-01 to SCB-06; its 12-minute step limit is unchanged.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**The gate total and severity mix are unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P5-R1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS and G-ISOLATION `OPEN` ([ADR-40](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It corrects two of P5's recorded defects, in code:
- **Reads bound to the session.** `GET /api/v1/customers` (cursor pages included) and `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}` now require `X-TMPOS-Session-CSRF` equal to the presenting session's token. A missing, repeated, malformed or other session's token is 403 `forbidden`, after the cookie's 401 and before the authorizer or any read. The shop sends the token its session check just answered, and after a refused read it checks the session once.
- **Session reuse.** The tenant session 200s now carry `subject`, a labelled digest of the session's own verified principal. A page load reads the current session first and reuses it, with no login, when the subject is the signed-in user's. No session, or another identity's, signs in; an outage, a 429 or a malformed answer signs nothing in.
- **Unchanged.** The treatment of an unconfirmed create, idempotency, rate limits, permissions, the tenant entry's ten routes and migrations 001–022. No dependency, secret or endpoint is added; one existing transitive dependency is updated for a security advisory (below).
- **Dependency advisory (a separate, earlier commit).** Two `@grpc/grpc-js` advisories, GHSA-m9gg-hp2v-232j (High) and GHSA-f596-whhp-79r4 (Low), both fixed in 1.13.6 and 1.14.5 (published 2026-09-17), reached the advisory database on 2026-09-30, after P5's final runs, so the dependency audit would have failed for any commit. All three copies now resolve to one `@grpc/grpc-js@1.14.5` with its own `@grpc/proto-loader@0.8.1`; the Firestore client keeps `@grpc/proto-loader@0.7.15` from its declared range. The two under `google-gax` move from 1.14.4 within their declared ranges (`^1.10.9`, `^1.12.6`). The Firestore client SDK's copy moves by its `package.json` override from 1.9.16 to 1.14.5. **That override is outside `@firebase/firestore`'s declared range `~1.9.0`**: this is an out-of-range compatibility change, not a patch update, because no fixed 1.9.x release exists. The browser bundle does not include grpc-js. The override's selector is unversioned, as it already was for 1.9.16, so a later `@firebase/firestore` release would also receive 1.14.5 until the override is revisited. Firestore's client header still reports `grpc/1.9.15`; that is telemetry only. The compatibility evidence is the Firestore emulator suite, the shop browser gate and the admin console containment suite, all run on a clean install of the updated lockfile: Firestore emulator rules suite 41/41, shop browser gate 22/22, admin console containment suite 41/41 and the full verification, every step exit 0 except the raw `npm audit` exit, which the dependency-audit reconciliation (exit 0) decides. Production audit: 0 Critical and 0 High (before the change, 4 High packages, all from the one High advisory). The full tree keeps its 4 recorded development-only High packages. No manifest exception was added, and the audit thresholds are unchanged. Commit `df3fc4e`, exact-commit CI 36789768621 green on all 62 steps.
- **Register.** DA-138 and DA-143 are closed in code, and DA-137 is amended. DA-147 to DA-149 are new. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **CI.** No workflow change. The existing steps run the new HTTP cases RB-01 to RB-06 (`tests/db/customerDirectory.integration.test.mjs`) and the browser scenarios SCB-07 and SCB-08 (`tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs`). Exact-commit CI: 36789768621 (`df3fc4e`) and 36791433899 (`5479d1e`), green on every step.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P6 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-41](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds customer contact records and makes recovery after an ended session deliberate, in code:
- **Contact records.** Migration 023 adds a nullable `phone` and `email` and a database-written `created_at` to the server customer. Customers created before 023 keep NULL in all three; the migration's time is never written as a creation time (DA-152). The create takes an optional phone and email (bounded, trimmed, no control or Bidi_Control character; the phone needs a digit, the email exactly one `@` and no whitespace) and still answers `{ id, name }`. A name-only body keeps its bytes and fingerprint, and the idempotency contract version is unchanged.
- **The profile read.** `GET /api/v1/customers/{id}/profile` answers `{ id, name, phone, email, createdAt }` under the detail read's policy and order of refusals, the session binding included; a foreign and a missing id are the same 404. The router admits exactly two templates by name (DA-136, amended). The tenant entry serves eleven routes.
- **Database.** Schema `tmpos_customer_contact` holds the create and the profile read, each delegating its whole authority decision to 020's create or 022's read. `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE on them, loses EXECUTE on 020's create, and still holds no table privilege. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–023, and D4 names the chain through 023. The rollback refuses while any customer holds a phone, an email or a creation time.
- **The shop.** The directory page's add form takes an optional phone and email, and a customer's page shows them ("Not provided" when absent) with the creation time ("Not recorded" for a legacy customer). The Customers screen and its records are unchanged and not linked (DA-144, amended). This part is commit `0f957c3`.
- **Deliberate Reconnect.** Once the shop has confirmed this user's session in an open page, a 401 ends it there with no login: only the Reconnect buttons sign in again. A valid session of the same user is still reused, a fresh page still signs in on its first load (DA-150), and an unconfirmed create is never re-sent during recovery (DA-137 unchanged).
- **Register.** DA-150 to DA-155 are new; DA-118, DA-136, DA-144 and DA-147 are amended (none closed); DA-137, DA-148 and DA-149 are unchanged. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; synthetic evidence closes no gate.
- **CI.** One new step runs `tests/db/customerContact.integration.test.mjs`. Exact-commit CI: 36859957536 (`52f17d7`), green on attempt 2 (attempt 1 failed only M6R-PERF, the recorded rate-limiter load flake; that commit changes no server code), and 36873703631 (`77508c1`), green on attempt 1. 36877615589 (`0f957c3`), green on attempt 1.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-42](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds the edit of a saved customer, in code:
- **The command.** `PATCH /api/v1/customers/{id}` under the new `edit_customers` (Customers at Edit or above, on the owner's stored levels; no role default changed) changes a customer's name, phone and email at an expected version, with an `Idempotency-Key`. `name` is required in every edit, a stated deviation (DA-156). A changed edit commits the new values, the version + 1, one audit record naming the customer and the sealed completion together; a current no-op is a sealed 200 that changes no customer, moves no version and writes no audit record or event, and records only its idempotency completion; a stale version is a sealed `409 write_conflict`; a missing or foreign customer the same 404. The key binds the target, and a completed edit replays before anything is evaluated again. The tenant entry serves twelve routes, and the router admits three templates by name (DA-136, amended).
- **The version.** Migration 024 adds `row_version` to the server customer (every existing row reads 1, nothing rewritten, no creation time written) and reports it as the profile 200's strong `ETag`, its JSON body unchanged and no conditional request processed.
- **Database.** Schema `tmpos_customer_edit` holds the edit, which restates 020's authority derivation at Edit, and the versioned profile read, which delegates to 023's. `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE on them, loses EXECUTE on 023's profile read, and still holds no table or column privilege. `public.audit_event` gains `subject_customer_id`. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–024, and D4 names the chain through 024. The rollback refuses while any customer has been edited or any audit record names a customer (DA-159).
- **The shop.** A customer's page offers Edit from the local role hint (DA-140, amended); "Saved" only on a validated 200; a conflict keeps the draft for a deliberate review and a new key; an unknown outcome is held in memory only and checked again only by hand with the same bytes and key, never re-sent on its own (DA-154, amended). P6's Reconnect is kept (DA-150, amended). The Customers screen and its records are unchanged (DA-144). This part is commit `5253304`.
- **Register.** DA-156 to DA-166 are new; DA-111, DA-113, DA-118, DA-120, DA-129, DA-136, DA-140, DA-150, DA-151, DA-152, DA-154 and DA-155 are amended (none closed); DA-153 and DA-155 stay open. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; synthetic evidence closes no gate.
- **CI.** Two new steps run `tests/db/customerEdit.integration.test.mjs` and, after every step that rolls 024 back, `tests/db/customerEditHttp.integration.test.mjs`; the browser step, which also commits edits, runs after it. Exact-commit CI: run 36924348695 on `8e0c775` failed only the secret-scan reconciliation, because two synthetic UUID test fixtures (a patterned v4 Idempotency-Key and the RFC 4122 DNS-namespace UUID) matched the generic-api-key rule; the follow-up `3de6088` assembles both at run time with identical values and adds two owner-approved history-only manifest entries for the two historical lines, and its run 36990574252 is green. Run 36993931444 on `5253304` is green.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P8 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-43](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds a server-side customer name search, in code:
- **The search.** `GET /api/v1/customers?q=…` under the list's unchanged policy (`view_customers`, the tenant from the trusted session only, the session binding and its CSRF header): a case-insensitive literal substring match on the name only (the ICU root locale's full uppercase of both sides; no normalization or accent folding), at most 200 code points, control and Bidi characters refused, `%`, `_` and `\` literal. A blank `q` is the unfiltered list, unchanged, and the response shape is unchanged; the whole query is bounded at 4,096 characters. A search page continues with its own cursor, bound to the text by a digest and refused for another text or the other kind. No refusal or log line carries the text.
- **Database.** Migration 025's schema `tmpos_customer_search` holds one `STABLE SECURITY DEFINER` routine that re-decides the list's authority exactly as 022's read does, with the tenant predicate inside the statement. `tmpos_app` gets USAGE and EXECUTE only. The startup preflight requires exactly 001–025, and D4 names the chain through 025. 025 refuses to apply without the ICU collation, holds no data, and so has no data guard on rollback.
- **Cost.** Linear in the tenant's customers for a rare or absent text: the `(tenant_id, name, customer_id)` page index keeps a search to the tenant's range in page order, but no installed index evaluates the uppercase substring predicate. No match took 8.459 ms over a synthetic 20,000-customer tenant (a sequential scan of 25,000 rows), bounded by the read's 2.7 s statement limit (DA-168).
- **The shop.** The directory page's search form ("Search customers by name", Search or Enter, Clear) applies a draft only on submission, says "No customers match" only after a successful empty search page, signs in only by the session client's plain rule (when no session is confirmed yet for this user or the cookie names another identity's session; after a confirmed end only Reconnect does; wording corrected in M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10), keeps the text out of the address, history and storage, and reloads page one under the applied search after a confirmed create or edit, a confirmed edit first putting the confirmed name in the edited customer's shown row. This part is commit `f326a4c`.
- **Wording.** M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7's records said a current no-op edit "writes nothing". It makes no customer change, moves no version and writes no audit record or event; its sealed `changed: false` response is recorded as the key's idempotency completion in the same transaction, so a retry replays it, and a sealed 409 likewise records only its completion. The behaviour is unchanged.
- **Register.** DA-167 to DA-174 are new: the access-log redaction of `q` required before deployment (DA-167), the linear search cost (DA-168), the dependence on ICU (DA-169), no normalization or accent folding (DA-170), the search cursor's unkeyed digest (DA-171), and three M7A-CUSTOMERS-P7 residuals recorded once here (DA-172 to DA-174). DA-84, DA-86, DA-88, DA-102, DA-108 and DA-150 are amended (none closed). DA-152, DA-153, DA-155, DA-156, DA-158, DA-163, DA-165 and DA-166 stay open. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; synthetic evidence closes no gate.
- **CI.** One new step runs `tests/db/customerSearch.integration.test.mjs` after the customer edit's HTTP step: it rolls back 025 alone, never 024 or earlier. Exact-commit CI: run 37046119243 on `8ed4e56` and run 37048495769 on `f326a4c`, each green on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P9 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-44](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It lets a new invoice draft name a customer saved on the server, in shop code only:
- **The reference.** A draft names its customer by one tagged, frozen field: none, a local id of the browser's list, or a server reference (the server's id, a display-only name, the shop user's UID and an opaque session context; no contact field). Local ids and every historical invoice, repair and order reference are untouched; nothing is mapped, imported, merged or backfilled, and no name, phone or email establishes identity (DA-176). The draft lives in React state only: no browser storage, and a reload loses it as it loses every local draft.
- **The picker.** A dialog over the existing list and P8 search, through the existing session and customers clients, offered while the plan includes the Customers feature and the local role state holds Customers at View (hints; the server's 403 decides, DA-140 amended). Rows show the name and the end of the id; nothing is selected automatically. One generation per checked session token, a new one clearing a running rate-limit wait; a window focus or a return to the tab checks the session once (plain), in the open dialog or, while it is closed, when a server customer is held; an ended or refused session, a 403 (unless its fresh session check finds another token) or another token drops a chosen customer with a notice, an undecided focus check keeps it, a user switch or lost View or Customers feature drops it at once, and late answers from an earlier draft, dialog or generation cannot bind. Only Reconnect signs in after an end, its token counting as the dialog's first (DA-150, amended). Focus returns to the search input when the focused control goes away, but there is no focus trap (DA-178).
- **The invoice draft.** Only the Create Invoice draft consumes a server reference, and Save stays disabled with an explanatory text: an invoice for a server customer cannot be created yet (DA-175; M7b). Create and edit for a local customer, the stored invoice shape, shipping and returns are unchanged. Repair drafts do not use the picker yet (DA-177).
- **No server change.** No route, permission, migration, grant, dependency or browser storage is added; the tenant entry still serves twelve routes, and migrations 001–025 are unchanged.
- **Register.** DA-175 to DA-179 are new: the server-customer draft that cannot be created (DA-175), no legacy-to-server mapping or import policy (DA-176), the pending repair-draft integration (DA-177), the dialog without a focus trap (DA-178) and five guards with no failing test (DA-179). DA-140, DA-144 and DA-150 are amended (none closed). DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; synthetic evidence closes no gate, and no gate closes.
- **CI.** No new database step. The shop's suites `src/tenant/ServerCustomerPicker.test.tsx` SCP-01 to SCP-47 and `src/components/Invoices.serverCustomer.test.tsx` INV-01 to INV-15 run in the frontend lane, whose ratchet now requires 26 files and 679 cases; the shop browser gate runs 29 cases, SCB-15 (the picker in a real browser) among them. Exact-commit CI: run 37075869185 on `a5f1416`, green on attempt 1, and run 37108347751 on `17a659b`, which failed and stays red (the later green runs do not change it): every step was green but the dependency audit, which refused two High advisories against `@fastify/busboy`, GHSA-xjh9-v7x6-24jw (CVE-2026-19484) and GHSA-x8mw-p69m-v3mx (CVE-2026-19481). Both were disclosed in August: the GitHub advisories' `nvd_published_at` field dates both CVEs' NVD records 2026-08-13. They entered GitHub's advisory database on 2026-10-02 (its `published_at` field: 23:16:17Z and 23:16:36Z), while run 37075869185 was in progress; that run's dependency audit step started at 23:26:44Z (the step's `startedAt`) and passed. *(Corrected in M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10: this record first said "published on 2026-10-02 at 23:16 UTC, after the first run", naming no source field.)* Commit `b19dfc4` moves that package to 3.2.2 within firebase-admin's declared range (lockfile only; the security manifest and the audit checker unchanged), and run 37109960449 on it is green on attempt 1. P8's documentation commit `d152702` ran green as run 37051807118 on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.

**M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-45](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It lets a new repair ticket name a customer saved on the server and makes the picker a keyboard modal, in shop code only:
- **The repair draft.** The new ticket's customer is the same tagged reference as the invoice draft's: none (nothing, or a typed walk-in name; the walk-in id is still minted at save), local (a pick from the browser's list, with its name and contact snapshot) or server (the shared field, below the customer box). Typing or a local pick replaces a server customer, Remove clears only the binding, and every close of the form drops it. A server choice fills no contact, device or other field. Create Ticket stays disabled, described by a limit text, and the handler refuses a server reference: no ticket, no reset, no close (DA-180; M7d). Local and walk-in tickets, editing, the ticket detail and every stored ticket are unchanged.
- **Authorization.** The plan's Customers feature and Customers at View offer the field, and Repairs at Create must also hold when a choice binds; all three are checks in the browser. The server's 403 decides customer reads only (DA-140, amended); no server route creates, stores or authorizes a repair ticket or an invoice.
- **The picker.** Its overlay is `z-[190]`, above every host dialog. While it is open every other `<body>` child present when it opened is inert, and closing it restores exactly those. Tab and Shift+Tab wrap around its enabled controls, Escape closes it alone, a backdrop press moves no focus, a press on its own text focuses the dialog itself (`tabIndex={-1}`) so Escape and Tab still reach it, focus returns to "Select a server customer" or Change (also when a check while it is closed drops the held customer from under a focused Change or Remove), and every control has a visible focus ring (DA-178, closed at code and browser level). The invoice and repair dialogs around it are not keyboard modals of their own (DA-181).
- **No server change.** No route, permission, migration, grant, dependency or browser storage is added; the tenant entry still serves twelve routes, and migrations 001–025 are unchanged.
- **Wording.** ADR-44 Decision 6, DA-140 and docs 05 and 09 put Invoices at Create beside "the server's 403 decides"; it is a browser check, and the 403 decides customer reads only. DA-150, the P8 block above and docs 03, 05, 09 and 10 said plain session steps "never sign in"; a plain check signs in only when no session is confirmed yet for this user or the cookie names another identity's session, and after a confirmed end only the explicit Reconnect signs in. The P9 block's CI record now dates the `@fastify/busboy` advisories by their source fields and keeps run 37108347751 red.
- **Register.** DA-180 (a repair draft naming a server customer cannot be created), DA-181 (the host dialogs are not keyboard modals) and DA-182 (a contact-free refusal assertion in a P6 server test can fail on a random request id) are new. DA-177 is closed for the repair draft only, DA-178 at code and browser level for the picker, and DA-179 is narrowed (SCP-48, SCP-49). DA-140 and DA-150 are amended. DA-175, DA-176, DA-155 (contact retention), DA-167 and DA-168 (the search's log redaction and cost) stay open. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; synthetic evidence closes no gate, and no gate closes.

Commit `c46d3eb` carries SCP-48 to SCP-57 and SCB-17 (frontend lane 26 files / 691 cases), and commit `5eeda24` REP-01 to REP-18, SCB-16 and SCB-18 (27 files / 711 cases). P9's documentation commit `706c319` ran green as run 37111259805 on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.

**M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-46](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It lets the store owner set the built-in roles' Invoices level on the server, as configuration only, and repairs DA-182's test:
- **The DA-182 repair (commit `ece44c6`).** Test code only: the contact-free refusal check excludes the random correlator and binds it to `X-Request-Id`, and the audit contact scan excludes whole canonical UUIDs (DA-182, closed). No runtime file changed.
- **The server (commit `fc42452`).** `PUT /api/v1/roles/levels` accepts domain `invoices` (`ROLE_LEVEL_DOMAINS` is `customers`, `invoices`), with the same body, owner rule (`edit_role_levels`), recent sign-in, reason, audit record (reason code `role_<role>_invoices_to_<level>`) and idempotency; one key reused across the two domains is 422. The new `GET /api/v1/roles/levels/invoices` reads the levels back under the same rule, `{ tenant, domain: "invoices", roles }`, no-store, 400 on any query, 503 on a malformed answer. The Customers routes are unchanged byte for byte. The tenant entry serves thirteen routes. `store_owner` stays Full and is never stored; the catalog defaults stay `manager` Full, `technician` View, `sales_staff` Create. No permission key, role default or level ordering changes.
- **Migration 026** (`026_m5_invoice_role_levels`). A new schema `tmpos_role_invoice` holds `m7a_invoice_role_level_states(uuid)` and `m7a_invoice_role_level_set(text, text, text, uuid, bigint)`, 021's two routines with the domain fixed to `invoices`, so an Invoices edit cannot write a Customers row; `SECURITY DEFINER`, EXECUTE and schema USAGE for `tmpos_app` only. It widens 020's and 021's domain CHECKs to `customers` and `invoices` under their existing names, and leaves every Customers object and grant as it was. Its postcondition checks `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 023's `tmpos_customer_contact.m7a_contact_create` (023 revoked 020's create). Its down runs only under READ COMMITTED and refuses while any `invoices` row exists in either table; no audit record is touched (DA-122, amended).
- **Deployment prerequisite.** The runtime and 026 move together: the startup preflight requires exactly 001–026 and 026's three grants, so the P10 build refuses a 026 database and this build refuses a 025 one. 026 is code-only (disposable PostgreSQL only); applying it to a managed database is not authorized, and none was touched.
- **The shop (commit `e0b9ddf`).** The Employees permissions screen shows a "Server-saved Invoices level" panel beside the Customers one (`ServerInvoiceLevels`, the same panel with a domain), with the Customers panel's save rules: "Saved" only after a PUT 200 and a matching read-back, a conflict needing Discard or Reapply and a new key, an unknown outcome held and re-sent only by hand. The grid's Invoices row says "Built-in roles: set in Server-saved Invoices level above". The two panels share one tenant session; a Reconnect or recent sign-in in one lets the other re-read with no login (DA-185). Reconnect and Try again move focus to the panel heading, in both panels.
- **Register.** DA-182 is closed. DA-183 (the Invoices level is configuration only), DA-184 (the materialized Invoices permission is not proven over HTTP) and DA-185 (the renewal notice lives in the panel module) are new. DA-85, DA-119, DA-121, DA-122, DA-124 and DA-125 are amended (none closed by this stage). DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; synthetic evidence closes no gate, and no gate closes.
- **CI.** Two new database steps, "Invoices role level routines against disposable PostgreSQL (M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1)" (`tests/db/invoiceRoleLevelRoutine.integration.test.mjs` M5IRL-01 to M5IRL-09) and "Invoices role level edits over HTTP against disposable PostgreSQL (M5-INVOICES-AUTH-P1)" (`tests/db/invoiceRoleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M5IRLH-01 to M5IRLH-09); `tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-C1 and `tests/db/tenantEntry.integration.test.mjs` M7TE-01 now pin thirteen routes. The frontend lane adds `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceLevels.test.tsx` SIL-01 onward and `src/tenant/roleLevelsClient.test.ts` RLC-10 to RLC-12, and its ratchet now requires at least 28 files / 729 cases; the shop browser gate runs 34 cases, SRL-13 (an owner's save, its read-back, the database row, version and audit record, and a second browser and a reload seeing it) and SRL-14 (a competing edit refused 409 with nothing written, then Reapply under a new key) new; the main runner stays at 162 suites. Exact-commit CI: run 37189788170 on `ece44c6`, green on attempt 1; run 37201875568 on `fc42452`, green on attempt 1; run 37203237043 on `e0b9ddf`, green on attempt 1. P10's documentation commit `44e5177` ran green on attempt 1 (run 37155948301). This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.
- **Still open for M7b.** Tenant or store ownership of invoices; the draft lifecycle and route rules; currency and amounts; numbering, tax, discounts and issuing; draft retention; the mapping of legacy browser customers; and which M7b operation comes first. The invoice-draft shape proposed after M7A-CUSTOMERS-P10 is not adopted as policy.

**M7B-INVOICES-P1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-47](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It adds store-owned invoice drafts (save, list, reopen read-only) under real store authority, in code only:
- **Store authority and discovery (commit `aedbee2`, with `b27354c`).** A store route is authorized only through the actor's own active store-scope grant at exactly the path store: none, two roles at one store, a grant elsewhere, a tenant grant and a missing, foreign or non-canonical store are one 403. No tenant-to-store inheritance. Two store route permissions, `view_invoice_drafts` (Invoices at View) and `create_invoice_drafts` (Invoices at Create and Customers at View on the same role), decided at store scope only on the tenant's owner-edited levels. Migration 027 (schema `tmpos_store_access`, one definer read) serves `GET /api/v1/stores`, a closed session-bound route with no authorizer. `b27354c` assembles 027's two checksum-pin keys at run time and adds the two owner-approved history-only manifest lines for them (DA-192).
- **The drafts (commit `29f35a9`).** Migration 028 (schema `tmpos_invoice_draft`: `invoice_draft`, `invoice_draft_line`, three definer routines; composite foreign keys to `public.store` and `tmpos_customer.customer` through two new UNIQUE constraints; `public.audit_event.subject_invoice_draft_id`). `POST`, `GET` (list) and `GET` (one) under `/api/v1/stores/{storeId}/invoice-drafts`: USD integer cents, 1–50 lines, BigInt totals within 2^53−1, the sealed 422 `customer_not_found` for a missing or foreign customer, the unsealed 409 `write_conflict` for another `expectedTenant`. One transaction: lease fence, store-scope revalidation, the create, one audit record (scope `store`), one outbox event `invoice_draft.created` v1, the sealed 201, COMMIT; a lost COMMIT answer is the 503 with an unknown outcome and the lease kept. Command contracts and mutators declare their context scope, and composition refuses any disagreement (`command_scope_mismatch`, also at the administrative root: DA-195). The tenant entry serves seventeen routes.
- **Deployment prerequisite.** The runtime and 027–028 move together: the startup preflight requires exactly 001–028 and the new grants. 028's up and down lock five (down: seven) tables `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` before any check, a maintenance window; 028's down refuses while any draft, line or draft-naming audit record exists; roll 028 back before 027. Code-only: no managed database was touched.
- **The shop (commit `15110e4`).** A separate "Server drafts" section on the Invoices page: an explicit store choice, the store's drafts with Load more, a read-only reopen, "New server draft" with exact cents; at most one POST per Save draft or Check again, an unconfirmed save held in memory and re-sent only by Check again with the same key and bytes, never after a user or session-token change; "Save as server draft" from the local form carries only the customer. Server drafts never enter the browser's invoices.
- **Register.** DA-183 narrowed (the stored Invoices level decides the drafts' save, list and reopen, and nothing else); DA-184 closed for the draft save and list (IDH-03; the reopen not driven through an owner edit over HTTP); DA-175 narrowed; DA-60 amended. New: DA-186 (deferred draft and invoice lifecycle), DA-187 (draft retention and privacy), DA-188 (store grants provisioned outside the product; no inheritance), DA-189 (store-only actors and the picker; the 201-versus-422 existence signal), DA-190 (a lost unconfirmed save and a second draft), DA-191 (the idle-window wait), DA-192 (the JWT-fixture manifest entry's texts), DA-193 (browser coverage of a user change), DA-194 (the shop browser scenario inventory: resolved in code at `15110e4`; one CI step still runs the whole file), DA-195 (the administrative root's scope rule), DA-196 (the launcher's signal case under load), DA-197 (other load losses), DA-198 (a sign-out scrubs nothing: the signed-out user's token held in page memory; the logout is best effort) and DA-199 (focus falling to the page body outside the drafts' dialogs). DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions; drafts saved by a direct routine call are not audited (028's header, as 020's create). In the closing round DA-184 is closed in full (the reopen too), DA-193 and DA-199 are closed, DA-198 is closed for the Server drafts section and the picker, DA-183, DA-190 and DA-197 are amended, and DA-200 (the Customer directory page keeps a token after a sign-out), DA-201 (New offered on the local role hint, refused by the server) and DA-202 (a closed server-customer field keeps an ended session's customer on screen) are new.

- **Closing round (commits `fa3dfd5`, `e79251d`).** `fa3dfd5` (tests only; no server, migration, pin or floor change) races the draft save in IDH-09 (AC-10) against four more changes, each in both halves: a tenant suspended, a tenant made read-only, an account suspended and a store grant deleted (the membership row itself, not a status). Committed while the save waits on 016's hold (the account suspension made inside the owner's hold, whose transaction commits while the save waits), each leaves the save refused inside its transaction (503, reason `transaction_unavailable`) with nothing written (no draft, line, audit record, event or completion) and its lease open; attempted while the save holds its locks, each change's backend is seen waiting on a lock the save holds (`pg_stat_activity`, never a sleep) and applies only after the save commits. It also drives the reopen through the owner's level edits, over HTTP (IDH-03) and on the get routine (IDR-05), closing DA-184. `e79251d` (shop, tests and the frontend floor; no server, migration or package change; `ServerCustomerDirectory.tsx` deliberately unchanged) makes a sign-out heard where it starts (`onSessionEnd`, `sessionEndsSoFar`), so the drafts hold store, the picker's pair, the mounted section and the open picker dialog give up the token at once (DA-198); gives the section one focus recovery outside its dialogs (DA-199); adds, for AC-17, a case that fails when its hunk of `15110e4` is reverted to each file that commit touched without one (`customersClient` CDC-23, `ServerCustomerPicker` SCP-58, `ServerCustomerLevels` SCL-63, `Employees` EMP-01, `tenantSessionClient` TSC-33; TSC-32, also new, is DA-198 evidence for `onSessionEnd` and passes with that hunk reverted); and proves in Chromium a user switch in one tab (SDB-05: AC-15, DA-193), every focus site (SDB-06), a browser-only custom role whose save the server refuses 403 `forbidden` with nothing written (SDB-07: AC-04, DA-201) and a direct switch with a read out (SDB-08). **M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-47](./10-architecture-decision-records.md), amended).** It takes up the P1 closing round's three shop residuals and adds one read-only route, which the shop now uses for its New offer, in code only:
- **Draft creation eligibility (commit `6b5cef8`).** `GET /api/v1/stores/{storeId}/invoice-drafts/eligibility`, a fourth closed store template whose literal last segment is matched before the `{id}` template: a session-bound read authorized by the save's own requirement object (`INVOICE_DRAFT_CREATE_REQUIREMENT`, `create_invoice_drafts`), so the chain's one tenant authorizer and evaluator decide both. `200 { "eligibility": "allowed" }` and nothing else; every refusal of the actor or the store, and a session-CSRF mismatch, is one 403 `forbidden`; 401, 429 with `Retry-After`, 503 and 404 are the boundary's. No business row is written; like every authorized read, a 200 refreshes the session's `lastSeenAt` and each request spends the request limit. It is a snapshot: the save decides again under its locks. No new grant, permission default or inheritance; no migration. The tenant entry serves eighteen routes.
- **Dependency (commit `f2f42f1`).** Lockfile only: `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.29.0 → 1.31.0, within its parents' ranges, for GHSA-6qxp-vccf-f47h (published after the last green run); no package.json, override or manifest change.
- **Session-end cleanup (commit `68bc927`).** The Customer directory's create hold store hears every sign-out from its module's load and gives up every token and retryable body, a late answer settling by key only (DA-200); a server-customer field drops its customer at a sign-out with its picker closed, and on mount when its context is dead, in the invoice and repair ticket drafts (DA-202); the new-draft dialog's hand-over applies nothing once its session check is overtaken (DA-198's evidence limit).
- **The shop's New offer (commit `33972c0`).** The Server drafts section offers "New server draft" only on the server's exact `allowed` for the current user, generation, store and request, never on the local role hint (`canCreate` is gone from the section); denied, unavailable, rate-limited and ended are said apart, with no local fallback; a 403 from a save or a Check again takes New away at that store (DA-201).
- **Register.** DA-200 closed for the create hold store (the edit hold store still follows the sign-in state); DA-201 closed for the Server drafts section; DA-202 closed; DA-197, DA-198 and DA-199 amended. No new row. DA-186 to DA-189 stay open and unchanged; DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **CI.** `tests/db/invoiceDraftsHttp.integration.test.mjs` gains IDH-15 to IDH-18 in its existing step; `tests/db/roleLevels.integration.test.mjs` M7RLH-C1 and `tests/db/tenantEntry.integration.test.mjs` M7TE-01 now pin eighteen routes. M7B-INVOICES-P1's documentation commits ran green on attempt 1: `b2b22e2` as run 37459989717 and `138f8e3` as run 37479504994 (59 of 59 quality steps, appsec 12 of 12). This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.

**M7B-INVOICES-P2 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-48](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It lets a saved store invoice draft be edited, in code only, and finishes the Customer directory's session-end cleanup. No gate closes.
- **Customer edit-hold session cleanup (commit `dc48862`).** The Customer directory's edit hold store hears every sign-out from its module's load, mounted page or not; a sign-out drops every user's held edit and recorded edit outcome whole (token, body with contacts, expected version, name, key), keeping only a per-user mark for one unnamed notice, and Save and Check again send nothing once a sign-out began (DA-200, now closed in full). The fix is discriminated in jsdom only; in Chromium SSE-04 is a regression guard.
- **The draft edit (commit `573de6c`).** `PATCH /api/v1/stores/{storeId}/invoice-drafts/{id}` and `GET /api/v1/stores/{storeId}/invoice-drafts/{id}/edit-eligibility` under the new store route permission `edit_invoice_drafts` (Invoices at Edit or above, no Customers level); migration 029; the audit action `store.invoice_draft.update` and the event `invoice_draft.updated` v1; the tenant entry serves twenty routes. Conflicts are sealed per operation: a stale version, a stale no-op included, is the sealed 409 `write_conflict`; a current-version no-op is the sealed 200 `changed: false` with no business row, version, audit record or event (its idempotency completion is still written); a state that admits no edit is the sealed 409 `invalid_state`; a missing or foreign draft or customer is one unsealed, non-enumerating 404; an `expectedTenant` mismatch is the unsealed 409. A lost COMMIT answer is the bounded 503 with an unknown outcome, recovered only by the same key and bytes. The contract is in [05](./05-canonical-data-ownership-and-api-db-contracts.md) §2.
- **Deployment prerequisite (029).** The startup preflight requires exactly 001–029 and the new EXECUTE grant: the 001–028 runtime refuses a 029 database and this runtime a 028 one. 029's up and down take `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on seven tables before any check (a maintenance window). Its down refuses while any draft's version is above 1; retained edit audit records and `invoice_draft.updated` rows do not block it, while draft-naming audit records still block 028's down (DA-187). Roll 029 back before 028. Proven on disposable PostgreSQL only.
- **The shop editor (commit `4f3c700`).** The reopen dialog offers Edit only on the edit eligibility's exact `allowed` for the user, generation, store, draft and request; the editor sends one PATCH per Save with the attempt frozen first, says "Saved" or "No changes were needed" only after the 200, shows a conflict beside the working copy with "Reapply my changes" (a new key) and "Discard my changes" (local only), and re-sends an unknown outcome only on "Check again" with the same key and bytes. Its unknown outcome is held only while the editor stays open (DA-208); which context changes are proven in Chromium and which in jsdom only is DA-209. The role screen's Invoices panel now says the server decides who may "save, list, open and edit server drafts" (DA-183).
- **Register.** DA-200 closed in full. DA-36, DA-118, DA-120, DA-159, DA-183, DA-184, DA-186 (editing delivered; the rest of the lifecycle stays open), DA-187, DA-190 and DA-197 amended; DA-199, DA-201 and DA-202 carry corrections. New: DA-203 to DA-209. G-MIGRATE's target line is corrected to 001–029. DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **CI.** Two new CI steps: `tests/db/invoiceDraftEditRoutine.integration.test.mjs` (IDE-01 to IDE-15) after the draft HTTP step, and `tests/db/invoiceDraftEditHttp.integration.test.mjs` (IEH-01 to IEH-14) after the shop browser step (DA-207). Main runner 168 suites at commit `573de6c` (`MIN_SUITES` 167 → 168, `server/runtime/invoiceDraftEdit.test.ts`). Frontend ratchet 29 files / 878 cases at `dc48862` (`MIN_FRONTEND_CASES` 857 → 878) and 914 cases at commit `4f3c700`; shop browser gate 50 scenarios at `dc48862` (SSE-04 added) and 55 at commit `4f3c700` (SDB-11 to SDB-15). Exact-commit CI: run 37627199542 on `dc48862`, green on attempt 1 (quality 59 of 59 steps, appsec 12 of 12); run 37857708848 on `573de6c`, green on attempt 1 (quality 61 of 61 steps, appsec 12 of 12); run 37862498219 on `4f3c700`, green on attempt 1 (quality 61 of 61 steps, appsec 12 of 12). M7B-INVOICES-P1-R2's documentation commit `6cf553a` ran green on attempt 1 as run 37598779938 (quality 59 of 59 steps, appsec 12 of 12). This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.

(2) Defect cases and regression guards: SID-50 passes on its entry source and is a regression guard (DA-199); the entry-source runs for DA-201 cover SID-58 to SID-69 only (DA-201); SCP-72 and INV-21 were never run on the entry source (DA-202). (5) External actions: besides the pushes and CI reads, the stage requested a rerun of run 37506410965's failed job (its attempt 2), its independent review requests sent change excerpts to an external review service, and commit `f2f42f1` read the public npm registry — `npm view` for the package's integrity, a download of its tarball (not executed), `npm install --package-lock-only --ignore-scripts` on a copy, and `npm audit` (production and full), which sends the dependency list to the registry's audit service. **M7B-INVOICES-P2-R1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-48](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It closes DA-204 for the runtime edit path while migration 030's trigger is enabled, in code and tests. No gate closes, and M7b is not complete.
- **The authority correction (commit `c7810bb`).** The edit runs `tmpos_invoice_edit.m7b_invoice_draft_update_v2`, which migration 030 adds in its own schema; `tmpos_app` no longer executes 029's routine. For manager, technician and sales_staff the edit takes the role's Invoices guard key (a transaction-scoped advisory lock per tenant and role) SHARED after the draft header and before the customer, then decides the level again by a plain read. Every sealed answer and the change path are decided after that point and commit while the key is held. An AFTER row trigger on `tmpos_role.tenant_role_level` takes the same key EXCLUSIVE for these writes of an Invoices row: an INSERT or DELETE of a non-NULL level, and every UPDATE (to or from NULL included), from 026's setter or any other writer; an INSERT or DELETE of a NULL row takes nothing. The edit writes and locks no role row: 029's step 10 and its anchor are removed.
- **Reproduction on the entry source (`919a377`).** An existing override downgraded to Create after the edit had passed its unlocked read let a current no-op seal `unchanged` and a stale version seal `write_conflict` (IDE-11). An absent override's first insertion did the same (IDE-16). A rolled-back move to `issued` answered `invalid_state` while the writer's narrowing was uncommitted (IDE-22). Over two tenant entries, the PATCH answered 200 or 409 after the PUT had committed on the other entry (IEH-15; expected 503). The cases were driven by lock-state barriers, not sleeps.
- **Ordering.** A downgrade committed after the request's unlocked checks and before the key is granted is read by the edit's own post-wait decision: the edit is refused with the existing transaction-time 503, the lease is kept, and nothing else is sealed or written; one committed earlier is refused by those earlier checks. An owner TRUNCATE is ordered behind an edit by the edit's ACCESS SHARE. A downgrade attempted while the edit holds the key waits for its COMMIT. A request that completed before a downgrade is not undone; replay keeps the chain's unlocked current-authority check and takes no key.
- **Outside the closure.** Owner DDL on the level table (`DISABLE TRIGGER`, an altered or replaced trigger function, `DROP`); the owner's direct call of 029's routine; a direct `tmpos_app` caller that holds a transaction past its session's expiry, or calls 026's setter outside the runtime; a login's holding of a guard key, and a hash collision, both availability only (DA-120); a table-owner transaction writing several (tenant, role) Invoices pairs, which can deadlock with another in the opposite order (40P01, one side aborted, no wrong authority answer; the least-key-first order covers one key-moving row only); a composed call that breaks THE RULE of 030's header (a direct `tmpos_app` holder running the edit and then 028's create in one transaction, or a table-owner transaction that locks another pair's version row, a customer or a draft's lines before a level), which can deadlock (40P01; availability, no wrong authority answer); the draft save's window (DA-210); and a rollback to 029, which restores the window.
- **Register.** DA-204 is closed for the runtime path. DA-36, DA-118, DA-120, DA-203, DA-207 and DA-208 are amended; DA-203 keeps its signal open, and its 404 wording now says that one identical body hides which reference check failed. The P2 summary's "non-enumerating 404" means only that the one 404 body hides which reference check failed; the operation keeps a customer-existence signal (DA-203). DA-205, DA-206 and DA-209 are unchanged. DA-210 is new and open: the draft save, frozen 028, has the same kind of window for a sealed `customer_not_found`; recorded, not changed.
- **Migration 030 and the chain.** The startup preflight requires exactly 001–030, with the edit's EXECUTE on the new routine and the schema's USAGE in place of 029's EXECUTE. 030's up and down take ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on seven tables before any check; its down refuses, changing nothing, only on the catalog or the applier's rights (030's header lists them), never on data, and restores 029's routine grant. Roll 030 back before 029. Proven on disposable PostgreSQL only; G-MIGRATE's target is 001–030.
- **CI.** Exact-commit run 37961559395 (attempt 1 failed in the rate-limiter suite on the recorded load races M6R-03 and M6R-PERF, a suite the commit changes only in its migration list; the failed-job rerun, attempt 2, passed: quality 61 of 61 steps, appsec 12 of 12) on the code commit `c7810bb`. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.
- **Still open.** DA-186 (the rest of the draft and invoice lifecycle), DA-187, DA-188 and DA-189; DA-203 (an applied edit to another customer id is the existence signal); DA-208 (the recovery limit of an unknown outcome); the DA-120 availability class; DA-210 (the draft save's window, frozen 028; an owner decision); and every invoice operation other than the drafts' save, list, reopen and edit (DA-183).
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database, live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters and ephemeral loopback servers. The stage's recorded network actions are ordinary fast-forward pushes to `main`, authenticated fetches of `origin`, CI reads, and independent review requests that sent change excerpts (diffs, changed source, the stage record and these drafts) to an external review service; a secret scan of every request sent found nothing.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7B-INVOICES-P1-R3 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-47](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It closes DA-210 for the runtime save's Invoices level while migration 030's trigger is enabled, in code and tests, and corrects SDB-05's ordering assertion. No gate closes, and M7b is not complete.
- **Reproduction on the entry source (`c4ba482`).** A create was paused at its customer read, after its unlocked Invoices decision, by a test-held lock on the customer table (a lock-state barrier, not a sleep); the owner's PUT, by a different operator on a second tenant entry, narrowed the role's Invoices level to View and committed; released, the create answered the sealed 422 `customer_not_found` and its completion committed, with no draft, line, audit record or event (ICA-01(a), an existing override; ICA-02(a), the role's first insertion; two runs, each failing on that safety assertion). The same ordering with an existing customer was already refused (ICA-01(b), ICA-02(b)).
- **The authority correction (commit `224a02a`).** The save's adapter calls migration 031's `tmpos_invoice_edit.m7b_invoice_draft_create_v2`. Beside that one routine, 031 makes exactly three privilege changes: the new routine's default EXECUTE (PUBLIC's) is revoked, `tmpos_app` is granted EXECUTE on it, and `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on 028's `tmpos_invoice_draft.m7b_invoice_draft_create` is revoked, so `tmpos_app` no longer executes 028's create. For manager, technician and sales_staff the save takes 030's guard key SHARED after the customer read, reads the database clock and the Invoices level again under it, and decides both sealed answers with the key held to COMMIT. Its lock order: the fence; 016's hold (the revalidator's, then the routine's again); the session and ledger rows `FOR SHARE`; the issuer key row `FOR SHARE`; the store row `FOR SHARE`; step 8's plain read of the levels; step 10's plain read of the customer; the key SHARED; the Customers role row (its NULL anchor) `FOR SHARE`; the customer `FOR KEY SHARE`; the new header and lines; the runtime's audit record, outbox event and completion; COMMIT. 028's Invoices NULL anchor and `FOR SHARE` are removed, so the save never locks an Invoices role or version row after the key (THE RULE of 030's header). store_owner takes no key. Permission, payload, responses, fingerprint, idempotency, lease, audit, event and unknown-outcome handling are unchanged.
- **Ordering and writes.** A narrowing committed after the save's unlocked checks and before the key is granted refuses the save with the existing transaction-time 503, and nothing is sealed: no completion, draft, line, audit record, event or role row (ICA-01, ICA-02, ICA-06); one committed earlier is refused by those earlier checks (before the request is authorized, the authorizer's 403, ICA-03; after it, the revalidation's or step 8's `refused`, the generic 503). One attempted while the save holds the key waits for the save's COMMIT, after its audit record, event and completion (ICA-03, ICA-04); a completion that fails rolls the transaction back and the key goes with it (ICA-08). A sealed refusal can write a completion without writing business/audit/event state. An unsealed transaction refusal retains the separately acquired lease. A lost COMMIT acknowledgement is an unknown outcome: the same-key retry replays what landed, one draft, audit record and event (ICA-12). Replay takes no key and keeps the chain's current-authority check (ICA-09).
- **Outside the closure.** Owner DDL on the level table (`DISABLE TRIGGER`, an altered or replaced trigger function, `DROP`); the owner's direct call of 028's routine, which takes no key; a call of the save routine outside the runtime's command transaction; a table-owner transaction that locks a Customers role row or a customer and then writes an Invoices level (40P01, availability; DA-120); any database where 031 is not applied, every managed database included; a rollback of 031, which restores the window; and the Customers corequisite of a sealed `customer_not_found` (DA-211).
- **Register.** DA-210 is closed for the runtime save's Invoices level. DA-120 and DA-207 are amended. DA-211 is new and open. DA-204 gains a dated note; its closure is unchanged: 030's edit guard and trigger are untouched, 031 leaves 001–030 byte-identical and alters none of 030's objects, and 030's down is refused while 031 is applied.
- **Migration 031 and the chain.** The startup preflight requires exactly 001–031, with `create_v2`'s EXECUTE in place of 028's create. 031's down never refuses on data and restores the exact 030-era surface (028's create EXECUTE re-granted to `tmpos_app`), which reopens the DA-210 window. Roll 031 back before 030 (030's down is refused atomically, 2BP01, while 031 is applied), then 029 and 028. Proven on disposable PostgreSQL only; 031 is applied to no managed database, and a managed apply through 031 is future work under the existing operator gates; G-MIGRATE's target is 001–031.
- **Tests.** `tests/db/invoiceDraftCreateAuthorityHttp.integration.test.mjs` (17 cases: ICA-01 to ICA-04, ICA-08, ICA-09 and ICA-12, two tenant entries over one disposable database) and `tests/db/invoiceDraftCreateAuthority.integration.test.mjs` (30 cases: ICA-05 to ICA-07, ICA-10 and ICA-11, at routine level with the real setters), each passing twice on the candidate. Labels: R red on `c4ba482` for DA-210 (ICA-01(a), ICA-02(a)); N the new contract; G a guard that holds before and after. On the entry sources the final HTTP suite fails 14 of its 17 cases: the two R subtests on their safety assertion, ICA-03, ICA-04 and ICA-08 on their key-hold assertion, and ICA-12 on its key-hold assertion alone; the guards ICA-01(b), ICA-02(b) and ICA-09 pass. The 23 other DB suites changed by the stage were threaded through 031 (the chain 001–031, and 031's down before 030's wherever they roll back), each passing alone; two CI steps run the ICA suites right after the edit routine's (DA-207).
- **SDB-05 (commit `7197712`; tests only, no product code changed).** A CI run saw an edit-eligibility GET recorded after the sign-out. By the client code that read cannot be initiated after the app's sign-out (its last await is the session connect, then a synchronous staleness check, then the fetch), and the page's own initiation log put the GET before the logout POST in seven real runs. The old assertion compared against the runner's observation index, taken before the runner cleared the persisted sign-in, which the app notices only on its Firebase poll, so a GET initiated before the logout could be recorded after that index: a test ordering, not a product defect. The corrected boundary is the page's own initiation of its one logout POST: from there until the next user begins signing in the page initiates nothing, and after the sign-out only that logout and GETs the page initiated before it (each listed) carry the old session's CSRF token or cookie. SDB-16 (initiation before sign-out: the GET parked across the sign-out, cancelled there, its late answer showing and starting nothing) and SDB-17 (sign-out before initiation: the session read parked, no edit-eligibility GET ever initiated) run in Chromium; SID-112 and SID-113 hold both orderings in jsdom. Limit: a request an `onSessionEnd` listener initiated in the same synchronous run as the logout could not be told apart in the page log from one initiated before it; that window is asserted by SID-112 and TSC-32. Floors: frontend cases 914 to 918; shop browser scenarios 55 to 57.
- **CI.** Exact-commit run 38036772301 on `224a02a`, green on attempt 1; run 38040423456 on `7197712`, green on attempt 1. M7B-INVOICES-P2-R1's documentation commit `c4ba482` ran as run 37967495391, green on attempt 4 after three failed-job reruns on the unchanged commit: attempt 1 failed at the shop browser step, whose Chromium launch timed out; attempt 2 at the rate-limiter step on the recorded M6R-PERF load race, the browser step skipped; attempt 3 at SDB-05, the ordering this stage corrects; attempt 4 passed (quality 61 of 61 steps, appsec 12 of 12). M7B-INVOICES-P2's documentation commit `919a377`, whose run `c4ba482`'s record left out, ran as run 37902069936, green on attempt 3: attempt 1 failed on the rate-limiter load race M6R-03, attempt 2 at the shop browser step's Chromium launch ("Browser.getVersion: timed out"), and attempt 3 passed (quality 61 of 61 steps, appsec 12 of 12). This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.
- **Still open.** DA-211 (an owner decision); DA-186 (discard, delete, issuing, payments, numbering, tax, discounts and the rest of the lifecycle), DA-187 (retention and privacy), DA-188 and DA-189; DA-176 (legacy customer mapping); DA-203; DA-208 (the dialog-limited recovery of an edit's unknown outcome); the DA-120 availability class and owner DDL; DA-118 and DA-119 (pre-deployment blockers); M6R-PERF, the recorded rate-limiter load race, unresolved and separate; and every invoice operation other than the drafts' save, list, reopen and edit (DA-183).
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database was contacted or migrated, and no live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters, ephemeral loopback servers and a local browser. The stage's recorded network actions are ordinary fast-forward pushes to `main`, authenticated fetches of `origin`, CI reads, and independent review requests that sent change excerpts to an external review service; each was scanned for secrets before it was sent and found clean.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7B-INVOICES-P1-R4 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-47](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It closes DA-211 for the runtime save while migration 032's trigger is enabled (DA-210's Invoices closure still needs 030's), in code and tests, and keeps DA-210's scoped closure. No gate closes, and M7b is not complete.
- **The requirement (unchanged).** The save needs Invoices at Create or above and, on the same role, Customers at View or above (`create_invoice_drafts`, `server/platform-identity/m5CanonicalPermissions.ts`); a role without a stored level has its catalog default for both domains (`manager` Full, `technician` View, `sales_staff` Create); `store_owner` is Full, never stored, and takes no key and no read. No threshold, grant, default, plan rule or owner shortcut changes, and holding that existing requirement through its deciding transaction needed no owner-policy decision.
- **Reproduction on the entry source (`30828fc`).** Two tenant entries over one disposable database, the create and the owner's change by different actors. A create was paused at its customer read by a test-held ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on the customer table (a lock-state barrier, not a sleep), observed holding the level table's ACCESS SHARE (its unlocked step 8 done) and no guard key; the owner's PUT with `domain: "customers"` on the second entry narrowed the role's Customers level to None and committed (200) while it waited; released, the create answered the sealed 422 `customer_not_found` and its completion committed, with no draft, line, audit record, event, role row or version row of its own (CCA-01a(a): manager, an existing Edit override; CCA-01b(a): sales_staff, no Customers row, the PUT writing the role's first, replacing its default Create). Two runs, each failing on exactly those two safety assertions with: "SAFETY (DA-211): the create answered the sealed 422 {"error":"customer_not_found"} AFTER the customers narrowing to none committed first — its completion committed: true". With an existing customer the same ordering was already refused by 031's applied-path re-decision (the (b) variants: the 503, the record unsealed, the lease held).
- **The correction (commit `cdd1ff1`).** The save's adapter (`server/persistence/postgresInvoiceDrafts.ts`) calls migration 032's `tmpos_invoice_edit.m7b_invoice_draft_create_v3`. Migration 032 adds, all in 030's schema, one trigger function (`m7b_customers_level_guard()`, 030's guard with the domain `customers`), one trigger (`trg_tenant_role_level_customers_guard`, AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE, FOR EACH ROW, enabled ALWAYS) and one routine, `create_v3`, which is 031's `create_v2` with exactly two changes: step 10b reads the Customers level again beside the Invoices level after the key and the clock, by separate plain SELECTs taken after any wait, and decides both as step 8 does; and 031's steps 11–12 (the Customers NULL anchor, its `FOR SHARE` and the locked re-decision) are removed. It makes three privilege changes: PUBLIC's default EXECUTE on the routine and the trigger function is revoked (the trigger function is granted to nobody), `tmpos_app` is granted EXECUTE on `create_v3`, and `tmpos_app`'s EXECUTE on `create_v2` is revoked. The trigger takes 030's key EXCLUSIVE for a non-NULL Customers INSERT or DELETE and for every UPDATE whose old or new row is a Customers row (the old and the new key, the smaller first); a NULL INSERT or DELETE takes nothing. Permission, payload, responses, fingerprint, idempotency, lease, audit, event and unknown-outcome handling are unchanged.
- **Lock order.** The save (`create_v3`): the fence; 016's hold (the revalidator's, then the routine's again); the session and ledger rows `FOR SHARE`; the issuer key row `FOR SHARE`; the store row `FOR SHARE`; step 8's plain read of the levels (ACCESS SHARE on the level table to COMMIT); step 10's plain read of the customer; the key SHARED; the clock; the plain Invoices and Customers re-reads; then `customer_not_found`, or the customer `FOR KEY SHARE` and the new header and lines; the runtime's audit record, outbox event and completion; COMMIT. It never waits for a role row or a version row. 021's Customers setter: the tenant-write prefix, its Customers role row (anchor, then `FOR UPDATE`), its version row `FOR UPDATE` (none on a role's first change), the level UPDATE, the key EXCLUSIVE (032's trigger; 030's takes nothing for a Customers row), 021's version upsert. 026's Invoices setter, 030's edit and 024's customer edit (which never takes the key) are unchanged. THE RULE of 030's header is widened to both domains: a transaction holding the key SHARED never locks a Customers or an Invoices role row or version row after taking it, and a writer taking it EXCLUSIVE holds before it nothing a key holder waits for after its key. The runtime keeps it: one mutator per command transaction, each runtime setter writing one row of one (tenant, role, domain). TRUNCATE fires no row trigger; it is owner-only and its ACCESS EXCLUSIVE waits for the save's ACCESS SHARE (taken for manager, technician and sales_staff; a store_owner save reads no level row).
- **Ordering and writes.** A narrowing of either level committed after the save's unlocked checks and before the key is granted is read by step 10b, and the save is refused with the existing transaction-time 503: nothing is sealed, and there is no completion, draft, line, audit record, event or role row. One committed earlier is refused by those earlier checks. One attempted while the save holds the key waits for the save's COMMIT or rollback, or times out and rolls back; COMMIT and rollback decide which change is visible. An authorized missing customer keeps the sealed 422, with its completion and no invoice, audit record, event or configuration row. A sealed refusal can write a completion without writing business/audit/event state. An unsealed transaction refusal retains the separately acquired lease. A lost COMMIT acknowledgement is an unknown outcome. Replay takes no key and keeps the chain's current-authority check.
- **Contention.** Customers level writes now wait for in-flight draft saves and draft edits of the same (tenant, role) — an edit decides only the Invoices level yet shares the key — and Customers and Invoices setters of one pair serialize on it. A Customers setter waiting for the key holds its Customers role row `FOR UPDATE`, so that role's customer creates and customer edits queue behind it, and a later save or edit of the role queues on the key behind it. Two pairs whose hashes collide are serialized. In runtime command transactions every such wait is bounded by the lock and statement timeouts and ends in the existing 503 with the lease kept. Availability only; none of it grants authority (DA-120).
- **Outside the closure.** Owner DDL on the level table (`DISABLE TRIGGER` of 032's or 030's trigger, an altered or replaced trigger function, `DROP`); the owner's direct call of 031's `create_v2` or 028's create, neither of which decides the Customers level under the key (`create_v2`, which takes the key and then its Customers anchor `FOR SHARE`, can now deadlock with a Customers setter, 40P01); a call of the save routine outside the runtime's command transaction, and a direct `tmpos_app` caller of 021's setter that holds its transaction on the key past its session's expiry (no recent sign-in re-check, G-AUDIT); a table-owner transaction that writes several (tenant, role) pairs, or both domains of one pair in separate statements, or locks a customer and then writes a level, and a direct `tmpos_app` transaction that holds the key SHARED and then runs the customer create or 024's customer edit, each of which can deadlock (40P01, availability, no wrong authority answer; DA-120); any login holding the key, and two pairs whose hashes collide (availability); any database where 032 is not applied, every managed database included; a rollback of 032, which restores this window; and customer editing: 024's customer edit is frozen and unchanged (its Customers NULL anchor and `FOR SHARE`, no key), and DA-205's status is unchanged.
- **Register.** DA-211 is closed for the runtime save while 032's trigger is enabled. DA-210's closure is kept, with a dated note: `create_v3`'s step 10b decides the Invoices level exactly as `create_v2`'s did. DA-120 (the key's contention widens to Customers level writes), DA-204 (its key is now also taken by Customers writers; its closure is unchanged) and DA-207 (the rollback chains through 032) are amended. DA-205 is not changed: 024's customer edit is frozen and keeps its Customers anchor, which takes no key.
- **Migration 032 and the chain.** The startup preflight requires exactly 001–032, with `create_v3`'s EXECUTE in place of `create_v2`'s; the 001–031 runtime refuses a 032 database and this one a 031 database. 032's up and down take ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on 030's seven tables before any check (a maintenance window). Its down never refuses on data and restores the exact 031-era surface (`create_v2`'s EXECUTE for `tmpos_app` again, no 032 trigger), which reopens the DA-211 window.
