Roll 032 back before 031, and 031 before 030: on throwaway clusters, 031's down while 032 was applied was refused atomically by its existing postcondition (55000, "migration 031 rollback refused: the save routine still exists, or schema tmpos_invoice_edit does not hold exactly migration 030's two functions"; surface and data unchanged), 030's down was refused (2BP01), 032's down restored the 031-era surface with the data identical, a second 032 down was refused (55000), 031's and 030's downs then ran, the chain re-applied through 032, and a second 032 up was refused (55000). Proven on disposable PostgreSQL only; 032 is applied to no managed database, and a managed apply through 032 is future work under the existing operator gates; G-MIGRATE's target is 001–032.
- **Tests.** `tests/db/invoiceDraftCreateAuthorityHttp.integration.test.mjs` (42 cases: CCA-01a, CCA-01b, CCA-02, CCA-03a, CCA-03b, CCA-09 and CCA-10, with ICA-01 to ICA-04, ICA-08, ICA-09 and ICA-12 retargeted to `create_v3`; two tenant entries over one disposable database) and `tests/db/invoiceDraftCreateAuthority.integration.test.mjs` (64 cases: CCA-04 to CCA-08, CCA-11 and CCA-12, with ICA-05 to ICA-07, ICA-10 and ICA-11 retargeted; at routine level with the real setters). Each passed in at least two runs on the candidate, and again in the CI-order gate on the committed tree after the review's title, label and inconclusive-guard corrections. Labels: R red on `30828fc` for DA-211 (CCA-01a(a) and CCA-01b(a), each failing there on its safety assertion in two runs); N the new contract; G a guard that holds before and after (the (b) variants of CCA-01, CCA-02(a) and (c), CCA-08 — ICA-06 and parts (1), (2) and (6) of ICA-07 run on `create_v3` — and CCA-10(a)). Two negative controls in the routine suite: with 032's trigger disabled inside the writing transaction, a Customers narrowing commits while the save still holds the key SHARED with its `customer_not_found` uncommitted (with the trigger enabled the narrowing waits on the key and is cancelled, 57014); and the owner's `create_v2` under 032's trigger deadlocks with a Customers setter (40P01), where `create_v3` in the same arrangement applies and the setter follows. The 25 other DB suites changed by the stage were threaded through 032 (the chain 001–032, three triggers on the level table, 032's down first wherever they roll back, a save that writes no role row), each passing alone. One of them, M5IRLH-07 in `tests/db/invoiceRoleLevels.integration.test.mjs`, failed before its pin on a consequence of 032, not on load: a Customers write of the role whose Invoices write was parked now waits for the parked write's key, so the park ran 1446 ms, past the 1400 ms lock bound, and the parked Invoices write answered 503 instead of 200. It now asserts that wait (the same role's Customers write blocked on an advisory lock held by the parked backend, another role's Customers write committing meanwhile, both writes committing after release) and treats a park longer than the lock bound as inconclusive. All 38 DB steps passed in CI order on one shared disposable cluster. Floors unchanged: frontend cases 918, shop browser scenarios 57.
- **Local verification.** The gate ran on a local commit with the same tree as `cdd1ff1` (`763e3c34`): typecheck, the three builds, the main runner (168 of 168 suites), the frontend runner (918 cases), the security, forbidden-pattern, lockfile and Firestore static and emulator checks, the 38 DB steps in CI order, the admin browser journeys (41 of 41) and the secret scan all passed. The shop browser step did not pass on its first three attempts. The first could not start Java ("Could not spawn `java -version`": a newly fetched JDK package would not execute on the host; no scenario ran). The first rerun, with an already-present JDK, ended with a host restart before it wrote any result. The second rerun failed one of 57 scenarios: SDB-06, when Chromium did not answer a CDP command for a new tab within the harness's 30 s (`Page.getFrameTree: timed out`); no assertion evaluated a wrong value. Its cause is not proven, and it is recorded as unresolved. A third, quiet full rerun passed 57 of 57; a sampler during it showed the host's available memory reaching zero, with out-of-memory kills, in the step's first minute. That rerun does not repair the failure, which stays recorded here.
- **SDB-05 (unchanged).** This stage changes no product UI code. SDB-05 remains supported by its reported initiation evidence (M7B-INVOICES-P1-R3 above) and its complementary tests SDB-16 and SDB-17 in Chromium, SID-112 and SID-113 in jsdom, and TSC-32.
- **CI.** Exact-commit run 38063910572 on `cdd1ff1`, green on attempt 2 after a failed-job rerun on the unchanged commit: attempt 1 failed at the rate-limiter step on M6R-03 ("every consume is an allowance or a limit", 14 of 15), the recorded M6R-PERF load race, unresolved and separate — the commit's only change to that suite is M6R-01's title and ledger pin, and the suite passed 15 of 15 in the local CI-order gate; attempt 2 passed (quality 63 of 63 steps, appsec 12 of 12). A failed verification run or CI attempt stays recorded here with its cause, and a later green attempt or descendant does not relabel it. M7B-INVOICES-P1-R3's documentation commit `30828fc` ran as run 38042197219, green on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.
- **Still open.** DA-186 (discard, delete, issuing, payments, numbering, tax, discounts and the rest of the lifecycle), DA-187 (retention and privacy), DA-188 and DA-189; DA-176 (legacy customer mapping); DA-203; DA-205 (024's customer edit, frozen); DA-208 (the dialog-limited recovery of an edit's unknown outcome); the DA-120 availability class, now covering Customers level writes, and owner DDL; DA-118 and DA-119 (pre-deployment blockers); M6R-PERF, the recorded rate-limiter load race, unresolved and separate; and every invoice operation other than the drafts' save, list, reopen and edit (DA-183).
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database was contacted or migrated, and no live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters, ephemeral loopback servers and local browser harnesses. The stage's recorded network actions are ordinary fast-forward pushes to `main`, authenticated fetches of `origin`, CI reads and one failed-job CI rerun, the local toolchain's re-fetch of the public nixpkgs package set and its fetch of a public JDK package, and independent review requests that sent change excerpts to an external review service; each review request was scanned for secrets before it was sent and found clean.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M6R-PERF-R1 changes no gate's status; G-IDEMPOT and G-PERF stay `OPEN` ([ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It explains two failure signatures recorded under DA-60 and DA-110 — M6R-03's consumes answering unavailable (a false test assumption, widened by an adapter defect) and M6R-PERF's `unhandledRejection write CONNECTION_DESTROYED` (a kernel defect) — and corrects them with two corrections those failures demonstrated, a third found in review, and a rewritten exactness test, in code and tests (commits `71bbb03` and `849de20`). M6R-PERF's "nothing unavailable" and M6RS-07 stay unexplained (below). The limiter's contract and bounds are unchanged; nothing is deployed, and no performance or throughput claim is made (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R2).
- **The failures (CI, 2026-09-25 to 10-10: 13 failing job attempts in the two rate-limiter steps, across 12 runs).** M6R-PERF `unhandledRejection write CONNECTION_DESTROYED <relay socket>` ×6, every one with the same stack (the driver's `terminate`, from the destroy timer of `end({ timeout: 0 })`); M6R-03 "every consume is an allowance or a limit" ×3 (the 200-consume mixed-cost burst, 50 deep in each instance's line) and its burst unavailable ×2 (37902069936: 66 unavailable; 37961559395: 116, of which the log lists six `55P03` and 83 kernel failures, answered grants only `36..39`); M6R-PERF "nothing unavailable" ×3; M6RS-07 ×1. The conformance diagnostic "6 665 ms (window 6 000 ms)" is not a failure: the suite's fixed-window check sleeps 6 075 ms by construction, and all 81 passing attempts printed 6 372–6 787 ms.
- **Cause 1 — a false test assumption (M6R-03).** Each consume waits in its instance's line for the bucket under its own 500 ms deadline (`LIMITER_DEADLINE_MS`), so a line 30 or 50 deep on a slow host answers its deepest consumes unavailable: a contract-compliant fail-closed answer, so M6R-03's requirement of none was invalid; how often it happens under load remains a DA-60 and G-PERF concern. Reproduced on the entry code with a test-owned 5 ms delay on every server reply: "none unavailable — 74 !== 0" and "every consume is an allowance or a limit" failed while every committed grant was exact. That run also carried Cause 2, so it does not separate the two; after the correction, RLR-04's delayed-reply schedule still lost 68 consumes before sending and 4 in flight to the deadline (CI 38083159776), which is Cause 1 alone (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R2).
- **Cause 2 — an adapter defect: dispatch past the deadline.** When the deadline timers fire, one callback at a time, the aborted transaction at the head of a line frees it in microtasks, and the next waiter — its own timer due but not yet run — sent a transaction that was aborted at once, retiring a pool and opening a fresh connection. Under the reply delay 55 of the hot burst's transactions were sent and aborted this way; a 450 ms stall of the client left 16 answered grants (24..39) — the same family as 37961559395 (grants 36..39), with different counts; that run's own cause was not isolated — and a 250 ms stall gave a `55P03` behind the stalled holder of the row. **Correction** (`server/persistence/postgresRateLimiter.ts`): each call fixes its deadline on the monotonic clock before its timer and checks it in the very step that hands its transaction to the kernel; started-then-aborted transactions fell from 55 to 4 in the hot burst (one per line in flight) and from 122 to 9 in the mixed one (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R2).
- **Cause 3 — an adapter defect found in review: a late answer obeyed.** A transaction's answer that arrived past the deadline, when a busy event loop ran the reply before the deadline timer, was obeyed. Latent: no CI failure is traced to it, and it is pinned by unit M6R-10c only. **Correction:** an answer is obeyed only while the bound holds; past it the call answers unavailable, its cost spent without an allowance (as the contract already allows), never a late allowance. The raw transaction still holds the line until the kernel answers (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R2).
- **Cause 4 — a kernel defect (DA-110).** postgres.js 3.4.9 sends its own array-type query on a new connection's first ReadyForQuery, outside the kernel's gate, and drops that query's promise; the kernel's retirement of the pool during it rejected the query with no handler. Reproduced on the entry code by withholding, at the relay, the reply to a named session's first query and ending its transaction — by an abort and by the limiter's own deadline: the CI signature and stack. Cause 2 widened the window (every aborted transaction opened a fresh connection). **Correction** (`server/persistence/supervisedPgClient.ts`): `fetch_types: false`, forced beside `prepare: false`. No statement sent through the kernel binds or returns an array type; jsonb, bytea, text, number and timestamp handling do not depend on the fetched types.
- **Test responsibilities after the stage** (`tests/db/rateLimiter.integration.test.mjs`). **M6R-03** keeps 120 consumes of one bucket (limit 40) and 200 of mixed cost (limit 100) over four instances on two kernels against real PostgreSQL, and proves exactness consume by consume through a kernel observer tied to each consume by its async context (never a wrapper of the adapter): each allowance or limit is exactly its own committed decision, inside its deadline; at most one transaction per consume and none sent past its deadline; an unavailable answer is the deadline behind a work-conserving line, or before it only PostgreSQL's lock or statement bound — never a decision withheld in time or another store fault; the committed grants fit one serial order summing to the owner-side count; a burst that lost no consume to its deadline grants exactly the limit and refuses past it; the rest of the window holds exactly what the burst left; every decision falls inside one window of exactly 6 000 ms on the database clock. How many consumes reached their deadline is reported, not asserted. **RLR-04** forces overload — every reply 5 ms late, and the client stalled past every deadline once every line has a transaction outstanding — and proves it fail-closed under the same account, with the production bounds pinned (500 ms, 64, 200/300/400 ms) and every instance recovering. **RLR-06** (with RLR-07's cleanup check) pins Cause 4. **RLR-09** feeds the account eight defects (an allowance for an unavailable consume, a limit answered as an allowance, an early unavailable answer, a retry, a send after the answer, a double spend, an early refusal, a store fault), each refused by an assertion naming its defect. Unit M6R-10b and M6R-10c pin Causes 2 and 3, and the kernel's option pin Cause 4; each fails with its correction reverted and passes with it. The fixed-window proofs (M6R-04 and the conformance suite) and the conformance suite itself are unchanged.
- **Limitations, documented in the test.** The account enumerates at most twelve grants whose COMMIT acknowledgement was lost; its causal early-refusal check compares a refusal only with grants sent after it was answered (sound, not complete); its dispatch bound allows the one synchronous step between the test's timestamp and the adapter's own deadline. The conformance suite's checks 4, 4b and 5 keep their own timing preconditions (25- and 6-deep lines inside the deadline; about 950 ms of slack in the fixed-window check (6 000 − 5 050)), never seen failing.
- **Local verification.** The full gate ran on an earlier local candidate (tree `b3cd3ce6`), which differs from `849de20` in comments in two files and in M6R-03's condition: typecheck, the three builds, the main runner (168 of 168 suites), the frontend runner (918 cases), the security, forbidden-pattern, lockfile and Firestore static and emulator checks, every CI database step in CI order on one shared disposable cluster (none failed), the admin browser suite (41 of 41) and the shop browser suite (57 of 57, first attempt), and the secret scan (the 34 known findings, none in changed files). After the review's last edits (two files: comments, and M6R-03's condition) only the affected checks ran again locally on `849de20`'s exact tree (`255b24ae`): typecheck, the server builds, the main runner, the security and forbidden-pattern checks, the rate-limiter suites (18 of 18; 17 of 17) and the secret scan; the remaining gate steps ran on `849de20`'s bytes only in its CI run (below). The earlier commit-1 tree (`890fe021`) ran the main runner, typecheck and the rate-limiter, runtime and transactional-store suites, and after its comment-only rebuild `71bbb03`'s tree (`fe230fda`) ran the rate-limiter suite again (16 of 16) and the kernel's unit test; typecheck and the main runner ran on `890fe021`, not on `fe230fda`, and on `71bbb03`'s bytes only in CI 38081281088. Each correction was reverted on a local candidate (tree not recorded) and its regression failed: RLR-06 with the CI signature, RLR-04 ("sent its transaction 502 ms after it was called"), M6R-10b and M6R-10c (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R2).
- **CI.** Exact-commit run 38081281088 on `71bbb03`, green on attempt 1 (quality 63 of 63 steps, appsec 12 of 12; RLR-06 passed on CI's shared database, and M6R-PERF answered nothing unavailable). Run 38083159776 on `849de20`, green on attempt 1 (quality 63 of 63, appsec 12 of 12; the rate-limiter step 18 of 18 — M6R-03's 120-consume burst granted 40 and limited 80 in 184 ms and its mixed burst granted 31 and limited 169, none unavailable; RLR-04's delayed-reply schedule lost 68 consumes before sending and 4 in flight to the deadline, its stall schedule 116 and 4, with the deadline timers 52 ms late; RLR-06 and RLR-09 passed). M7B-INVOICES-P1-R4's documentation commit `595eb09` ran as run 38066849932, green on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the stage report.
- **Still open.** DA-60 (narrowed): M6R-PERF's "nothing unavailable" (3 CI attempts) and M6RS-07 (1) are probably the same lane-and-deadline family, but were neither reproduced nor changed here; DA-60's original local `55P03` replay is consistent with a stalled client holding the row (reproduced at 250 ms), but no run isolated that occurrence. DA-110's remainder: the platform's admin and runtime pools, the migration executor's two clients (a frozen path) and the identity-validation script's client keep the driver default. Each new connection of theirs sends the type query, and a connection failure during it — not only a retirement — can reject it unhandled; the platform pools open new connections while serving requests. Not reproduced for these clients and not changed here. SDB-06 (the shop browser CDP timeout) and the unidentified host memory kills are untouched and open. G-PERF stays OPEN: the measured numbers are this sandbox's, not a service level (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R2).
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database was contacted or migrated, and no live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters, ephemeral loopback servers and local browser harnesses.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M6R-PERF-R2 changes no gate's status; G-IDEMPOT and G-PERF stay `OPEN` ([ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It assesses, each on its own, the rate-limiter verification failures M6R-PERF-R1 left unexplained — M6R-PERF's "nothing unavailable" (3 CI attempts) and M6RS-07 (1) — and explains M6R-PERF's as two fail-closed mechanisms, each reproduced locally with a schedule the test owns, and probably attributes M6RS-07's to the second (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R3); and it adds diagnostics, in tests only, that record each refused consume's timing, how its transaction ended and what the store was doing, the next time either test fails. No runtime defect was demonstrated and no runtime behaviour changed: two server files change in comments only, there is no migration, and the bounds are unchanged (the 500 ms deadline, lane depth 64, the 200/300/400 ms store bounds). Nothing is deployed, and no availability repair, performance or throughput claim is made.
- **The failures, assessed separately.** M6R-PERF 37453777754 (`13335168`): 3 unavailable, all `55P03`, exact accounting true, maximum latency 457 ms. 36883413179 (`eed3a9b5`): 29 unavailable — 2 `55P03`, 18 kernel failures and 9 with no transaction failure — exact accounting false, maximum 507 ms. 36568875233 (`ead1974a`): 4 unavailable — 1 kernel failure and 3 with none — exact accounting false, maximum 501 ms. M6RS-07 36996687809 attempt 1 (`f91415e3`): in the second (tenant) call the 429 count was 5, not 6, while the 401 count (300) passed, so exactly one of 306 replies was neither; its status and reason were never printed. All four ran on commits without M6R-PERF-R1's corrections (`71bbb03`, `849de20`). Run time does not separate them: two of the three failing M6R-PERF durations (4 034, 4 447 and 5 111 ms) lie inside the 81 passing attempts' 1 974–4 537 ms.
- **Mechanism 1 — a hot bucket's line reaching the 500 ms deadline (all 4 unavailable answers in 36568875233; 27 of the 29 in 36883413179).** M6R-PERF's 600 hot consumes serialize on one row across both instances, and the hot tail is waiting in line, not executing (a passing local run: waiting until hand-over p95 204 ms, maximum 248; hand-over to the routine's answer p95 16 ms, maximum 47). Reproduced (related): runs with three synchronous client stalls of 150 or 250 ms failed M6R-PERF with 7, 16 and 32 unavailable — consumes never sent, aborted at the deadline, or committed after it and answered unavailable; and a test-owned 3 ms delay on every server reply sits at the edge: one run failed with 22 unavailable (15 aborted at the deadline, 4 never sent, 3 committed after it; the kernel answered each of the 15 as unavailable, an answer it never gives after a COMMIT that could have committed), exact accounting false, and the account reported no bucket outside its range (a run without the one-window guard, far shorter than the 60 s window; reported, not asserted, since the run had already failed; wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R3) — the shape of both runs — while 2 ms passed (p95 346, maximum 457 ms). CI's server log: nothing in 36568875233's M6R-PERF window, so its 4 were client-side deadlines (1 aborted after hand-over, 3 never sent); in 36883413179's, 2 lock timeouts and 3 cancellations at the client's request on one row (inferred: statements still queued on the row when their consumes reached the deadline).
- **Mechanism 2 — a consume reaching the 200 ms row-lock bound, `55P03` (all 3 in 37453777754; the other 2 in 36883413179; probably M6RS-07's one). Reproduced by holding back another consume's COMMIT; CI's logs establish the lock timeouts, not what held the rows.** Holding one hot transaction's COMMIT 250 ms while the process ran, three times at least 1 s apart, failed M6R-PERF with exactly 37453777754's signature: 3 unavailable, all `55P03`, exact accounting true, maximum 477 ms (a second run added 2 kernel failures at the deadline); 150 ms passed. Each `55P03` consume was handed over, began its work, waited about 200 ms, and ended `55P03` with its ROLLBACK acknowledged, not committed. In M6RS-07, holding one limiter COMMIT 250 ms in wave 7 of the tenant call failed "expected 6, actual 5" — CI's exact assertion and counts — the odd reply a 503 `rate_limit_unavailable` whose transaction ended `55P03`, not committed, and the holder's routine-answer-to-end 261 ms; 150 ms passed. CI's server log: 37453777754 has three lock timeouts on bucket rows in its M6R-PERF window, matching its 3 `55P03`, and nothing else, its maximum latency 457 ms, under the deadline; 36996687809 has one lock timeout on a bucket row inside the failing tenant wave, and its counts (401 = 300, 429 = 5) mean the odd reply's transaction committed no allowance. What delayed the holders in CI — the client, or the server's COMMIT and flush — is not attributed.
- **M6RS-07's probable cause, the weaker hypotheses, and what stays unresolved.** For M6RS-07, mechanism 2 is the probable cause: the odd reply's status and reason were not logged, CI's log shows one lock timeout on a bucket row in that wave, and holding one limiter COMMIT 250 ms reproduced a 503 whose transaction ended `55P03` with the same counts. M6R-PERF-R1's deadline hypothesis is less likely for that event but not ruled out (wording qualified in M6R-PERF-R3). Raising M6RS-07's reply latency reproduces mechanism 1 only as a related failure (8 ms on every server reply: 89 replies 503, with both reasons; 4 ms passed at the edge), and one 150 or 300 ms stall early in wave 7 did not fail it. Three synchronous stalls of a holder during M6R-PERF produced no `55P03`: the suite's in-process relay freezes too, so a waiter whose statement has not yet reached the server never starts its lock wait — not a reliable `55P03` schedule in these runs (M6R-PERF-R1's single 250 ms stall during an M6R-03 burst did give one), and replaced by the late-COMMIT schedule. M6R-PERF-R1's adapter correction does not measurably change M6R-PERF under uniform latency: at the 3 ms edge the current adapter and M6R-PERF-R1's entry adapter each failed once (22 and 26 unavailable) and passed once (p95 396 and 394 ms, maximum 445 and 447). DA-60's original local `55P03` replay is consistent with mechanism 2 and still not isolated.
- **A new local observation, a test assumption: M6R-03's dispatch bound.** The stage's first candidate run (M6R-03 unchanged from `3d3a6a0`, with the host under CPU contention) failed M6R-03 once locally: "consume burst0:117 sent its transaction 503 ms after it was called" — M6R-PERF-R1's 1 ms dispatch bound, measured from the call's start. In 16 further runs (8 calm, 8 beside three CPU-bound processes) the call's synchronous step, in which the adapter fixes its deadline, took up to 17 ms, so R1's premise that the step takes microseconds is false: a correct adapter whose deadline was fixed late in a long step can hand over inside the gap. None of the 16 dispatched late from either origin, and the failure was not reproduced on demand (it needs a long step and a hand-over in that gap). **Correction, in the test:** every "no later than its deadline" bound is measured from the end of the call's step, and every "not before" bound keeps the call's start; the call's step itself is capped at 100 ms, so an adapter that stalls before fixing its deadline is still refused, and the two margins that span a microtask gap are 5 ms. That is an observation envelope: it refuses only what no correct adapter could do, and that holds only while the host's pauses stay inside its margins (a longer pause can refuse a correct adapter). It is not a proof of the adapter's deadline; M6R-PERF-R3 gives that in the adapter's unit test M6R-10d. Not an adapter defect, by the code: the adapter's only hand-over is inside its guarded step.
- **Diagnostics added (tests only; every original assertion and workload unchanged, apart from M6R-03's bounds above).** **M6R-PERF** prints, before any assertion, an account of every consume — a synthetic id, its instance, when its call's step returned, its deadline sentinel, hand-over, when the kernel began its work, the routine's answer and decision, how and when the transaction ended (SQLSTATE or kernel failure), the answer, and, inferred, how many consumes were in its line at its call — with each transaction's outcome class (not sent, committed, not committed, commit unknown) taken from how the kernel answered for it, never from the limiter's answer (named by the kernel's phases in M6R-PERF-R3); the owner-side count of every bucket reconciled against its acknowledged grants plus those whose acknowledgement was lost (from that second snapshot); the event-loop delay; the time split into waiting in line, hand-over to work, and the routine; and up to twelve failing consumes in full. Its owner-side snapshot and its five original assertions are where they were; the account is taken afterwards, with a second snapshot used only for the reconciliation, and four assertions follow the originals: the account was computed; every allowance or limit was traced to its own transaction; the kernel answered for every transaction it sent within 3 s of the last answer (the kernel's answer, not the server's end: the database server may still be ending an aborted one); and, only when the run fitted one window on the database clock, every bucket reconciles (otherwise the account reports the reconciliation as inconclusive — a run-wide guard, made per bucket in M6R-PERF-R3). **M6RS-07** prints one line per call — each reply neither 401 nor 429 with its wave, instance, status, logged reason and time, and that instance's limiter transactions open during the reply (all of them, not only the reply's own, including those handed over before it, such as a row's holder), including the holder's routine-answer-to-end time; the slowest wave; the event-loop maximum — and passes it as the message of both original count assertions, their values unchanged. **RLR-10** (no database) pins the account's own limits and the observation envelope's bounds — the envelope, not the adapter's deadline (a hand-over 500 ms after the call's step passes and 506 ms after it is refused; a late answer is refused; a 150 ms step is refused).
- **The account's limits.** The twelve-unknown boundary is an explicit named failure: more than twelve grants whose COMMIT acknowledgement was lost fails "at most twelve grants whose COMMIT acknowledgement was lost (n)" before any subset is tried, never a silent pass, and an owner-side count outside its acknowledged grants plus the unacknowledged ones is reported (RLR-10). The early-refusal check stays sound but not complete: it uses the burst's final count, compares a refusal only with grants sent after it was answered, and leaves out uncertain grants and refusals that did not commit.
- **`fetch_types: false` compatibility (the kernel, DA-110).** In the installed postgres.js 3.4.9 the setting only registers array-type parsers and serializers on each new connection; the scalar handlers are built at construction and do not depend on it. With it off, an array column comes back as its text literal. No kernel caller binds or reads an array, enum, domain, composite, numeric or timestamptz value (none in the signatures of the 42 routines called; no `create type` or `create domain` in the migrations), and the kernel refuses a JS array parameter before the driver sees it (its unit test). Each shape in use has real-kernel database evidence: jsonb parameters and results with int4 and uuid (M6-PG-03, M6-PG-04, M6-PG-05); a JSON-array jsonb parameter and an int8 at 2^53−1 read exactly as a string (IDH-06); jsonb memberships (M5P-06, M5A-03); bytea parameters with int4 results (M6R-04, M7M-10); int8 through the tenant directory (M5T-03); role-level jsonb (M7RLH-*, M7RL-G01); boolean, text and integer preflight results (M6PF-02); boolean through the store-access entry. The off state is pinned in the kernel's unit test and against the database (RLR-06). Not proven, and bound or read by no caller: Date and JS bigint parameters, and an array result read as text. M7CE-10 runs over a stand-in kernel with the driver default, so it is not evidence.
- **Outside the kernel (DA-110's remainder; open, not changed).** The platform's admin pool `getDb()` (max 3) and runtime pool `getRuntimeDb()` (max 10) in `server/platform-identity/db.ts` (`idle_timeout` 20 s, closed with `end({ timeout: 5 })`); the migration executor's `createPostgresExecutor` and `createManagedDevExecutor` (`server/platform-identity/migrationExecutor.ts`, frozen, max 1, `idle_timeout` 0); and the one-shot `scripts/supabase-identity-validate.ts`. All keep `fetch_types` at the driver default. In the driver's code a socket error, a close or a termination during a fresh connection's type query rejects it with no handler (read in the code; not reproduced for these clients). A static walk of the production entries' imports found no caller of the platform pools (their callers are the `identity:api` development surface and the identity repositories); it does not see dynamic imports. The frozen migration executor is unchanged.
- **An observation, not a demonstrated defect: a bucket's line is held until the kernel answers.** The adapter frees the line when the kernel's transaction settles. After a normal end that is after COMMIT's outcome and the connection's reset; after an abort mid-statement (BEGIN, the settings or the routine) the kernel answers at once, before PostgreSQL has cancelled the statement or ended the session, so the next consume of the bucket can be sent while the abandoned backend still holds the row, and wait on it up to its lock bound (inferred from the code). 36883413179's server log (code before M6R-PERF-R1) showed three statements queued on one row at once, more than the one-per-instance line admits — consistent with this, not shown. The adapter's comment now says so; nothing else changed.
- **Still open.** DA-60 (OPEN: availability under load is G-PERF's and an owner decision); DA-110 outside the kernel (above); SDB-06; the host's memory kills (the cgroup's out-of-memory kill counter was 63 at this stage's entry, 46 at M6R-PERF-R1's); G-PERF. Availability-policy options were put to the owner with the stage report (not in the repository); none is adopted, no service level is set, and the current gates — M6R-PERF's "nothing unavailable" and M6RS-07's exact counts — stay as they are (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R3).
- **Local verification.** The full gate ran on a local commit whose tree, `884b8a18`, is `bb874ab`'s exactly, in CI's step order: the typecheck contract and both projects, the server builds and the client build check, the main runner (168 of 168 suites), the frontend runner (918 cases), the security, forbidden-pattern, lockfile and Firestore checks (static and emulator), every database step in CI's order on one shared disposable cluster (38 steps, none failed — the rate-limiter suite 19 of 19, the runtime suite 17 of 17), the secret scan (the 34 known findings, none in a changed file), and the protected files (68 of 68). The administrative and shop browser suites failed their first attempt (40 of 41 and 55 of 57: a browser protocol timeout and an aborted navigation) while the host ran out of memory (its out-of-memory kill counter rose 93 to 118 during the shop suite); each was run once more on the same tree and passed (41 of 41, 57 of 57). Before the gate, the two rate-limiter suites ran twice each on the candidate and twice each again after the review edits; one of those runs failed under a 7-second stall of the whole process (two review processes ran beside it, the load at 5.9 on four processors; the account recorded an event-loop delay of 7 193 ms; M6R-12, unchanged, failed in the same window) and is kept on record, not counted. With the adapter's deadline check removed, RLR-04 still failed under the new bounds ("510 ms after its call returned"). The documentation commit was checked with the main runner's document readers and the secret scan.
- **CI.** `bb874ab` (the tests and the two comment-only server files, parent `3d3a6a0`), pushed as an ordinary fast-forward: CI 38093050742 succeeded on attempt 1 (quality 63 of 63 steps, application security 12 of 12). In that run M6R-PERF's account printed as designed — no consume unavailable, maximum latency 193 ms, the hot line's longest wait 181 ms, all 2 000 transactions committed, 281 buckets reconciled under the run-wide one-window guard (wording corrected in M6R-PERF-R3) — and M6RS-07's two calls printed no reply other than a 401 or a 429 (slowest waves 59 and 48 ms). The documentation commit's own CI is recorded in the stage report.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database was contacted or migrated, and no live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters and ephemeral loopback servers.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M6R-PERF-R3 changes no gate's status; G-IDEMPOT and G-PERF stay `OPEN` ([ADR-26](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It tightens the evidence behind M6R-PERF-R1 and R2 — what proves the limiter's deadline, what is established about each transaction, and when the owner-side reconciliation applies — and qualifies claims the evidence did not support. Tests and test comments only (`d70f3c5`): no runtime file, bound, migration, workload or gate changes, and no runtime defect was found. Nothing is deployed, and no availability repair, service level or performance claim is made.
- **The deadline, proved against the adapter.** The adapter fixes its deadline from one reading of its monotonic clock, inside the call's synchronous step (`postgresRateLimiter.ts`); it hands work to the kernel, and obeys an answer, only while the clock reads strictly before it — when the clock reads exactly the deadline, nothing is sent and nothing obeyed. Unit **M6R-10d** drives the real adapter with a scoped clock and held timers (the deadline timer never runs): a line freeing one clock reading (1 ms in the test) before the deadline sends and its answer is obeyed; at or past it nothing is sent; an answer at or past it is unavailable; and a stall in the call's step after the deadline is fixed moves nothing (stalled 600 ms, nothing is sent though the call has just returned; stalled 499 ms, an answer 51 ms after the call returned is refused). With either check changed from "at or past" to "past", either check removed, or the deadline read after the step's later work, M6R-10d fails at its own case. M6R-10b and M6R-10c (late by 20 ms) are unchanged. The integration bursts' call-return bounds are an observation envelope: they refuse only what no correct adapter could do, and that holds only while the host's pauses stay inside their margins. They are not a proof of the deadline.
- **Transaction outcomes, named by the kernel's phases.** In the transaction kernel (`supervisedPgClient.ts`) a value is given only after COMMIT is acknowledged; the body's own failure only after its ROLLBACK is acknowledged; `outcomeUnknown` only once COMMIT was handed over and no answer establishes what it did; and `unavailable` never after a COMMIT the server could have committed — COMMIT was never handed over (after an abort, the check after the body and the gate's checks at hand-over each keep it off the connection), or the server answered it with an error or as a rollback. A new kernel unit test pins the paths no earlier test reached: an abort while COMMIT is on its way waits for COMMIT's own outcome (acknowledged: the value; lost: `outcomeUnknown`), a close then is `outcomeUnknown`, never `unavailable`, and a body that returns after an abort hands nothing over. M6R-PERF's account now names each transaction as not dispatched, established commit, established non-commit, commit unknown or still pending — from the kernel's answer, never the limiter's — and, separately, whether the server's end of it is established: the kernel's answer settles the caller, not the database server's own process for that transaction, so after an abort that process may still be running and holding its row (not established). The account also reports whether the test's database login had no transaction left running before the second snapshot (per login, not asserted). The limiter's unavailable answer can stand for any of these; reconciliation never turns an unknown outcome into a known one.
- **Reconciliation, per bucket.** M6R-PERF's window is 60 s (not M6R-03's 6 s). The second snapshot now reads each bucket's window and the database clock in the same statement, and a bucket is eligible only when no transaction of it was unanswered at the snapshot, its window opened at or after the run's start and less than one window length (60 s) after it (so it is the run's first), and was still open at the snapshot — or, with no row, when no window of the run could yet have ended. An eligible bucket outside [acknowledged grants, those plus the unacknowledged] fails; an ineligible one is inconclusive with its reason and is never a pass; and with every transaction answered and the snapshot within one window length of the run's start, every bucket must be eligible, so a defect that made every bucket ineligible fails the test instead of silently checking nothing. RLR-10 pins each boundary on both sides and a mixed call. M6R-PERF-R2's run-wide guard was sound for the four runs that recorded it — the CI run, the local gate and both re-verification runs, one of which failed under R2's 7-second stall — each with `oneWindow` true and none of 281 buckets outside its range; but 22 of R2's 26 saved accounts predate the guard, so their eligibility is inferred, not recorded (keys fresh to each run; each consume loop took at most about 9 s on the client clock, well under the 60 s window with the settle and end waits added), and two experiment runs whose tracing was invalid printed every bucket outside its range. R2's "every bucket reconciled" for its failing experiments is corrected accordingly. The original snapshot and its five assertions are unchanged and first.
- **Claims qualified.** M6RS-07's one failure is attributed, probably, to the row-lock mechanism: its odd reply's status and reason were not logged, CI's log shows one lock timeout on a bucket row in that wave, and holding one limiter COMMIT 250 ms reproduced a 503 whose transaction ended `55P03` with the same counts — a mechanism consistent with the failure, not proof of its cause; M6R-PERF-R1's deadline hypothesis is less likely for that event but not ruled out. R2's commit messages (`bb874ab`, `445eb06`) keep their stronger wording; this record supersedes it. Synchronous holder stalls are now described as not a reliable `55P03` schedule in R2's runs, rather than as refuted. R2's stage report (not in the repository) called the cost of its new tracing on timing not material; its 4 alternating traced and untraced pairs showed no clear slowdown, but they do not bound that cost. This document's R2 record, DA-60's R2 note, the roadmap and the README are corrected in place.
- **Availability options stay UNADOPTED.** One option put to the owner with the stage report: were the owner to choose a measured budget for M6R-PERF and M6RS-07, any assertion that a consume's answer is its own committed decision would apply to allowances and limits only, and unavailable and unknown outcomes would be classified by their evidence. No figure has been proposed as a target; any number is an illustration, not a validated threshold. The current gates — "nothing unavailable" and M6RS-07's exact counts — stay; no service level is set and no performance host is provided. G-PERF (M8, MED) is a launch performance gate, not a prerequisite for code-only product work.
- **Still open.** DA-60 (OPEN; availability under load is G-PERF's and an owner decision); DA-110 outside the kernel (unchanged); SDB-06; the host's memory kills (counter reset by a host restart before this stage; 3 at entry, 10 by the documentation commit, cause not identified); G-PERF.
- **Local verification.** A bounded gate ran on a local commit whose tree, `d5ce43fc`, is `d70f3c5`'s exactly: the typecheck contract and the three TypeScript projects, the main runner (168 of 168 suites), the security gates (structural only; the scanner reconciliation runs in CI), the forbidden-pattern and lockfile checks, the rate-limiter and runtime database suites twice each on disposable clusters (19 of 19 and 17 of 17 each time), the secret scan (the 34 known findings, none in a changed file), whitespace, the excluded paths, the two runtime files byte-identical, and the protected files (68 of 68). Before review edits, the same database suites passed twice each and the unit suites 41 of 41. The builds, the frontend, Firestore and browser suites, and the other database steps were not run locally; the exact-commit CI covers them.
- **CI.** `d70f3c5` (the four test files, parent `445eb06`), pushed as an ordinary fast-forward: CI 38133162450 succeeded on attempt 1 (quality 63 of 63 steps, application security 12 of 12). In that run M6R-PERF's account printed as designed — no consume unavailable, maximum latency 353 ms, the hot line's longest wait 326 ms, all 2 000 transactions an established commit with the server's end established, the test login's sessions quiet, 281 buckets eligible and passed, none inconclusive — and M6R-PERF, M6RS-07 and RLR-10 passed. The documentation commit's own CI is recorded in the stage report.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database was contacted or migrated, and no live secret, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred; no Preview server was running at entry or exit. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters and ephemeral loopback servers.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7B-INVOICES-D1-P1 changes no gate's status (documentation only).** It proposes how a saved store invoice draft could be discarded, in [11](./11-m7b-invoice-draft-discard-decision-pack.md): the owner decisions, the route contract, the concurrency and idempotency rules, the shop's behaviour, the migration's implications, planned tests and implementation slices. The pack is PROPOSED / UNADOPTED.
- **Nothing is implemented or adopted.** No route, state, migration, permission key or UI button exists for it.
- **Discard stays deferred (DA-186)** until the owner records the pack's decisions.
- **DA-187 (retention and privacy) stays open.** The proposed logical discard keeps every row, so it removes no data and adopts no retention period.
- **Nothing deployed.** No live secret, configuration, managed database, provider, deployment or Preview action occurred.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-49](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It records the owner's acceptance of the five discard decisions of [11](./11-m7b-invoice-draft-discard-decision-pack.md) (D-DIS-1 to D-DIS-5, by the owner's stage instruction of 2026-10-11) and delivers them in code: a store team member at Invoices Manage or above can discard a saved draft after confirming it; the draft leaves the store's open drafts and is not deleted. No gate closes, and M7b is not complete.
- **The decisions.** D-DIS-1: a logical, terminal `draft` → `discarded` (nothing deleted, no restore, neither issuing nor erasure). D-DIS-2: `discard_invoice_drafts` at Invoices Manage or above, on any draft in the trusted store, with no Customers corequisite and no recent sign-in. D-DIS-3: hidden from the list and the reopen (404); `expectedVersion` required and the version stepped once; a stale version the sealed 409 `write_conflict`; a fresh key against a discarded draft the sealed 409 `invalid_state`, decided ahead of the version, never a no-op. D-DIS-4: a confirmation naming the draft, no reason field, one audit record, no outbox event. D-DIS-5: an unknown outcome held only while the confirmation stays open, "Check again" with the same key and bytes within 23 hours, on a press only.
- **The server (commit `efca7f3`).** `POST /api/v1/stores/{storeId}/invoice-drafts/{id}/discard` (the command `store.invoice_draft.discard`; the closed body `{ expectedTenant, expectedVersion }`, at most 1 KiB; `200 { draftId, version, status: "discarded" }`) and `GET …/{id}/discard-eligibility` (target-blind, exactly `200 { "eligibility": "allowed" }`); the load-time guard widened for that one key at Manage; the chain's command target read directly before the matched route's own suffix, with the save's and the edit's fingerprints unchanged; `PRECONDITION_UPDATE_KINDS`, a frozen closed set of the edit's and the discard's kinds; the discard's mutator, binding the revalidated store; the drafts' list and reopen on 033's open-only routines; twenty-two tenant routes; the startup preflight at exactly 001–033 with its new grant set. The audit record is the kernel's (action `store.invoice_draft.discard`, permission `discard_invoice_drafts`, scope `store`, the draft as subject, metadata `{}`), and nothing is enqueued.
- **Migration 033 and the chain.** `033_m7b_invoice_draft_discard` adds schema `tmpos_invoice_discard` with the discard routine (030's `update_v2` derivation, header lock and guard, with Manage in place of Edit; the state decided before the version), the open-only `page_v2` and `get_v2`, two ENABLE ALWAYS terminal triggers (header and lines), the partial index `invoice_draft_open_page_idx`, the status CHECK widened to `draft` and `discarded`, a rollback-order anchor and six privilege changes. Its up and down take ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on 030's seven tables before any check (a maintenance window). Roll 033 back first: with 033 applied, the downs of 032, 030, 029 and 028 are refused by the anchor's recorded dependencies (2BP01) and 031's by its postcondition (55000), and with the anchor dropped 032's down completes; 033's down with a discarded draft is refused (55000) and changes nothing; a clean down restores the 032-era catalog, and a re-apply is identical (`tests/db/invoiceDraftDiscardRoutine.integration.test.mjs` IDD-13, on disposable clusters). The open page is served from the partial index (IDD-12). Checksums: up `ea4c3080…`, down `310aaf2f…` ([05](./05-canonical-data-ownership-and-api-db-contracts.md) §2). Applied to no managed database; G-MIGRATE's target is 001–033.
- **Ordering, sealing and writes.** Each sealed answer (`applied`, `invalid_state`, `conflict`) is decided after the header lock and, for manager, technician and sales_staff, under 030's guard key held SHARED until COMMIT; store_owner takes no key. A narrowing committed after the unlocked checks and before the key is granted refuses the discard (the 503, nothing sealed, the lease kept); one attempted while the discard holds the key waits for it. Two discards, or an edit and a discard, of one draft serialize on the header. Every refusal is reached with nothing written; a fault at the audit record or the completion rolls everything back (the 503); a lost COMMIT acknowledgement is an unknown outcome; a replay passes the chain's current authorization first.
- **The shop (commit `6048bfb`).** "Discard draft…" in the reopen dialog, only on the discard eligibility's exact `allowed`; an accessible confirmation that names the draft, with no contact detail and no reason field; "Draft discarded." only after a validated 200 or its sealed replay; each refusal said apart, with no silent rebase or automatic resend; an unknown outcome held only while the confirmation stays open (DA-212); the reopen dialog's discard row holding one control's height from the moment its eligibility is asked, so an allowed or refused answer never moves the centred dialog's Close (SDB-22; below) *(M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1-R1: true at the tested desktop width only; at 600 and 320 CSS px and at a 24 px default text size an unavailable answer's wrapped hint still grew the centred dialog and moved Close — reproduced and corrected in that stage, recorded below)*; the role screen's Invoices panel description updated. The browser-only Invoices screen is unchanged.
- **Register.** DA-186 amended (discarding delivered in code; the rest of the lifecycle stays open; not closed). DA-120 (the line trigger's owner-only 40P01), DA-183 (the stored level now also decides the discard), DA-184 (the discard's own HTTP witness), DA-187 (a discard removes nothing) and DA-207 (033 rolls back first; the order of the two discard steps) amended. DA-212 (the discard's dialog-scoped unknown outcome, beside DA-208) and DA-213 (a data-only restore meets the terminal guards) new. G-DBROLE's and G-MIGRATE's lines name 001–033; G-BACKUP gains an input note (DA-213) and stays OPEN. DA-118 amended (033's discard routine, called directly by a `tmpos_app` holder, writes no audit record); DA-118 and DA-119 remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions.
- **Tests.** `tests/db/invoiceDraftDiscardRoutine.integration.test.mjs` (21 cases: IDD-01 to IDD-14 with their subtests, at routine level with the real level setters, the edit routine and owner paths, including four negative controls inside rolled-back owner transactions: without the state check a discarded draft at a wrong version answers `conflict`; without the key a discard applies at once under a held narrowing; without the reads' filter a discarded draft is served; without the line trigger a discarded draft's line changes) and `tests/db/invoiceDraftDiscardHttp.integration.test.mjs` (24 cases: IDDH-01 to IDDH-18, IDDH-04 with six subtests; two tenant entries over one disposable database). `server/runtime/invoiceDraftDiscard.test.ts` IDU-01 to IDU-07, and discard tests added to the permission, revalidator (M7B-RV-04), route, adapter, store, preflight, tenant-entry and runtime-contract suites. The 26 existing database suites changed by the stage were threaded through 033 (the chain 001–033, 033's down first wherever they roll back, the runtime login's reads on `page_v2` and `get_v2`, the route tables at twenty-two). In `tests/db/invoiceDraftRoutine.integration.test.mjs` (IDR-07) and `tests/db/invoiceDraftEditRoutine.integration.test.mjs` (IDE-02, IDE-04, IDE-07, IDE-14, IDE-22) cases needed more than a pin: 033's terminal trigger answers a non-draft status before 028's CHECK (23001 before 23514), so the CHECK is now asserted with the trigger lifted inside a rolled-back owner transaction, as is the edit suite's `issued` simulation; the trigger reads are ordered; and IDE-14 compares the status CHECK per era and leaves the new partial index out of its table-ACL list — the suite's first run failed 1 of 25 (IDE-14, whose ACL list still named `invoice_draft_open_page_idx`), a test defect, corrected before it passed. `tests/quality/managed-m005-launcher.test.mjs` now pins the launcher's derived migration list at 033 (the protected launcher is unchanged). The shop's tests: `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceDrafts.test.tsx` SID-114 to SID-147 (34 ids, 49 cases), `src/tenant/invoiceDraftsClient.test.ts` IDC-24 to IDC-28 (5 cases), `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SDB-18 to SDB-22 (5 real-browser scenarios: a confirmed discard; the discard as the server's decision; an unknown outcome and "Check again"; closing an unresolved confirmation; the reopen dialog holding still as the discard eligibility's answer arrives), and copy pins in `src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.test.tsx` and `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceLevels.test.tsx`. [11](./11-m7b-invoice-draft-discard-decision-pack.md) §10 maps every planned test, DSC-01 to DSC-26, to the executed ones.
- **Local verification.** Failures are recorded first, and none is relabelled by a later pass. The routine suite's first run failed 2 of 21 on two test preconditions (corrected), then passed 21 of 21 in three runs on fresh clusters and twice on one shared database. The HTTP suite's first run failed 6 of 24 on test defects only (a parked PUT's own lease counted, an authorizer answering before the lease, login audit rows, the save's audit record also naming the draft), then passed 24 of 24. After the review's changes (comments, 033's down postcondition, the frozen kinds set), one HTTP run failed IDDH-14 in its setup: a seed save (the existing create route, not the discard) answered 503 after about 13 s while the host's load was 5.5 on 4 CPUs and its out-of-memory kill counter had risen during the session (not attributed); it is classified as load, not proven, and stays recorded unresolved; an unchanged rerun passed 24 of 24, which does not repair it. A first full local gate, on an earlier local candidate that was never pushed, ended with the working session: its typecheck was killed (exit 137; the cgroup's out-of-memory kill counter rose by one), and the main runner failed 2 of 169 suites — `tests/quality/managed-m005-launcher.test.mjs` (126 of 131), a real defect (the test still pinned the derived migration list at 032), corrected in `efca7f3` and 131 of 131 alone; and `server/runtime/app.test.ts` (42 of 43; the failing subtest is not in the truncated log), which passed 43 of 43 alone on the same tree, load-class and unresolved. Both stay recorded. The shop's first full frontend run, at the old floor, failed SCP-61 and SCP-62 in the untouched `src/tenant/ServerCustomerPicker.test.tsx` under load; they passed alone (74 of 74, twice) and in the later full runs; recorded, not repaired. The final local gates on the delivered trees: gate A, on local commit `c93ba03` whose tree `909cf9e6` is `efca7f3`'s exactly, passed every step but the frontend runner — the typecheck, the builds, the main runner (169 of 169), the security, forbidden-pattern, lockfile and Firestore static and emulator checks, every CI database step in CI order (none failed), the admin browser file (41 of 41), the shop browser file (57 of 57), the secret scan (34 known findings, none in A's paths) and the protected-file check (68 of 68); its frontend failure is recorded with the shop's below; gate B, on `efca7f3` plus the shop change, failed its first attempt at the shop browser step only (below) and passed every step on its second, on local commit `ecef9fc` whose tree `8702b060` is `6048bfb`'s exactly. The shop side, failures first, none relabelled. Gate A's frontend step failed: the out-of-memory kill counter rose from 0 to 10 during it, vitest could not start its worker for `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceLevels.test.tsx` (28 of 29 files and 893 cases ran), and 11 cases failed in `src/tenant/ServerCustomerDirectory.test.tsx` and `src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.test.tsx` (mostly 5 s timeouts, two state assertions), files A does not touch; its solo rerun ran 918 cases and failed 2 (SCD-159 and SCL-46, "Test timed out in 5000ms"; the counter 29 → 34, a review process running alongside *(attribution corrected by M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1-R1: this record first said "a browser process"; the stage's report records a review process. The run's result and its classification as load, unresolved, are unchanged)*). Both are classified as load and stay unresolved locally: A changes no file under `src/` and no frontend runner, configuration or package file, so its frontend inputs are byte-identical to `52a9ad4`'s, and A's exact-commit CI ran the frontend step green. The shop browser file's full runs on the shop candidate, all kept: the first 60 of 61 (SDB-11, "the reopen dialog closed" — the layout defect below); the second incomplete (the working session ended; SDB-04 "Input.dispatchMouseEvent: Internal error"); the third and fourth 0 of 57, because the harness's in-test production build was killed by the host's memory limit (SIGKILL) after the environment restarted with the workspace's development server running — fixed by building from a clean export of the candidate; the fifth incomplete (9 of 9 passed, then the host ran out of memory and the environment restarted a second time); the sixth, on a prebuilt bundle, 60 of 61: SDB-07 (older) timed out at sign-in ("Runtime.evaluate: timed out") while the out-of-memory kills rose 82 → 95 with an independent review process running alongside, and SDB-07 alone then passed. Gate B's first attempt, on local commit `ce92383` (tree `4ff25c92`: `efca7f3` plus the shop change before the fix below), passed every other step (the typecheck, the builds, the main runner 169 of 169, the frontend runner's 967 cases, the security, forbidden-pattern, lockfile and Firestore checks, the database steps with none failed, the admin browser file 41 of 41, the secret scan with its 34 known findings), but the shop browser file ran 60 of 61: SDB-01 (older) "waitFor timed out after 15000ms: the reopen dialog closed", with no memory pressure in that step (at least 3051 MB available). It was diagnosed as a real layout defect of the shop change: the reopen dialog is vertically centred, and its discard row was empty while the discard eligibility was asked and gained the 34 px "Discard draft…" button when allowed, so the dialog grew after the draft was shown and Close moved up about 17 px under the pointer (with the edit row's own ~7 px, a click aimed at Close just before the answer missed). The fix holds one control's height in the discard row from the moment it is asked, so an allowed or refused answer never moves Close; SDB-22 measures Close's box and the dialog's height before and after a parked allowed answer (the owner) and a parked refused one (sales_staff at Invoices Edit, the 403). Negative controls: SDB-18 to SDB-21 each fail under a mutation of the behaviour it names, and SDB-22 fails on a build without the held height (Close's y 314.30 → 297.30, the dialog 421.41 → 455.41 px). *(M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1-R1: "never moves Close" held at SDB-22's desktop width only; that stage's record below gives the narrow-width and enlarged-text reproduction on `bb7063a` and the correction.)* Gate B's second attempt passed every step: the typecheck contract and the typecheck, the server builds and the client build check, the main runner (169 of 169), the frontend runner (967 cases at a floor of 967, the ratchet passing), the security, forbidden-pattern and lockfile checks, Firestore static and emulator, every CI database step in CI order on one shared disposable cluster (none failed), the admin browser file (41 of 41), the shop browser file (62 of 62), the secret scan (34 known findings) and the protected-file check (68 of 68), with no excluded path; the out-of-memory kill counter rose 117 → 121 during the typecheck, which still exited 0.
- **CI.** Two new steps, `tests/db/invoiceDraftDiscardRoutine.integration.test.mjs` then `tests/db/invoiceDraftDiscardHttp.integration.test.mjs`, right after the create-authority steps and before the shop browser step: every step that rolls 033 or an older migration back runs before them, and each ends with 001–033 applied and no draft of its own (DA-207). Main runner floor 168 → 169 (`server/runtime/invoiceDraftDiscard.test.ts`, a required sentinel); the migration-executor containment allowlist gains the two discard database suites. Shop floors: `MIN_FRONTEND_CASES` 918 → 967 in `scripts/run-frontend-tests.mjs` (pinned in `tests/quality/run-frontend-tests-contract.test.mjs`), and the shop browser file's `REQUIRED_SCENARIOS` gains SDB-18 to SDB-22 (62 scenarios). Exact-commit CI: run 38172711197 on `efca7f3`, green on attempt 1 (quality 65 steps and appsec 12, each a success, the two new discard database steps and the frontend render/behavior step included); run 38183710446 on `6048bfb`, green on attempt 1 (quality 65 steps and appsec 12, each a success). A failed verification run or CI attempt stays recorded here with its cause, and a later green attempt or descendant does not relabel it. M7B-INVOICES-D1-P1's documentation commit `52a9ad4` ran as run 38143562161, green on attempt 1. This documentation commit's own run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.
- **Still open.** DA-186 (deleting, restoring, issuing, numbering, tax, discounts, shipping, payment, fractional quantities, catalog prices, store moves and deletions and the rest of the lifecycle), DA-187 (retention and privacy; a discard is not erasure), DA-188 and DA-189; DA-176 (legacy customer mapping); DA-203; DA-208 (the editor's dialog-limited recovery) and DA-212 (the discard's); DA-213 with G-BACKUP; the DA-120 availability class, now including the line trigger's owner-only 40P01, and owner DDL; DA-118 and DA-119 (pre-deployment blockers); G-PERF, G-OBSERVE and the availability targets (DA-60), unchanged; M6R-PERF, the recorded rate-limiter load race, unresolved and separate; and every invoice operation other than the drafts' save, list, reopen, edit and discard (DA-183).
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database was contacted or migrated, and no live secret, configuration, provider, managed migration, deployment or Preview action occurred. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters, ephemeral loopback servers and local browser harnesses. A Preview development server was already running when the stage began (started by the workspace's process manager about 18 minutes earlier). The environment restarted twice (about 20:52–20:59 and 21:12–21:13 UTC): the process manager relaunched the development server with the first restart, and it was not running after the second. The stage neither started nor stopped it. The stage's recorded network actions are ordinary fast-forward pushes to `main` (`52a9ad4`..`efca7f3`, `efca7f3`..`6048bfb` and this documentation commit), authenticated fetches of `origin`, CI reads, and independent review requests that sent change excerpts to an external review service; every excerpt was scanned for secrets before it was sent and found clean.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1-R1 changes no gate's status; G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY stay `OPEN` ([ADR-49](./10-architecture-decision-records.md) and its addendum).** A bounded correction of M7B-INVOICES-DISCARD-P1 under the owner's stage instruction of 2026-10-12: the reopen dialog's layout at narrow widths and at an enlarged text size, real-HTTP evidence for the discard route's request bounds, the role screen's picker label, and one attribution in the record above. The five accepted discard decisions (D-DIS-1 to D-DIS-5), the route, permission, transaction, audit coupling and recovery contract are unchanged; no migration, route, permission, dependency or server runtime code changed, and migrations 001–033 with the four frozen operator/executor paths (70 files) are unchanged.
- **What P1 claimed, and where it held.** P1 recorded that an allowed or refused discard-eligibility answer "never moves" the centred reopen dialog's Close (SDB-22) and listed a narrow or zoomed viewport as a known limit ([11](./11-m7b-invoice-draft-discard-decision-pack.md) §10). The claim held at the tested desktop width only. On `bb7063a`'s shop, at 600 and 320 CSS px and at a 24 px default text size, an unavailable answer's hint wrapped, grew the discard row past its held height (34 → 62.5 px, 34 → 79 px and 51 → 84.5 px) and so grew the centred dialog, which moved Close up by half the growth: 14.25, 22.5 and 16.75 px; Edit draft moved by the same amounts. At 320 px a press at Close's old centre landed on the dialog's panel and did not close it; at the other two Close still lay under that point (it moved less than half its own height), but its box moved. At 320 px the five-column lines table was also wider than its content box (about 247–249 px in 223, with SDB-23's one-line $1.00 draft), and with the largest amount the shop accepts the entry's dialog itself scrolled sideways at 320 px (539 px of content in 256) and at 24 px text (768 in 520), and its table overflowed the content at 600 px (515 in 503). An allowed answer moved neither Close nor the dialog's height in any of these configurations, as SDB-22 asserts at desktop width.
- **The reproduction.** Before any fix, the new real-browser scenario SDB-23 ran against a build of `bb7063a`'s exact bytes and failed for that cause ("the reopen dialog held still and stayed usable in every cell"): Close and Edit draft moved at 600 px, at 320 px and at 600 px with 24 px text; at 320 px the old centre hit the panel, the press did not close the dialog and the table was wider than the dialog. The 1440 px configuration passed on that run. From the first review's fixes on, SDB-23 also expects the lines region in the Tab order, so at 1440 px the entry build fails on that check alone. Every later version of the test failed on the entry build for the same causes, and SDB-22 passed there each time (the runs are under "Local verification" below). The last entry-build run, with the test as revised in the second review round, measured Close moving up 14.25, 22.5 and 16.75 px at 600 px, 320 px and 24 px text, with Edit draft moving the same; at 320 px Close's old centre was the dialog's body, a press there did not close it, and with the $1.00 draft the dialog was one pixel too wide (272 in 271); at the largest amounts there was no named scrolling region, the dialog scrolled sideways at 320 px (539 in 256) and at 24 px text (768 in 520), and the table overflowed the content at 600 px (515 in 503).
- **The correction (commit `862fed5`: the shop's layout and SDB-23 only; then commit `664c87c`: the subtotal's type size only).** `DialogFrame` in `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceDrafts.tsx` takes an `anchor` (centred by default). The reopen dialog alone is laid out from a fixed 5vh top offset (with 5vh below it, so its 90vh panel still fits whole and scrolls inside itself), so content arriving below its header extends it downward and does not move Close, the header or Edit draft; on desktop it now sits 5vh below the viewport's top instead of centred. Every other dialog, the discard confirmation included, stays centred. The discard row keeps its held height, so an allowed or refused answer still does not change the dialog's height (SDB-22). The lines table now sits in its own named region ("Draft lines", reached by Tab) that scrolls sideways when the table is wider than the dialog — the data-table exception to reflow — so the dialog itself never scrolls sideways and no amount breaks; the description wraps between words as before, the subtotal line below the table may now wrap (`break-words`) rather than widen the dialog, and the dialog's focus containment includes the region. After `862fed5`'s CI run read the subtotal's amount on two lines at 320 px (below), commit `664c87c` (`src/tenant/ServerInvoiceDrafts.tsx` only) sets the subtotal line one type step smaller below the `sm` breakpoint (`text-sm`, 14 px heavy type at the default text size, in place of 16 px) and keeps `text-base` from `sm` up, so desktop is unchanged; `break-words` stays, so a wider font or a larger text size than SDB-23 measures breaks the amount rather than widening the dialog. A non-breaking amount was considered and rejected: it would scroll the dialog sideways at 320 px with enlarged text. SDB-23 is unchanged by `664c87c` and is its check. At 1440 px the table is laid out exactly as before; the one desktop difference is a Tab stop (the region) after Close, even when nothing scrolls. No text is clipped or hidden, nothing overlays the controls, and the initial focus, Tab containment, Escape and Close, the inert dialog beneath, focus return and the unknown-outcome flow are unchanged.
- **The tested configurations (SDB-23).** `tests/browser/shopRoleLevels.browser.test.mjs` SDB-23 (DSC-19, DSC-21): real Chromium over the real tenant entry, the Firestore emulator and disposable PostgreSQL; a fresh page per configuration at a height of 1000 CSS px and a device scale factor of 1; 1440, 600 and 320 CSS px at the default text size, and 600 px with a 24 px default font set through the browser's font-size preference (each precondition asserted: the viewport width, the root font size, the panel not capped at 90vh while the answer is measured, and the unavailable answer wrapping the discard row at 600 px, 320 px and 24 px text — it grew 28.5, 45 and 33.5 px — and not at 1440 px). It seeds two drafts: a one-line $1.00 draft for the held answers, and one line at the largest amount the shop accepts (unit price and line total $90,071,992,547,409.91) under a 205-character description, reopened in each configuration with both answers in. 320 CSS px is the reflow width of a 1280 px viewport at 400 % zoom; actual browser zoom is not exercised, and shorter viewport heights are not browser-tested. Rows are opened from the keyboard. In each configuration, with Edit draft offered and the discard-eligibility answer parked at the harness's front, Close's box is measured; the answer is then dropped at the front, so the page sees a cut connection (unavailable: Try again and its hint); Close's and Edit draft's boxes are unchanged within 0.5 CSS px, the point that was Close's centre is still Close, and a raw pointer press there closes the dialog; the hint's exact text is inside the dialog, focus stays on Close, Tab from Close moves through the lines region, Edit draft and Try again and back to Close inside it, the dialog stays inside the viewport and does not scroll sideways (re-measured after those Tab presses), the lines table fits the dialog or scrolls inside its region, and no amount wraps. Then a reopen whose allowed answer (the owner's) arrives moves neither Close, Edit draft nor the discard row, leaves the dialog's height unchanged and the dialog unscrolled sideways, and Escape closes it; after Close and after Escape, focus returns to that row's own Open button (`drafts-open-<id>`). With the largest amounts, both answers in, a precondition measured apart from the hint's wrap is asserted: the table is wider than the dialog's content exactly at 600 px, 320 px and 600 px with 24 px text, and fits at 1440 px (measured: about 515, 515 and 732 px of table in 503, 208 and 448 px). There the unit price and the line total each read exactly "$90,071,992,547,409.91" on one line, and the subtotal reads "Draft subtotal $90,071,992,547,409.91" with its amount on one line; where the table overflows, it sits in a region (role `region`, named "Draft lines", `tabindex` 0) that is Tab's first stop from Close and that ArrowRight scrolls; the dialog, re-measured after Tab and the arrow key, has not scrolled sideways; and Escape returns focus to that draft's own Open button. In the 320 px and the 24 px-text configurations that dialog's content is taller than its 90vh panel, which scrolls vertically (measured, not asserted). No discard is posted and both drafts stay `draft`. The checks within a configuration are collected, and when a hard precondition or wait fails later in the run, the problems already found are reported with it. The rate-limited hint is not produced in the browser: `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceDrafts.test.tsx` SID-116 (jsdom) holds its text and the disabled Try again. SDB-18 to SDB-22 are unchanged.
- **Residuals, not fixed.** An edit-eligibility answer that lands after the discard answer can still move the discard row (the edit row above it holds no height; at worst a press opens the confirmation or the editor, neither of which discards); Close, the header and Edit draft do not move. The lines table's five header labels stack in its first column at every width (pre-existing, unchanged; an owner decision); that stacking widens the first column and is part of why the largest amounts overflow at 600 px. On desktop the lines region adds one Tab stop even when nothing scrolls. At 320 px the shop's own sidebar lies over the drafts list (an unrelated screen; SDB-23 opens rows from the keyboard), and the Invoices screen behind the dialog is wider than a 600 or 320 px viewport: page-level reflow of the Invoices screen is not claimed. The subtotal's amount on one line depends on font metrics: it is claimed only at SDB-23's configurations on the fonts the local gate and CI render. No WCAG conformance of any kind is claimed, for this dialog or the product.
- **The copy (commit `23a1457`, with the HTTP evidence below).** The role screen's Invoices panel (`src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.tsx`) now reads "(at Manage or above — Full Access in this picker)", matching the picker's option label; its pins in `src/tenant/ServerCustomerLevels.test.tsx` and `src/tenant/ServerInvoiceLevels.test.tsx` changed with it. P1's "Full in this picker" stays quoted as history in DA-183's note.
- **The attribution.** The record above said gate A's frontend solo rerun had "a browser process running alongside"; the stage's report records a review process. Corrected in place with a dated note; the run's result (918 cases, 2 failed) and its classification as load, unresolved, are unchanged, and no host out-of-memory kill is claimed repaired.
- **Real-HTTP request-boundary evidence (commit `23a1457`, with the copy above; no server code).** `tests/db/invoiceDraftDiscardHttp.integration.test.mjs` IDDH-19 (DSC-09), one test with six subtests (A to F), over the real composed tenant entry and a disposable PostgreSQL at 001–033, with a valid Manage session, Origin, both CSRF headers and fresh keys unless a case removes one, and hand-framed requests on a real socket where framing matters. The order in `server/runtime/app.ts`: the request limiter; the body's metadata from the headers alone (`admitBody`); Origin and the pre-session CSRF; the session, its CSRF, the authorizer, the trusted context and the target; the bounded body read (`readBoundedBody`); the `Idempotency-Key` check; the lease. The cases below drive the metadata check ahead of Origin, both CSRF headers, the session and the authorizer, and the bounded read after the session and the authorizer and ahead of the key check (no lease is left by any refusal); the metadata check's place after the limiter and before the key, and the bounded read's after the session's CSRF, the trusted context and the target, are app.ts's order only, not driven by a case. (A) Positive controls: a valid discard commits through Node's client (A1) and through the hand framing the refusals use (A2). (B) A declared length over 1,024 bytes is the 413 `content_too_large` from the headers alone (logged `body_declared_too_large`): a 2,048-byte body (B1); Content-Length 1025 with no body byte sent, answered without the body (B2); with no session (B3, not the 401), a foreign Origin (B4, not the 403), `text/plain` (B5, the length judged before the type), neither CSRF header (B6, not the 403), and from a technician below Invoices Manage (B7: the declared size refused before the authorizer, not the 403). (C) A chunked body with no Content-Length is admitted (no 411) and read under the cap; past 1,024 bytes it is the 413 at the bounded read (logged `body_streamed_too_large`), answered at the crossing: 4,096 bytes in 512-byte chunks (C1); with no `Idempotency-Key` (C2) or a malformed one (C3) still the 413; with no session the same stream is the 401 (C4: the session before the read); a coding beside chunked is the 400 `transfer_encoding_unsupported` before any read (C5); a Content-Length beside chunked encoding is refused by Node's HTTP parser (C6: its 400, with no app body or log line, nothing written); 1,536 bytes with no last chunk, a stream never finished, is the 413 within the deadline (C7); from a technician the same stream is the 403 (C8: the authorizer before the read); and 687 characters of multibyte UTF-8 (1,287 bytes), never finished, is the 413 at the byte cap (C9: bytes are counted, not characters). (D) A type other than `application/json` with at most a `charset=utf-8` parameter is the 415 `unsupported_media_type` (logged `media_type_unsupported`): `text/plain`, another or a quoted charset, an extra parameter and a longer subtype (D1 to D5), two Content-Type lines (D6), and `text/plain` with a foreign Origin (D12), with neither CSRF header (D13) and from a technician (D14), each the 415, not the 403; a body with no type is the 415 `media_type_missing` (D7, and D8 with no session, not the 401); a Content-Encoding is the 415 `content_encoding_unsupported` (D9); no type and no body is the 400 `body_required` (D10); `application/json;charset=UTF-8` (D11) and `application/json; charset=utf-8` (D15) commit. (E) Exactly 1,024 bytes (valid JSON padded with whitespace) commits, declared (E1) and chunked (E2), each the 200 at version 2 with one discard; 1,025 bytes is the 413 declared (E3) and chunked (E4). (F) A key refused at the boundary stays fresh: refused declared and chunked, the same key then commits the discard, not as a replay. Each app-layer refusal answers only `{ error, requestId }`, echoes nothing that was sent, and writes no draft change, audit record, outbox row, completion or lease and no idempotency record for its key (the counts, and the draft's header and lines with its version and `xmin`, unchanged); C6, answered before the app, is checked for its status and for nothing written. The evidence records the request limiter's unit spent on each refused request; that is recorded, not asserted. No runtime defect was found and no runtime code changed; IDU-07 (the bound, at unit level) is unchanged, and IDDH-19's socket evidence is added beside it, not in its place.
- **Local verification.** Failures are recorded first, and none is relabelled by a later pass. Protected files at entry: 70 of 70.
