The HTTP suite's first run with IDDH-19 failed 4 of 31 on test bookkeeping only (positive cases counted without their own key, so the completion count read 0 against the expected 1; every refusal case passed), then passed 31 of 31; after the first review's additions it passed 31 of 31, and after the second review's (B7, C9, D13 to D15, and the test's chunk framer counting bytes) 31 of 31 again; the unit file IDU-01 to IDU-07 passed 7 of 7. SDB-23's first two runs on the entry build failed at 320 px for a reason of the test's own (a pointer reopen hit the shop's sidebar), so it opens rows from the keyboard; its third run was the reproduction above, and a fourth, with the test as it then stood, failed the same way. The shop browser file's `REQUIRED_SCENARIOS` gains SDB-23 (63 scenarios); the frontend floor stays 967 cases and the main runner floor 169 suites. **M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-D1-P1 changes no gate's status (a test-ordering correction and documentation).** It proposes how a saved store invoice draft could be issued, in [12](./12-m7b-invoice-issue-decision-pack.md), and corrects five test snapshots. The pack is PROPOSED / UNADOPTED.
- **The pack.** Seven owner decisions (D-ISS-1 to D-ISS-7): the meaning and lifecycle (a separate, immutable issued invoice; the draft becomes terminal `issued`; no amount due, payment or legal-validity claim), authority, per-store numbering, amounts and tax, the issued snapshot and privacy, concurrency and recovery, and retention and correction. It adds a capability map that separates adopted rules, current code, open items and missing facts; a conditional route, transaction and migration contract; and planned tests that have not run.
- **Facts the repository does not hold.** The legal issuer per store, the stores' jurisdiction, whether and how sales tax applies, the store time zone and how long issued invoices must be kept. The pack invents none of them and names the branch that works without each.
- **Nothing is implemented or adopted.** No route, state, migration, permission key, setting or UI control exists for issuing. Issuing, numbering and tax stay deferred (DA-186) until the owner records the pack's decisions; DA-187 stays open.
- **The test correction (commit `fba801b`).** Five routine-grant snapshots built their grant lists with `array_agg(... order by 1)`. Inside an aggregate, `1` is a constant, so the list kept the catalog's order. Each now orders by its own format expression under the C collation (`tests/db/customerCreateRoutine`, `invoiceRoleLevelRoutine`, `roleLevelVersionRoutine`, `tenantRoleLevelRoutine` and `tenantSessionMint.integration.test.mjs`; M7CR-01, M5IRL-02, M7RV-02, M7RL-02, M7M-01). Every expected list there holds no entry or one, so no current result changes; grants, duplicates, expected values and assertions are unchanged. The local disposable cluster is `en_US.UTF-8`, so the collation is pinned so that a later multi-entry list orders the same way locally and on CI. IDD-13's index list was changed in `ba9f98a` to order by its own expression, without a collation pin.
- **Verification.** The local gate on `fba801b` passed every step it ran: typecheck, the main runner (169 suites), the frontend runner (967 cases), the security, forbidden-file and lockfile checks, all 40 database steps in CI order and the secret scan. Exact-commit CI on `fba801b` (run 38872647754): attempts 1 and 2 failed only in the distributed rate-limiter step — M6R-PERF (one consume, then 29, answered unavailable at the limiter's deadline or lock timeout) and, on attempt 2, RLR-09's negative control, which under the runner's delay saw no consume answered `limited`. That is the recorded load class (DA-60); the commit does not touch the limiter suite, which passed 19 of 19 locally on the same commit. Attempt 3, a rerun of the failed jobs with the reason recorded, passed: quality 65 of 65 on attempt 3, with security 12 of 12 from attempt 1. The pass is not a repair, and DA-60 stays open.
- **Nothing deployed.** No live secret, configuration, managed database, managed migration, provider, deployment or Preview action occurred. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters only.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-P1 keeps G-IAM, G-PERSIST, G-AUDIT, G-IDEMPOT, G-DBROLE, G-MIGRATE, G-CPLOGIN, G-STATUS, G-ISOLATION and G-PRIVACY `OPEN` ([ADR-50](./10-architecture-decision-records.md)).** It records the owner's acceptance of the seven issuing decisions of [12](./12-m7b-invoice-issue-decision-pack.md) as amended (D-ISS-1 to D-ISS-7, by the owner's stage instruction of 2026-10-16, with a per-store tax mode in place of one product-wide branch) and delivers their server foundation in code: through the API, a store team member at Invoices Approve or above with Customers View can issue a saved, open draft as a separate, immutable, store-numbered invoice, and the store owner sets each store's issuing settings. No shop UI is built. No gate closes, and M7b is not complete.
- **The decisions.** D-ISS-1 (amended): a separate issued invoice with its own id, a store number, the issue time and an immutable snapshot under snapshot contract version 1; the draft terminal `issued`; a **Total** that claims no amount due, balance or payment; one audit record and no event; void before live issuing. D-ISS-2: `issue_invoices` at Invoices Approve with Customers View, `view_issued_invoices` at Invoices View with Customers View, `edit_issuing_settings` the tenant owner rule with a recent sign-in on the write. D-ISS-3: one integer series per store from 1, from a locked counter; no legal gaplessness promised. D-ISS-4 (changed): an explicit per-store tax mode, `exclusive_rate` or `none`, with no default and an explicit 0 rate distinct from `none`. D-ISS-5: the seller fields in versioned per-store settings, the invoice-facing store name among them; no phone or email copied; UTC. D-ISS-6: the issue binds the reviewed draft, settings and customer versions; one invoice per draft, forever; the shop's recovery is the next slice. D-ISS-7: no delete, purge, restore, void or edit of issued records; 034's down refuses once issuing data exists.
- **The server (the delivering commit is recorded in the next stage's documentation).** [12](./12-m7b-invoice-issue-decision-pack.md) §10.3's seven tenant routes (the settings read and write, the issue eligibility and preview, the issue, and the issued list with its `sourceDraft` filter and the detail), twenty-nine tenant routes in all; the commands `store.invoice.issue` (update, store scope) and `tenant.issuing_settings.update` (create, tenant scope), neither with an event; the store guard admits `issue_invoices` at Approve with its Customers View corequisite, and the tenant guard `edit_issuing_settings` beside `edit_role_levels`; the kernel's bounded extension (§10.7: the two sealed 409s `issuing_not_configured` and `amount_out_of_range` an update contract may opt into, `store.invoice.issue` in the closed set of precondition update kinds, the settings write's rolled-back 404, and the audit subjects); the two mutators and four reads (`server/persistence/postgresInvoiceIssuing.ts`); the startup preflight at exactly 001–034 with its new grant set. The issue's audit record names the draft, the issued invoice and the customer at store scope through one fixed statement; the settings write's names the store alone at tenant scope; nothing is enqueued.
- **Migration 034 and the chain.** `034_m7b_invoice_issue` adds schema `tmpos_invoice_issue` with four tables (`store_issuing_settings`, `store_invoice_counter`, `issued_invoice`, `issued_invoice_line`), eighteen functions (six routines granted to `tmpos_app`; the trigger functions, the tax function, helpers and a rollback-order anchor granted to nobody), ENABLE ALWAYS guards and a deferred commit check, the draft status CHECK widened to `issued`, the header terminal trigger rebound to a 034 function, and two nullable audit subject columns. Its up and down take ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on the seven tables 033 locks (the down also on 034's four) before any check (a maintenance window). Roll 034 back first: with 034 applied, 033's down refuses (55000) and 034's anchor refuses dropping the 033 functions it names (2BP01); 034's down over any issuing settings, number counter, issued invoice, issued draft or audit record naming an issued invoice or a store is refused and changes nothing; a clean down restores 033's exact catalog, and a re-apply is identical (`tests/db/invoiceIssueRoutine.integration.test.mjs` IIR-01 to IIR-05, on disposable clusters). Checksums: up `5f74386b…`, down `7cdf8f1b…` ([05](./05-canonical-data-ownership-and-api-db-contracts.md) §2). Applied to no managed database; G-MIGRATE's target is 001–034.
- **Ordering, sealing and writes.** The issue routine re-derives the caller from the session key, locks the draft header FOR UPDATE, for manager, technician and sales_staff takes 030's key 530030 SHARED and re-reads the Invoices and Customers levels under it (store_owner takes no key), then takes the settings and the customer FOR SHARE and the store's counter FOR UPDATE; every refusal except number exhaustion and the session's last liveness check is decided before the counter. A session that fails a re-check after a lock wait, the last one after the counter, is refused and rolled back before the routine writes any row, with no number used (the idempotency lease taken before the transaction remains to expire). A sealed refusal (`invalid_state`, `issuing_not_configured`, `write_conflict`, `amount_out_of_range`) commits its completion and nothing else; a rolled-back fault returns the number. Issues, edits and discards of one draft serialize on the header; two issues of one store serialize on the counter in commit order. A settings write takes a per-store advisory key (530034) EXCLUSIVE before its version check, so two first writes give one version 1 and one sealed `write_conflict`, and after the routine's lock waits it judges the recent sign-in's deadline again on the database clock (passed: the 503, rolled back). A lost COMMIT acknowledgement is an unknown outcome; a replay passes the chain's current authorization first.
- **Register.** DA-118 and DA-119 amended (034's issue and settings routines, called directly, write no audit record; a direct level set now changes issuing authority); both remain OPEN pre-deployment blockers and G-AUDIT exceptions. DA-183 (the stored level now also decides `issue_invoices` and `view_issued_invoices`), DA-184 (their HTTP witnesses, IIH-01 and ISH-02), DA-189 (the preview's store-scope disclosure of one customer's name), DA-213 (issued rows and settings meet 034's ENABLE ALWAYS guards on a data-only restore; G-BACKUP's input widened, the gate OPEN) and DA-60 with G-PERF (issues of one store serialize on its counter; unmeasured) amended. DA-186 and DA-187 were amended at adoption. G-DBROLE's and G-MIGRATE's lines name 001–034. No DA is new.
- **Tests.** `tests/db/invoiceIssueRoutine.integration.test.mjs` (17 cases: IIR-01 to IIR-17, at routine level with real sessions, owner paths and owner fault triggers), `tests/db/invoiceIssueHttp.integration.test.mjs` (13 cases: IIH-01 to IIH-13 over the composed tenant entry, two tenant entries for the races: issue against issue, edit and discard, and a customer rename, a settings write, a level narrowing and a session revocation committed on the second entry or by the owner while the issue waits) and `tests/db/issuingSettingsHttp.integration.test.mjs` (9 cases: ISH-01 to ISH-09, two tenant entries for the concurrent first writes, and a session revocation committed while a write waits at 016's hold). `server/runtime/invoiceIssuing.test.ts` ISS-R01 to ISS-R13, `server/runtime/issuingSettings.test.ts` ISS-S01 to ISS-S11 and `server/persistence/postgresInvoiceIssuing.test.ts` ISS-P01 to ISS-P12 (14 cases with ISS-P06b and ISS-P11b), and issuing tests added to the permission, revalidator, route, command-transaction, store, audit-writer, preflight, production-transactions and tenant-entry unit suites. The 28 existing database suites changed by the stage were threaded through 034 (the chain 001–034, 034's down first wherever they roll back, the route tables at twenty-nine); the discard routine suite proves 033 with 034 rolled back in its setup and restores 034 in its teardown. Three quality suites (the migration files contract, the executor containment allowlist and the managed launcher's derived list) pin 034, and the production runtime contract pins the issuing modules and their routes. [12](./12-m7b-invoice-issue-decision-pack.md) §10.11 maps each planned test to the executed ones.
- **Local verification.** Failures are recorded first, and none is relabelled by a later pass. The routine suite's first run failed 3 of 17 on test bugs, then passed 17 of 17 in three runs, the last two against the integrated candidate's 034. The issue HTTP suite's first run failed 3 of 12 (a test path and a leak probe), its second 1 of 12 (the test needed a fresh sign-in after a status change), then passed 12 of 12 twice. The settings HTTP suite's first run failed 2 of 8 on test mistakes, its next three went to ISH-04's waiter design (it uses two owners, because one owner's two sessions serialize on the account row at 016's hold before the settings key), then it passed 8 of 8 twice. None of these found a product defect. The migration's smoke script passed 10 of 10 on its last three runs: run 9 on the worker's 034, run 10 after the integrated 034's message fix and run 11 after the migration review's changes; its earlier development runs are not in the stage ledger. On the integrated, uncommitted candidate the typecheck contract and the root, server and tenant typechecks passed, and the main runner ran 172 suites with one failure: `tests/quality/gap11-grant-diff-artifact.test.mjs` case 15 counted the two new 034 files as modified only because they were marked intent-to-add (its diff is against HEAD); with that mark removed it passed 18 of 18, and it is to be run again on the committed candidate. Independent reviews of the migration, the authorization, the kernel and a cross-model pass changed 034 (an exact table-and-column ACL proof in its postcondition, the deferred check also requiring the counter at or above the number, the line guard reading the draft line without a row lock, and invisible characters in its patterns written as `\u` escapes) and the kernel and adapter (the issue's audit subjects bound to its action and scope, the settings change set's and the page query's exact keys each read once, and the production command set's check also requiring an equal `issuingRefusals`); two findings were rejected with reasons. After those changes the root and tenant typechecks passed and `server/platform-identity/auditEventWriter.test.ts` passed 29 of 29, `server/persistence/postgresInvoiceIssuing.test.ts` 14 of 14, `server/persistence/postgresTransactionalStore.test.ts` 43 of 43 and `server/platform-identity/auditTransaction.test.ts` 14 of 14. The stage's local gate on the integrated, uncommitted candidate (started 14:41 UTC, with the routine suite already in the candidate; only documentation changed in the candidate while it ran) failed in three places: the root typecheck was killed (exit 137 after 59 s), while the typecheck contract and the server and tenant typechecks passed; the main runner failed 1 of 172 suites, `tests/quality/gap11-grant-diff-artifact.test.mjs`, with the new files still marked intent-to-add; and the CI-order database replay failed 2 of its 43 steps, `tests/db/tenantSessionMint.integration.test.mjs` (15 of 16; M7M-09b) and `tests/db/invoiceDraftCreateAuthority.integration.test.mjs` (63 of 64; ICA-10 with CCA-12), while the other 41 passed, the three issuing suites among them (17, 12 and 8). The frontend runner passed (29 files, 967 cases), and no PostgreSQL process was left. These failures stay recorded here, and a later pass does not relabel them. Their causes: the root typecheck was killed by the host's memory limit (about 1.9 GB available at the time), not by a type error; the main runner's failure was the intent-to-add artefact above; and both database failures were defects in the stage's own test ripple — M7M-09b re-applied 034 before the migrations 034 needs were back, and ICA-10's before-snapshot of audit rows was taken with 034's two new columns present — each corrected in its test (16 of 16 and 64 of 64 alone). A second gate run (from 15:03 UTC) replayed the CI database order: 43 of 43 steps passed and no PostgreSQL process was left, while its root typecheck was killed again (exit 137); the root typecheck then passed alone in the foreground. Independent reviews of the tests and documentation then changed tests only: IIR-02 carries a pre-034 audit row through the up; each of IIR-01's widenings asserts its exact refusal — three are caught by a postcondition clause other than the one written for them (`tmpos_app` made a member of the owner, the anchor without its dependencies, and the schema comment dropped), so 034 still refuses each, with a message that names another check; the settings suite removes its customers; the issue HTTP suite has its own advisory lock key; M7M-09b proves its hand-issued grant present; and IIH-13, ISH-09, IIH-08's four further fault points and IIH-01's issued-read rows were added. After those changes the routine suite passed 17 of 17 twice, the issue HTTP suite 12 of 12 and then 13 of 13 three times, the settings HTTP suite 8 of 8 and then 9 of 9 twice, and the session-mint suite 16 of 16 twice; none found a product defect. A second review round then made the two revocation cases assert the revocation itself and, because a refusal after a wait and a lock timeout share one 503, proved on the wall clock and on the database clock that each refused wait in IIH-13 and ISH-09 was released well before any lock, statement or deadline bound (answers 27 to 92 ms after the send; at least 1,363 ms of the 1,400 ms lock bound left at each release); it also pinned IIR-01's three remaining widenings to the messages that fire and gave M7M-09b a control. Its runs were on a loaded host (load average about 6.5 to 7 on 4 CPUs, under 1 GB of memory available): the routine suite passed 17 of 17 and the session-mint suite 16 of 16; the issue HTTP suite failed 1 of 13 twice — IIH-09 (f), a discard that answered 503 before it was seen waiting at the header, and IIH-01, a sign-in that answered 503 — and then passed 13 of 13; the settings HTTP suite passed 9 of 9, failed 1 of 9 (ISH-06's recent-sign-in deadline schedule: "sent 1308 ms, answered 1414 ms"), then passed 9 of 9. Those three failures are in cases the round did not change; they stay recorded, unresolved, and are not relabelled by the later passes. The local gate on the committed candidate is recorded in the next stage's documentation with the delivering commit's CI.
- **CI.** Three new steps, `tests/db/invoiceIssueRoutine.integration.test.mjs`, then `tests/db/invoiceIssueHttp.integration.test.mjs`, then `tests/db/issuingSettingsHttp.integration.test.mjs`, right after the discard steps: the suites leave issued data that 034's down and, through it, every older down refuse, so every step that rolls 034 or an older migration back runs before them; the routine suite rolls 034 back and re-applies it itself, so it runs before the HTTP suites; each teardown removes what its suite wrote, and each ends with 001–034 applied. Main runner floor 169 → 172 (the three new unit suites); the migration-executor containment allowlist gains the three issuing database suites. The adoption commit `e09ee45` ran as run 38904272251, green on attempt 1 (quality 65 of 65 steps and appsec 12 of 12). A failed verification run or CI attempt stays recorded here with its cause, and a later green attempt or descendant does not relabel it. The delivering commit's exact-commit run is recorded in the next stage's documentation.
- **Still open.** Void (required before live issuing); payments and amounts due; discounts, shipping, fractional quantities and catalog prices, and the rest of DA-186's lifecycle; retention and privacy for drafts and issued records (DA-187, F-5); the stores' jurisdictions and legal review (F-2); each merchant's real issuer and tax values; the shop's issuing UI and its recovery ([12](./12-m7b-invoice-issue-decision-pack.md) ISS-19, ISS-23 and ISS-24's client half); DA-176, DA-188, DA-189, DA-203, DA-208 and DA-212; DA-213 with G-BACKUP; DA-118 and DA-119 (pre-deployment blockers); G-PERF and DA-60, unchanged but for the new per-store counter.
- **Nothing deployed.** No managed database was contacted or migrated, and no live secret, configuration, provider, managed migration or deployment occurred. Work ran on disposable PostgreSQL clusters and ephemeral loopback servers, in isolated worktrees. The stage's recorded network actions are ordinary fast-forward pushes to `main` (`fc1c79b`..`e09ee45`, and the delivering commit), CI reads, and independent review requests that sent change excerpts to an external review service.

**M7B-INVOICES-ISSUE-P2 keeps every gate `OPEN` ([ADR-50](./10-architecture-decision-records.md) addendum).** It delivers the shop's use of the seven issuing routes, in code: the owner's issuing settings with an explicit tax mode; the review and explicit issue of a saved server draft; manual recovery of an unknown issue (Check again with the same key and bytes within 23 hours of the first send, and Find issued invoice); and the issued list and detail. The server, its 29 tenant route contracts, its permissions and migrations 001–034 are unchanged. No gate closes, the total stays 37, and M7b is not complete.
- **Commits.** `1b20041` (A: the client and the owner's settings; exact-SHA CI 38988457470, attempt 1 green). `c834b4d` (B: the issued list and detail; exact-SHA CI 39029335800 failed on attempt 1 at one browser cell: SIS-09 at 1440 px measured the lines table at 628.8 px in a 607 px region on CI's fonts, where it fit locally; the test-only `a7d645d` makes that expectation one-sided, and its exact-SHA CI 39030992472 passed on attempt 1; `c834b4d`'s own run stays recorded as failed). `45c9e55` (C: the issue review, confirmation and recovery; exact-SHA CI 39070294278 failed on attempt 1 at one browser scenario: SIS-14 waited for a session check on a window focus that the page rightly never sent, because the test ended the session while Escape's own re-read of the drafts list was still out and that read's 401 found the end first; the test-only `ae3d2b1` waits for that re-read and asserts the one check the focus then sends, and its exact-SHA CI 39076181405 passed on attempt 1; `45c9e55`'s own run stays recorded as failed). This documentation commit (D) has no CI run of its own in this record.

At commit C's candidate the frontend runner ran 30 files and 1,088 cases, and the shop browser file passed 78 of 78 locally. Each commit went through independent review rounds, including a cross-model pass; the findings were reconciled and fixed before the commit.

Each is set out in [12](./12-m7b-invoice-issue-decision-pack.md) §10.12.

DA-214 to DA-220 are new: the Invoices page's sideways scroll at 320 px from code outside the slice; the unmeasured contrast of the older `bg-amber-600` pair; the Total's one-line check without a failing mutant; the narrow-column evidence limits; the edit dialog's Save reason; the settings write's panel-held attempt; and a sign-in renewed in the Issued section reaching the drafts section as a new session. ISS-19, ISS-23 and ISS-24's client half are mapped to executed tests in [12](./12-m7b-invoice-issue-decision-pack.md) §10.11.

The host started the project's development server on port 5000 three times during the stage (2026-10-17 at 08:48:25, 10:16:06 and 21:24:40 UTC); each was found and stopped by its process identities, and the last check found no listener on ports 5000–5002.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

**M7B-INVOICES-VOID-P0 changes no gate's status (documentation only).** It proposes how an issued store invoice could be voided and an issuing mistake corrected, in [13](./13-m7b-invoice-void-and-correction-decision-pack.md). The pack is PROPOSED / UNADOPTED.
- **The pack.** Six owner decisions (D-VOID-1 to D-VOID-6): the meaning and the first slice's boundary, authority and recent sign-in, visibility, confirmation, reason and records, repeated and unknown outcomes, and the correction scope; a conditional contract (with a planned migration 035 that does not exist) and planned tests that have not run. Payments, refunds, credit notes, tax filings and document delivery stay outside it.
- **Nothing is implemented or adopted.** No route, state, permission, read change, migration or UI control exists for voiding. Void stays deferred (DA-186) until the owner records the pack's decisions.
- **Register.** DA-221 and DA-222 added (P2 evidence limits found unrecorded); no other row amended. Every gate and every open DA item stays open. The pack's §8 names the P2 evidence limits it carries, with these two ids.

**The gate total and severity mix is unchanged (37 / 21 BLOCKER / 14 HIGH / 2 MED).**

- **M0 carry-forward gates still open:** G-EMU, G-HIST, G-4USER.
- **M2's lockfile recovery is now proven on a CI runner.** Every push to `main` runs the lockfile-portability gate before a public-registry `npm ci`, then the typecheck contract and the typecheck ratchet (`ci.yml` quality job); all were green on `8883473d` (run 34779503875), the runner whose missing internal mirror caused the original failure. No M2 gate changes state here: moving G-EMU, G-APPSEC or G-COVERAGE is the owner's decision against each gate's own pass rule.
- **Go-live rule:** every `BLOCKER` must be `PASS` and every `HIGH` must be `PASS` or explicitly owner-waived (with documented risk) before G-GOLIVE can be requested.
- **Security-HIGH waiver rule:** waiving any **security** HIGH gate (e.g. G-APPSEC, G-WEBHARDEN, G-PROVIDER) requires, in addition to owner sign-off, (a) **Security sign-off**, (b) a **documented compensating control**, and (c) a **time-boxed remediation date**. This is why the previously-waivable security-integrity gates (webhook signature verification, the hardcoded PIN, the RLS-bypassing DB role, money-path idempotency) were **elevated to BLOCKER** rather than left owner-waivable.
- **Non-waivable carve-out — G-APPSEC's lockfile-portability subcriterion.** A G-APPSEC waiver may cover SAST/DAST/SCA **findings** (e.g. an open transitive High). It may **never** waive the pre-install lockfile-portability checker, which is **non-waivable and blocks merge independently of G-APPSEC's disposition**. Without this carve-out a subcriterion silently inherits its parent's waivability, and a waiver granted over an unrelated advisory would dissolve supply-chain lock integrity along with it — which nobody granting such a waiver intends. The carve-out is required because the usual "it would fail anyway" reasoning is **false here**: an unportable lockfile does **not** reliably break the build — npm *skips* an unfetchable `optional`/`devOptional` package and `npm ci` still exits 0. That silent path is the realised failure this gate exists to prevent (a green install that omitted `@types/react`), so the control cannot rest on the build failing loudly. Per the precedent above, a security-integrity control that must not be waivable is treated as blocking regardless of its host gate's severity.
- **Legal-HIGH waiver rule:** **G-PRIVACY is a statutory gate** (GDPR Arts 15–22 DSAR, CCPA/CPRA) — its absence at go-live is a legal violation, not a discretionary risk. Waiving G-PRIVACY (or its core export/deletion sub-scope) requires, beyond owner sign-off, (a) **legal-owner sign-off**, (b) a **documented lawful basis**, and (c) a **time-boxed remediation date** — an owner alone may not waive it. The core DSAR-fulfilment sub-scope is treated as effectively blocking for any deployment processing EU/CA-resident PII.
- **Severity semantics:** a gate's blocking severity may **exceed** the defect severity of its source gap (defect severity ≠ go-live-blocking severity). Examples: GAP-18 (defect High) → G-EMU (BLOCKER); GAP-24 (defect Medium) → G-HIST (HIGH). This is intentional, not an error.
- **Operational residual — carrier-credential rotation (owner action, not a code gate).** The eliminated Shipping sidecar accepted carrier credentials over unauthenticated routes and held them in plaintext process memory, and the preview surface that reached it was network-reachable. **This repository holds no evidence that any real carrier credential was ever entered, and none is asserted.** The residual is strictly conditional: **if** a real carrier credential was entered into the provider-settings surface at any time while the legacy preview/sidecar was reachable, **then** before production the store owner must (a) rotate that credential at the carrier/aggregator, and (b) review that provider account's billing and activity for the exposure window. The condition is **not decidable from repository evidence** — absence of historical misuse cannot be proven from this codebase, because the sidecar persisted no credential audit trail and its process memory is not recoverable. Accordingly this is an **owner operational production gate**, discharged by owner attestation, not by any test, scan, or code artifact in this repository; no local gate can close it and none claims to. **No email address, provider account, key fragment, credential identifier, or secret is recorded here, and none may be added.** Related code-side state: G-SECRETS is `PARTIAL` (the in-memory store is gone; encrypted-at-rest and rotatable durable secrets remain M6/M8).
- **Owner decisions RESOLVED (no open M1 owner decision remains):** **G-PCI — integrated payment capability is IN SCOPE** (semi-integrated/tokenized/provider-hosted; no raw cardholder data in TM POS2026; provider selected in M7b; PCI category validated before production). **G-PRIVACY — privacy & DSAR foundation is IN SCOPE** (implement, not exclude; distributed M1/M5/M6/M7/M8/M9). Both directions are **DECIDED**; both remain **evidence-based production gates** until their evidence passes.
